in writing. the cancún climate conference proposed a solution whereby central and eastern european member states could take a limited amount of their unused emission allowances in return for supporting the european union's 30% emission reduction commitment. the solution seems to be advantageous in several respects. on the one hand, the european union as a whole must strive to take the lead in the fight against climate change, even in times of economic crisis. with appropriate regulation, we can encourage european companies to innovate in the areas of energy efficiency, renewable energy, water and resource efficiency. in the future, these energy and resource-efficient technologies will form the basis of european competitiveness. without innovation there is no competitiveness, without a competitive economy there is no employment, without jobs there is no sustainability of the european social model. on the other hand, such an agreement would also benefit the central and eastern european member states. by selling their climate assets, they can replace the resources needed to shift the economic model towards sustainability. for hungary, this climate asset is estimated at around eur 900-1000 million, which would be a huge help in generating revenue for the green economy in hungary. i therefore urge the representatives of the member states in the council to agree on the transfer of the central and eastern european allowances during the danish presidency, which has a positive attitude to the initiative.
in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on the successful negotiations which have given birth to a key principle of european resource efficiency. we are all aware of the fact that the increasing quantity of electronic waste we generate each year is posing numerous challenges for the union. the unskilled collection and treatment of waste not only has serious health and environmental consequences, but also leads to the irresponsible waste of valuable raw materials in europe in the face of increasingly intense global competition for resources. the current revision of the waste electrical and electronic equipment directive provides a framework to address these challenges. the compromise reached sets recovery targets for the member states which, in addition to being ambitious, take account of the consumption habits of the various countries. for example, in eastern europe, consumers are less likely to replace their household appliances within three to four years of purchase and even to use outdated appliances in their holiday homes. the 'one size fits all' approach of the previous directive, with a target of 4 kg/person/year, was unable to address these market characteristics. the most important element of the current agreement is therefore that in the future, depending on the choice of the member state, the quantity of products placed on the market or the amount of waste actually generated may also form the basis for calculating the collection targets. this possibility makes the objectives of the directive feasible and can thus make a real contribution to increasing resource efficiency in europe.

mr president, like many of us in the house, i am somewhat torn. on the one hand, i can appreciate that the majority of european decision-makers recognise the need for immediate action in order to handle the crisis, which i agree is no longer solely economic. on the other hand, i am convinced that our point of departure is not the proper one. of course we cannot do without financial stability and strict rules but, in my opinion, the root of the problem is that there has not been enough attention paid to and emphasis on growth, employment and social inclusion. understandably, the majority of the conservative leaders were too preoccupied with the austerity measures, which was a big mistake. in addition, i am not convinced about the way in which the crisis is being handled. we should follow the community method because we cannot let europe diverge from what it represents – the foundation of european construction, a way of working together, irrespective of the differences between the member states. apart from this, i underline an important point. the european parliament is the only directly elected body among the institutions. our president should fully participate in the euro summits.
in writing. 23.7 million women and men. that's how many people were unemployed in europe at the end of 2011. the fiscal pact that was finalized at last week's summit was barely dry when the shocking statistic emerged: the unemployment rate in the eurozone countries was at its highest since the introduction of the single currency at the end of last year. youth unemployment is particularly worrying in spain and greece, where almost half of those under 25 are unemployed. i am glad that the council has finally recognised what the socialist members of the european parliament have been saying for a long time: the situation is unsustainable, and the growing problems in europe cannot be solved by budgetary cuts alone. despite the successive laws and treaties that tighten fiscal rules, recession seems inevitable. without economic growth, deficit reduction cannot be sustained, new jobs cannot be created, the unemployment rate does not fall but rises, hopelessness increases, and extremist ideas and opinions take over. the council took the long-awaited first step by putting the issue of employment, and in particular youth employment, on its agenda. however, the measures decided are insufficient and lack ambition. immediate decisions and action, and a more targeted use of the eu resources on offer, are needed to ensure that today's young people do not feel that they are europe's 'unnecessary generation'.
in writing. i welcome the fact that representatives of all member states are calling for proposals to strengthen competitiveness, consolidate national budgets and create additional jobs. you want to focus on three areas: encouraging the employment of young people, completing the single european market and increasing aid to the sme sector, reducing its burden. in my view, these three directions are good, but their measures are late and incomplete. the possibility of introducing a transaction tax is still rejected, although it is considered economically reasonable that the amount of eur 200 billion from it could give a new impetus to the european business sector, thus generating internal consumption in the union. the millions of young unemployed will undoubtedly be helped by the support of new programmes for upskilling, training and scholarships, but until we sanction employers and encourage them to recruit new young workers, no breakthroughs can be expected in this area. the council's statements did not calm the markets: interest rates on portuguese government bonds, for example, at 17-20%, have not decreased at all in the past two days. i, as a member of the s&d group, take the view that the inclusion of the treaties on economic stability and consolidation in a definitive framework does not provide an adequate answer to the union's current economic and social problems and does not serve the purpose of a united europe. good work takes time, they say. at this point, we're running out of time...
madam high representative, please. as a member of the itre committee, i would like to ask you a question: specifically about the energy embargo imposed by the european union on iran, which i believe is a clear political decision. when you made this decision, did you give any thought to the serious consequences this could have for the security of energy supply in europe? how are we going to explain to our voters here in europe why a litre of fuel costs more than 2 euros and how are we going to solve the problem of the queues at petrol stations? i ask this in light of the fact that, to my knowledge, the iranian state has allowed the iaea's board of inquiry to visit its territory, and at this time, no evidence has been found or any evidence that iran is actually developing nuclear energy for war purposes.
thank you for the floor, mr president, and i would like to congratulate you in particular. i'll continue in english. iran's nuclear program is i think without any question a source of potential danger for the wider west asian region, but we shouldn't allow ourselves to be mesmerised by the nuclear issue because that way we run the risk of missing several other strategic developments. for one, iran is a major power in conventional military terms; indeed militarily it hardly needs nuclear weapons. second, iran has the potential to block the strait of hormuz. this has already been mentioned. this would affect oil exports from the entire gulf region and it would involve around a fifth of the world's oil consumption. then, with iraq gradually evolving into an iranian satellite, tehran's power has increased markedly. the uprising in syria, another of iran's allies, directly affects iran's interest, hence the support extended to the assad regime. in all it is not hard to see the ultimate objective of iran's strategy: control of the gulf, the neutralization of saudi arabia and the exclusion of the united states from the region. the nuclear question is only a part of these strategic issues.

in writing. russia has lagged behind in the past decade in socio-political and economic reforms that are essential for modernisation. in the latter, even china has overtaken russia, as indicated by the 10-year gap between the two countries' wto accession. the russian leadership must urgently initiate comprehensive economic and political reforms. you need to restore the political institutions and the freedom of the press. it must embark on comprehensive economic reforms to kick-start growth. it must strengthen the rule of law and curb corruption. the russian example also shows how harmful it is when a single political force gains supreme power, a constituent majority and removes the checks and balances of power. such practices must be combated within and beyond the borders of the european union. the protests that have been going on for two months now following the russian elections have highlighted the multi-level crisis of the political system in russia. the political capital of the putin-medvedev tandem that controls the country has been depleted, and the support for the two politicians has been significantly reduced. the special system of directed democracy, which tied the functioning of political parties to the support of the executive for the sake of political stability, also came to a standstill. he adapted the electoral law to the expectations of those who govern the country. he restricted the freedom of the press, the right to freedom of assembly and association. this is a warning example for authoritarian policies in the union.

in writing. – the participation of the most disadvantaged communities in sport activities and their access to related facilities is way below the average. their higher involvement in competitive or mass sport would not only improve their physical and mental health but would also serve as an effective tool for social inclusion and education. moreover, extending the sport sector could also function as an employment creation factor, particularly in disadvantaged regions. the full access to sport should therefore not only be viewed as a matter of leisure, but as a social service of general social and economic interest. member states must take appropriate measures to increase the number of socially disadvantaged children in competitive and leisure sport activities and to improve skills and develop a positive attitude to an active life for disadvantaged youth, and the structural funds could be mobilised in order to develop sport facilities and opportunities so that disadvantaged children can engage in sport outside school in disadvantaged regions. special incentives – such as scholarships – might also be developed to help disadvantaged children enter the talent care programmes of all main branch associations.

in writing. in my view, the 14th company law directive on the crossborder relocation of companies is essentially intended to strengthen the union's competitiveness. this requires a dynamic and flexible company law. as we have seen, even a broad interpretation of the most significant judgments is not sufficient to dispel all the uncertainties that have arisen in relation to the freedom of establishment and to create complete legal certainty. and that's what the economy needs. in contrast to restrictions, the competitiveness of the union as a whole can be helped by regulatory competition at corporate and tax law level, which may result from the free choice of the seat. companies are free to choose where they want to have their headquarters. member states are obliged to adjust their legislation if they wish to avoid the emigration of companies which opt for a more favourable legal regime. all this leads to indirect harmonisation within the community. the question of how to proceed is left behind. it has now been established that the criticisms which, in principle, called into question the future and the validity of this directive were unfounded. however, it is clear that the rules that have been established are not without flaws. and your future destiny will be determined by your usefulness and your usefulness.
it is clearly welcome that the european parliament has an opinion on tax policy issues in europe. the report rightly recognises the importance of coordination of tax policies in the exchange of information, the absence of which in the long term leads to taxation opacity and the possibility of tax avoidance. however, this in itself is much more than a lack of millions of euros, as over time the smooth functioning of the single market and cross-border businesses may be at stake. however, it should be clarified that, as long as tax policy properly remains a matter of national competence, member states should cooperate in making tax policies, which also represent the main revenue for their budgets, transparent and comparable, as well as the tax collection system and the tax planning strategies used. however, in order to make europe competitive in the fight against tax avoidance and double taxation in the context of different tax policies, the european community itself needs to be more actively involved in addition to the oecd.
i am voting in favour of this report because i believe that strengthening positive relations with the bric countries is in the vital interest of the european union, and thus of hungary, in today's socially and economically accelerating world order. the strengthening of this relationship is well supported by the fact that these countries' growth potential is becoming more and more prominent in the world, thereby claiming more and more space for themselves in shaping world politics. this rapid development affects the interests of the european union and its member states in all aspects, both economically and politically. this situation has, of course, direct implications for the union's economic, foreign and security policy, and i am thinking here in particular of chinese investments. i therefore believe that the european union should play a proactive role in engaging with the bric countries, thereby strengthening its global role.
as a lawyer, i voted in favour of the report on the directive on the transfrontier relocation of companies. i did so because the freedom of establishment enshrined in articles 49 and 54 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union clearly serves the european union's aim of achieving the single market at all levels by breaking down national frontiers. strengthening the single market is in our common interest, since it is in the interest of all member states that the european union should be a genuine free area without restrictions. by extending the freedom of establishment to enterprises, investments and business establishments of the hungarian economy are also fully protected and secure as part of the internal market. the very successful removal of restrictions so far makes the union and the member states individually competitive in the world. we must support this endeavour, as the case law of the european court of justice confirms.

in writing. − the 2008 judgment of the european court of justice in a hungarian case confirms the urgent need for a harmonised system for the crossborder relocation of companies within the union. the adoption of the report sends a clear signal to the commission to get to work and put forward a legislative proposal on the subject.
in writing. − collective enforcement increases legal certainty and can have a preventive effect on businesses that normally cause damage of a small amount but affecting many people. in such cases, the deterrent for the injured party is the risk of incurring legal costs, because the cost of individual enforcement usually exceeds the expected benefit. i want to emphasize that i do not support the widespread abuse of unfounded class actions in america to invade europe. however, i believe that a uniform european regulation on collective redress would significantly reduce consumer distrust and could be a suitable tool for developing a practical and more effective remedy that would serve the interests of both the injured parties and the companies.
in writing. − in the interests of the european union and its member states and of international peace, i have voted in favour of the resolution of the european parliament on iran and its nuclear programme. in recent months, iran has undermined the european union's efforts towards international peace on several occasions. this included repeated violations of articles 2 and 3 of the npt, secrecy about its nuclear programme and threats to close the strait of hormuz, which, as a strategic point, could have a very serious impact on europe's oil supply. this measure may also undermine the relatively calm situation in the region. based on all of this, and the iaea report, i believe that iran's nuclear program is not for peaceful purposes. the negative measures already taken against him and those to be taken pursuant to the council decision of 23 january 2012 are therefore, in my view, justified and serve the interests of the international peaceful world order and the european union.
in writing. − as a former minister for youth and sport, i am convinced that sport plays a decisive role in both the economy and society. sport is a link between citizens of the union, since it is open to all citizens, regardless of gender, ethnic origin, religion, age, nationality or social status. the economic added value of sport in the union is significant: it represents 15 million jobs (5.4% of the active population) and contributes 3.65% of european gdp. in addition, sport has a positive impact on the health budgets of the member states, as it has a positive impact on the health of european citizens. we must not forget that sport can also play an important role in boosting tourism. in the light of all this, i agree with the rapporteur that the economic and social role of sport needs to be further strengthened. the fact that sport can benefit from the european structural funds is an important step in this direction. special training is essential for elite athletes who wish to pursue higher education. the fight against doping must be continued unabated. sport should be promoted in education, as it (among many other positive effects) helps to break down social barriers and to integrate marginalised groups. sport is an effective means of strengthening european identity. to this end, i support the rapporteur's proposal to organise an annual european day of sport, to elect a 'european capital of sport' every year and to set up mobility programmes for young athletes to learn new training methods while promoting intercultural dialogue.
in writing. the european union is undergoing rapid and dramatic change. some challenges and problems, although of varying probability, are predictable: recovery from the crisis, the consequences of global competition and the difficulties in managing sustainable development. but there is no doubt that the eu will face challenges that are not yet visible. however, it is also clear that, since the existence of competition policy, it has always been able to cope with a major transformation of its environment, against the background of the stable framework of the treaty. the eu's competition policy remains one of the european union's greatest assets, thanks to its flexibility and adaptability. i therefore consider it important that the adoption of the commission's guidelines on enforcement priorities for the application of anti-competitive and abusive conduct by dominant undertakings has succeeded in embedding the consumer-centred economic approach in the framework for the implementation of antitrust measures. i support the measures outlined by rapporteur andreas schwab, as the effective application of eu competition policy tools is in the interest of all european citizens, both within and outside the union, and is therefore a duty of the european parliament.

madam president! my fellow members of the house of representatives! based on the experience of the last few years, we can confidently say that january is the month of gas crises. in early 2009, russian gas was cut off for several weeks. last year, the ukraine-russia dispute raised questions about whether europe should receive russian gas. things haven't improved this year either. in january, gas from russia fell significantly overnight in a member state of 9 million. my fellow members of the house of representatives! we have a solution. it has been said many times in this house that we need new resources. however, the construction of the import pipelines seems to take too long. in the short term, therefore, efforts should be made to build at least interconnectors between member states. the security of supply of the most vulnerable countries in central and eastern europe can only be ensured by connecting them with the western european member states which already have alternative sources of supply. this solution is in the interest of the whole european union.

in writing. − the single register of companies is not only useful for consumers, but also has significant advantages for businesses, as free access can save businesses time and considerable costs. this could mean savings of up to tens of millions of euros. unfortunately, at present, business operators face many difficulties because the business registers in each member state contain only certain types of data, the information is not always up to date, access is difficult and the translation of the data is often not available. it is for these reasons that i believe that the further integration of the internal market in this area should be a top priority and that it is therefore important to remove the existing legal and practical obstacles as soon as possible. today, more and more companies are advertising their services across national borders, which has led to a significant increase in online shopping within the union, for example. it is therefore necessary, in the interests of consumer safety, to establish a system to monitor the lawfulness of the companies in question. the aim of the proposal under discussion is precisely to increase legal certainty and transparency in the legal relationship between consumers and companies, and i therefore support the report.
in writing. − with the increase in cross-border mobility of companies, there has been a recent increase in the need for information on companies to be made publicly available throughout europe, equally to citizens of any member state. however, trade repositories are currently regulated differently at member state and, in some cases, regional level, which creates legal uncertainty for third parties and hinders the restoration of confidence in markets. in my view, the effective functioning of the internal market requires that economic operators have easy access to up-to-date and reliable company information. i think that the adoption of this proposal will bring us closer to that goal.

in writing. the agricultural trade agreement with morocco is contrary to the interests of european consumers and farmers. it is contrary to the interests of consumers as it would facilitate the entry into the union markets of fruit and vegetable products that are often treated with plant protection products already banned in the eu. in recent years, peppers from morocco have been found in hungary containing banned chemical residues. the agreement also runs counter to the interests of european farmers, who must comply with increasingly stringent eu environmental and food safety rules, while imports from morocco are not subject to these rules. the spanish farmers pointed out that the european commission had not carried out any impact assessment on the likely effects of imports. we agree with and support the position of the largest european economic association, copa-cogeca, of which magosz is a member, which calls for the convention to be rejected.

in writing. the package of legislation mentioned by gunnar hökmark is an important step towards strengthening the european union's competitiveness. it is gratifying to see that, in addition to tackling the economic crisis, there is finally a growing focus on long-term competitiveness. the proposal adopted states that the availability of the 800 mhz frequency band from 2013 will facilitate the creation of a pan-european telecommunications market and open up the possibility for new services to be available to users. the proposal envisages the provision of 1200 mhz for mobile data traffic by 2015, at the request of the european parliament. the allocation of frequencies is a matter for the member states, but the rules on spectrum management are the responsibility of the european union. the frequency bands released for mobile broadband contribute to the digital agenda's target of a minimum speed of 30 mbps by 2020, thus guaranteeing high download speeds even for those living in remote areas. i would like to highlight the role of the hungarian presidency in the drafting of the proposal, as during the six months of the presidency a political agreement was reached on effective frequency management. with the successful closure of the dossier, a growing part of society can benefit from the benefits of the internet, leading to economic growth and a stronger european competitiveness.

in writing. - mr president, i have to go. my fellow members of parliament! growth, fiscal consolidation, and job creation. these three areas are what will determine europe's successful future, its recovery from the protracted economic crisis. growth can be achieved through research and development, the application of new technologies, fiscal adjustment of public expenditure, the transformation of large supply systems, and the creation of new jobs by encouraging employers and expanding the skills of young workers. i therefore maintain my position that a pan-european economic policy should not be based on austerity alone. as a hungarian politician, although i believe that the treaties on economic stability, consolidation and governance and the european stability mechanism do not offer a clear cure for the situation in europe, it is in my country's fundamental interest to join them. i welcome the fact that the hungarian government, led by fidesz, has changed its initial negative, withdrawn position and committed itself to signing the agreement. however, it should be noted that these measures may be temporary, but in the long term they will certainly not solve the future of europe. we need to find new sources of energy that will increase consumption, stimulate micro, small and medium-sized enterprises and thus create new jobs. the introduction of eurobonds and a transaction tax, which have long been the subject of debate in brussels, could provide a solution. my fellow members of parliament! the road ahead is not smooth, and perhaps the end is not in sight, which is why we must have particularly precise, predictable solutions.
mr. president, my dear officer, my dear colleagues. as the employment coordinator of the epp group, i welcome mrs berès' report on the employment guidelines. and i'm doing that because i was the lead reporter for the initial report in 2010. so we thought we'd put together a guideline that would remain unchanged through 2014. we thought we'd review and renew it on a yearly basis, on a regular schedule. i was secretly hoping that it wouldn't. i was hoping that perhaps the results would require a change, perhaps a change to more ambitious objectives. unfortunately, that's not the case. the 2010 guidelines have not been repealed. the unemployment figures are still bad, dramatic, and in some ways, even worse. the unemployment rate went from 9.5 to 9.9 percent, right? there are nearly 24 million unemployed, and this has been said before. and what's even more dramatic is that five and a half million is essentially the number of young unemployed. this calls for immediate action. i therefore welcome the council's decision of 30 january, which specifically focuses on the problem of youth unemployment. i think it's the right thing to do, and i think it's the right thing to do, even if there are countries where things are a little bit better. so i would like to emphasize and call on the member countries to give priority to the youth in action flagship initiative. indeed, getting young people into careers within four months is a key issue for the future. i am of course in favour of keeping the guidelines in place.
mr president, i welcome the fact that the committee on employment and social affairs has drawn up this report and i also hope that parliament’s position will be taken into due account. youth unemployment figures in europe are extremely worrying and they are getting worse – 5.6 million young people are unemployed, which is the equivalent of 22% in the eu. this is twice as high as the adult unemployment rate, which stands at 10%. we risk condemning an entire generation to despair and social exclusion because young people are being forced into inactivity. therefore the party of european socialists, under its campaign ‘your future is my future’, has to put concrete solutions on the agenda. we want a european youth guarantee that will ensure that every young person in europe is offered a job, further education or work-focused training within four months at the latest of leaving education or becoming unemployed. society needs young women and men, and their talents and skills, on the labour market in order to strengthen europe. yes, it will cost us eur 10 billion, but if they remain unemployed it will cost the european union eur 100 billion every year.
in writing. – i welcome the fact that tackling the social consequences of the crisis is one of the five priorities listed in the recent annual growth survey. it is very important that the sector of employment and social affairs is not overshadowed by the focus on fiscal consolidation, and that the headline target of increasing employment is kept on the agenda and further promoted by investing in sustainable job creation. it must be ensured that consolidation measures will not have further negative impacts on people at risk of poverty and/or social exclusion. the commission should therefore put more emphasis on guiding member states in coordinating their national reform programmes with the goals of the europe 2020 strategy, in particular increasing employment and introducing effective incentives for work. the successful launch of the national roma inclusion strategies and its european framework is indispensable in this respect, since roma constitute a huge reserve of idle workforce and are disproportionately over-represented among those living in poverty. increasing the employment of roma – so that this approximates to the regional average – would already trigger substantial economic growth that many countries with a significant roma population and hit by the sovereign debt crisis are so desperately in need of.
mr. president, i have to go. the 2012 annual growth survey rightly emphasises that the success of economic governance depends to a large extent on the fiscal austerity of individual member states, but that it is equally important to kick-start economic growth and at the same time complete the internal market. unfortunately, this has not been given enough attention in the six-package or the current fiscal pact. but there is no point in member states cutting public spending in the short and medium term if structural reforms are not implemented. the commission is not satisfied with the reforms and efforts of the member states. it wants a quick result, it urges, it forces these results, which leads to unnecessary austerity and further increase in unemployment. that is why this report is good, and why it is important that the commission finally raises the issue of putting this back at the centre of its country-specific recommendations.
mr. president, i have to go. i accept your generosity, and i will next time.
mr. president, i have to go. i voted no to the amendment rejecting the council's position. the union's frequency policy is part of its competitiveness and i agree that the european union should strive to be a leader in terms of speed, mobility, coverage and capacity of broadband services. the legislative proposal reflects well the emergence of a triple priority: consumers, content and competition. increased demand from consumers indicates that additional frequencies are needed in terms of both capacity and coverage. content, i.e. universal access to digital services, is helping to deliver the eu 2020 strategy. an effective spectrum policy means that consumers can choose between providers, thus creating healthy competition. the way in which the frequencies are sold varies from country to country, and i think that a common allocation mechanism is needed. finally, it should not be forgotten that a well-designed spectrum policy and frequency expansion are also a prerequisite for the growth of the cultural and creative industries.
in writing. − the economic crisis and the resulting rise in food prices are having a serious negative impact on the food security of the most deprived eu citizens. i therefore welcome the fact that parliament opted to accept the council’s position without amendments since, following a two-year obstruction, any further delay would worsen the situation and undermine the credibility of the programme. both the commission and the council have taken on board most of the ep’s recommendations at first reading, so the scheme will be based solely on food of community origin, purchased on a competitive basis, with preference given to locally-produced fresh products, and with a clear obligation for distribution points to advertise the eu’s involvement. it is also very important that storage costs incurred by charitable organisations will be reimbursed and that, by doubling the sources of supply, distribution will no longer be limited to products to which intervention applies, so member states in cooperation with civil society can make their choices on the basis of nutritional balance. further issues, such as the review of the 100 % ratio of eu funding and the introduction of co-financing, might be discussed again later, given the possibility of reviewing the plan in exceptional circumstances.
in writing. − in addition to my opinion on the pan-euro-mediterranean system of cumulation of origin, i support not only the extension of the diagonal system of cumulation but also its practical introduction and integration into the eu regulatory system. i agree to the conclusion of the agreement, as it is the conclusion of the barcelona process. this agreement will enable the eu to respond effectively to the overly complex mechanisms that have arisen with its mediterranean partners in the context of existing bilateral arrangements, and to the new opportunities and challenges that have emerged in the context of the arab spring. i would like to stress in particular the desire to extend the diagonal cumulation system to the western balkan countries as a long-term objective, thereby facilitating agreements with future new members from this region.
in writing. − one of the main reasons for the 2008 crisis in the european dairy sector was the increase in milk quotas voted in the council by the previous socialist government. the fidesz european parliament delegation opposed both the increase in quotas and the subsequent abolition of the quota system from 2015. in our view, supply-limiting measures would be necessary even if the quota system were to be abolished. however, the current proposals of the european commission on the milk market could lead to the single internal market for milk being broken up into member states' markets, where different national rules may apply to the sale of milk. i've submitted a motion to amend in the interests of the producers. this would mean that in the case of a crossborder transport contract the rules of the place of residence of the owner would apply, but my proposal did not receive a majority.
in writing. - mr prime minister! first of all, i would like to thank you for your report today, in which you also highlighted the sad fact that the growth-enhancing aspects of european policies continue to receive insufficient attention. but i must emphasize at this point that these proposals are only useful if they can be integrated into the common european economic policy framework and, in addition, fit into a well-constructed crisis mechanism. i am concerned that the commission always sets out ambitious plans, and that the positive impact is sometimes only marginal. the range of measures taken to reduce youth unemployment is a good example. there is no doubt that long-term reform of labour markets is needed, but reforms that are delayed are slow to deliver the expected results. a pan-european crisis map is needed to identify and demarcate the most affected crisis areas on the basis of their most fundamental characteristics. the availability of jobs, the rate of unemployment, the quality of public services and other environmental conditions must therefore be taken into account. if we do not do this, europe's problems could become a cause for further concern and we could find ourselves facing another wave of discontent. in addition to addressing the challenges arising from the current economic crisis, it is therefore essential to step up our joint efforts to ensure sustainable and employment-enhancing growth.
mr president, i think we can all agree that what is happening in syria – the use of force against people calling for political change – is unacceptable and deplorable. we have heard a great deal this afternoon on this topic. the question in my mind is: why are syria’s rulers so obstinate in resisting change of any kind? the assad regime has not offered a single meaningful concession to the social forces demanding change. i think this requires an explanation that few of the assessments have so far offered. it is a mistake to see syria as a unified society, far from it: it is wholly divided, with basically five major groups living side by side. the group that has monopolised power is the alawites, to which assad’s own extended family belongs. the alawites have co-opted the druze, the christians and the shia. taken together, they make up about 30% of the population. the majority of those at the heart of the anti-assad movement are sunni muslims, who have been very largely excluded from power and its benefits by the assad system. the fear of the ruling establishment is that a simple majority system would put power in the hands of the sunni majority, with no guarantees that other groups would be included in a future regime run by the sunnis. any settlement, if it is to work, must take these factors into account. otherwise, sadly, the fighting and killing will go on, because neither side will see any benefit from a settlement. international guarantees for the security and wellbeing of syria’s minorities are essential if a settlement is to last.

in writing. − i supported the resolution on the future of global environmental and security surveillance (gmes) as i believe it is important to maintain an already established eu-wide earth observation system. by comprehensively examining the global environmental and security situation in five areas - land, sea, air, emergency response and security - gmes helps europe to respond in advance to any adverse environmental and security policy changes. furthermore, the system, by virtue of its characteristic of providing basic services accessible to private individuals, can also promote european and national innovation. the reason is that the funding framework is only provided until 2013, after which time not only the material and intellectual energy invested in the system would be wasted, but also our common aspiration to develop a branch of european space research. that is why i thought it was important to support the resolution on gmes.
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, parliament is commemorating the 101st international women’s day by putting two very important reports on its agenda: on equality between women and men in the european union and on women in political decision-making. the goal that equal work deserves equal pay, set more than one and a half centuries ago by the protesting female labourers of new york, is still valid today. women earn 15% less in the european union and, although more than half of the total voting population is female, they only share 10% of the global income. in addition to eliminating the gender pay gap, still pending are the proper recognition and reinforcement of women’s role in families and enforcing the principle of equal treatment. it is unacceptable that being a woman and being a mother still constitute a disadvantage and a risk poverty factor in 21st century europe. besides the importance – as commissioner reding recalled – of the presence of highly qualified women in leading positions in the public and private sector, much more emphasis should also be placed on improving the situation of vulnerable women, such as single mothers or orphan girls, elderly, minority and immigrant women, mothers of large families and those who interrupted their studies because of childbirth, as they are especially exposed to the risk of poverty and multiplied discrimination. for them the glass ceiling is much thicker than we would think. women in ethnic minorities, especially roma women in europe, have completely different problems from their male counterparts or the majority of women and find themselves in an especially vulnerable position when it comes to child care and state child-care facilities. this crisis compels us to take into account, at once and seriously, the economic importance of gender equality because gender-based exclusion is not only unfair – as we always say – but offsets the economy as well. member states and the private sector should be encouraged to integrate gender experts into their crisis management. all the political groups in this parliament are agreed that, in spite of the programmes set up in the last few years, no real achievement has really been made when it comes to equal opportunities. although both of the reports ask questions that address many of the issues – and, as some of them come under the principle of subsidiarity, many colleagues are finding it difficult to agree with them – we in the ppe still believe that the questions raised during the discussions of the two reports are extremely important and should be kept at the top of the european agenda.
madam president, some of you, hopefully very few people, might wonder why we are talking about gender equality when we have just celebrated international women’s day last week and when we also have the crisis to deal with. the reason is that, even though there have been developments, and goals have been achieved, a lot still needs to be done when it comes to gender equality. i will not let anyone use the crisis as an excuse for not doing this. the gender pay gap remains at an unacceptable level, gender-based violence is still taking thousands of victims every year, sexual and reproductive rights are threatened, women have difficulties on the labour market – just to give you a few examples. i am asking for a clear commitment from the commission and from the member states to change these unacceptable conditions. clear commitment in my understanding means binding measures and quotas. that is what i have been calling for, for a long while now. it is true that it will take some political will. this is why i say that we need more women in decision-making positions. i do not expect men to fully grasp our difficulties and i do not require them to do so. we will work with them in our common interest. all we need is the possibility to do so at regional, national and european level. commissioner reding, let us have the campaign, as we had with margot wallström in 2009; let us have the campaign on parity, because this is a great example for european citizens that we are for women, for men, and for equal treatment for women and men. i believe that we can conduct a very good campaign. it is good for us; it is good for europe, because if you educate a man you simply educate the man; if you educate the woman, you educate society. that is what we badly need.

the single market, the free movement of capital, services and labour is one of the union's most important achievements. more and more cross-border relationships are being established, both in the personal and business spheres. this requires, on the one hand, the settlement of cross-border civil, commercial and family disputes at the level of the member states and, on the other hand, the mutual recognition of judgments and decisions of the courts of the member states. but freedom of movement also requires that european citizens have confidence in the legal certainty of each member state, in the readiness of the law enforcement authorities, in the independence of the courts and in the independence of the courts from political influence. it is therefore important to know each other's legal systems, to know common european standards and directives for lawyers, and to apply them properly. as the shadow rapporteur on the 2014-2020 justice programme, i consider it important that sufficient and well-spent resources are available to train judges, lawyers and to build this trust, to guarantee the conditions. lawyers should know and apply eu law, and should feel that they can embrace both the letter and the spirit of eu law.

mr president, the decision of the european council to endorse serbia’s candidacy has a two-fold significance. with respect to serbia, it marks the definitive end to the aftermath of the wars of the yugoslav succession, and the lingering assumption widely held at the time that the serbs were somehow the hereditary villains of the balkans. with the acceptance of serbia’s candidacy, that particular canard has been laid to rest. serbia can now be regarded as having returned to the european family and this has its long-term significance. after all, stability in south-eastern europe is inconceivable without serbia – and stability here has to extend to all policy areas, notably crime. without serbia’s full commitment in the fight against organised crime, the balkans would continue to constitute an easy prey for transnational mafias. but the step has a second, possibly greater significance. it makes it clear that eu enlargement, though moving forward only slowly, has not stalled. this obviously has implications for the rest of south-eastern europe. what is equally noteworthy is that, despite the economic crisis, the eu continues its commitment to enhancing the stability of its neighbourhood, relying on soft power to achieve this. the encouragement of the countries of the eastern partnership and the planned elaboration of a road map are evidently steps in the direction of ensuring that objective. all these strategies and policy objectives, however, depend on overcoming the long drawn-out economic crisis, the central concern of the summit. here the decisions point in the right direction and it is absolutely crucial that fiscal discipline goes hand in hand with the recognition of the importance of economic growth. in the absence of this, the eu will find itself much less attractive and with much reduced soft power.
for the first time in two years, the european council did not deal with firefighting, but the fire was not yet out. europe is still in a danger zone, because we will not be able to get out of the debt spiral without economic growth. a lot has been said: the development of the internal market, the early introduction of eurobonds, but i am convinced that we also need a comprehensive eu growth programme, an eu development package to stimulate job creation. on the other hand, there is a fundamental structural problem with the way the euro works. it has been found that the euro, as a single currency, is not suited to managing the economies of countries with different levels of competitiveness. the economic difficulties of greece, ireland, spain, portugal, the near bankruptcy of their governments, are not only the fault of their governments, but also the failure of the euro as a whole. the euro therefore needs to be fundamentally reformed!
in writing. at the last summit, a majority of member states signed the fiscal compact, which is responsible for the stability of economic and monetary union and for its even closer coordination. this was not only another intergovernmental agreement, but also the culmination of a very long process in which the european parliament provided key support and expertise. however, it is important that, in addition to the rules of the common economic policy, which clearly provide a clear framework for the budgetary policies of the member states, the european union should in future assess the member states' actions in the field of common economic governance in a transparent manner, on the basis of criteria that are quantified in the stability and growth pact. the council conclusions also indicate that fiscal consolidation should be implemented in a differentiated manner, taking into account the specific circumstances of the member states. it is therefore unacceptable to make an unfair assessment of the performance of individual member states on the basis of subjective factors, without recognising that the tendency of the economic policy of a member state is to avoid austerity in the medium and long term and to pursue the commonly agreed objectives of reducing the budget deficit and gradually reducing the public debt. if the successfully established economic policy system is operated irresponsibly, with unnecessary differences between member states and with constant tension, the credibility of the whole european institutional system and the sustainability of the common economic management system are at stake.
in writing. – i am particularly worried after the signature of the fiscal compact during last european council. we, socialists and democrats, have been defending from the start balanced budgets and fiscal responsibility, but this cannot be the only reaction to the dreadful crisis europe is facing. while we witness every day the dramatic social consequences of measures focused exclusively on austerity, we urgently need concrete measures to restore growth and tackle unemployment. i would like to emphasise the issue of youth unemployment, which is now reaching an average rate of more than 22% in europe. this is unacceptable. that is why the party of european socialists, with the support of the s&d group, has launched a campaign to tackle youth unemployment, calling for binding measures and sufficient financial support to ensure the access of young people to quality jobs. therefore i strongly hope that the commission and the member states will take into consideration our demand to introduce a youth guarantee that would prevent any young european from being without a job or training for more than four months. how can we prepare the future of europe if we do not do everything we can to ensure a decent future for our children?

in writing. - first of all, i would like to thank mr lechner, because we can all be proud of the enormous work he has done, especially since this is mr lechner's last report, given that he will leave the ep in march this year, after 14 years. i am pleased that we have the opportunity today to vote on this subject which has been long awaited by our citizens. as i live in sopron, a border town, i am particularly aware of the administrative and enforcement difficulties that citizens often face in cross-border cases. this unfortunately reduces legal certainty, which is nevertheless of paramount importance in the field of succession law. i supported the report because its adoption will significantly simplify the handling of cross-border succession cases, giving citizens a secure, predictable basis for the settlement of their legacies, saving them a significant amount of time, energy and money. although the report has not been the subject of much international media scrutiny, i think that votes like this really show the value of our work. we meps are here to address these issues and to represent the real interests of european citizens! we represent them, rather than debating the unfounded accusations that are heated up by certain political interest groups and affect only certain member states!
in writing. − i supported the report 'the contribution of the eu institutions to consolidating and advancing the bologna process', as high quality higher education is one of the cornerstones of the future of the european union. in my view, the objective of the report, which is to provide compatible higher education that is attractive to students from outside the member states, is an important step towards achieving employment mobility with appropriate language competences. this kind of mobility leads to a significant reduction in unemployment, including frictional unemployment. it should also be noted that the cooperation resulting from the process makes a significant contribution to the exchange and exploitation of experience and to research and development. it is therefore of the utmost importance that universities in the member states are able to establish appropriate cooperation with higher education institutions in other non-member countries, and that these links can lead to the promotion and dissemination of culture. it is very important that quality education and the opportunities offered by the bologna process are made available to citizens of all member states. i therefore consider that it is worth considering further the development of a uniform, objective selection system for students who may continue their studies abroad.

in writing. we regret to inform you that the dutch prime minister did not appear today at the debate on the extremist dutch website that insults eastern european citizens. while european liberals attack populist governments for partisan purposes, citing fundamental rights, the liberal dutch prime minister openly collaborates with the extremist freedom party, which maintains the website. we find it deeply outrageous that the website of the freedom party incites the dutch population against eastern european citizens on the basis of origin. such manifestations and their tacit tolerance undermine trust between member states at a time when solidarity needs to be strengthened. you can't hide behind freedom of speech when other citizens' fundamental rights to privacy are trampled on. we cannot tolerate the humiliation of human dignity and the questioning of equality of origin. we cannot tolerate openly insulting the citizens of eastern europe. the dutch website case is not an abstract case but a concrete case where fundamental rights are violated in practice. but the liberal prime minister is not shutting down the website, despite widespread international calls. such behaviour undermines eu integration and weakens the belief in common values.
in writing. – there has been an alarming and accelerating increase in the amount of discriminatory online content in the past few years. according to the estimates of the simon wiesenthal centre, there are approximately ten thousand online groups, blogs, chat rooms, videos and games that were created by racist, homophobic and anti-semitic persons, the most serious of which are of course those operated by terrorist groups and other criminal associations. the commission and member states must consider developing a code of conduct for combating online incitement of hatred, and modifying their legislation to effectively respond to the new challenges of information technology. first, those websites that are fundamentally discriminatory in nature or instigate crimes need to be shut down; the national and international procedural rules for doing this need to be properly established. second, discriminatory content appearing in mainstream websites and most typically in social networks must also be rejected. although these pages have so many visitors that supervising them is almost impossible, authorities need to reach agreements with content providers and server hosts to at least limit such activities. third, it must be considered that certain websites might be discriminatory by negligence, such as official and administrative pages of public interest that are non-accessible for people with disabilities.

in writing. the proper allocation of the 2013 budget is particularly important for both the member states and the eu as a whole, as the impact of the crisis continues to be felt and the urgency for effective, concrete action is growing. one of the most important issues is the issue of smes. they are the engine of our economy, as they generate the vast majority of new jobs. our europe 2020 strategy sets out that the union's fundamental objectives are to create and maintain economic growth, jobs and competitiveness. i believe that we need to provide significant resources for smes, to provide the right environment to help start-ups. boosting and enlivening the sme sector will result in higher labour market demand. in this context, the focus should be on a topic that is also relevant, namely young people, who are the future of the eu. the funds allocated to education and research and development and innovation are also indirectly allocated to smes. if you think about how many entrepreneurial-minded young people there are, and how many of them are willing to start their own businesses. instead, they are profiting from their creative, innovative ideas in different multinational companies, which is a barrier to the eu's competitiveness. there are a number of alternatives that can help boost the sector, such as investing in business incubation centres, whether it be start-up centres that help and support start-ups, or technology centres, which are the right kind of community for innovative, technology-oriented companies. to do so, the budget needs new resources, which may be the issuance of eurobonds or the introduction of a transaction tax.
in writing. the 2013 budget of the european union is the closure of the current seven-year financial framework. member states will be reaching their peak in the number of catch-up programmes this year, with the largest amounts to be paid out now. at the same time, the 2013 eu budget will also reflect the views of the institutions on the future, as, in the event of no agreement, this year will be the base for the next period. the european parliament sees the european budget as a means of overcoming the crisis, as a motor for growth and job creation: we want a strong europe, guided by common policies and adequately resourced. some member states would reduce their own contributions in order to meet short-term objectives in the interests of a smaller eu budget. the risks are high: a decline in european solidarity, a loss of trust in the union, a turn away from the eu institutions. in the long term, this could even threaten the very existence of the union. in the medium term, the success of the europe 2020 programme will also be called into question if the financial means necessary to achieve it are not allocated to it. we must not hesitate to show our citizens that we live in a union which uses its resources wisely and well in order to achieve common goals and to strengthen the european idea.

in writing. i am pleased that the draft resolution focuses on issues such as the peaceful coexistence of the macedonian peoples and the situation of national minorities. the text includes a section on the significance of the ohrid convention, signed 10 years ago, which was based on the principle of mutual respect for national identity and laid the foundation for the settlement of interethnic conflicts. the laws that have been adopted and amended in the spirit of the convention are welcome. however, as i have stressed in other speeches, all laws are worth what they actually do in practice. the basic condition for peaceful coexistence is the proper upbringing of the rising generation, the promotion of sensitivity and positive relations with neighbours. a new generation raised in this spirit is the key to the emergence of an independent and open multicultural civil society. it must be ensured that every child has access to education in his or her mother tongue from appropriate textbooks. but it is equally important that, in addition to his own language and culture, he should learn the language, culture and history of his traditional neighbours from an early age. the pre-accession facility should emphasise support for such mutual understanding projects and existing civil society organisations. the establishment of a historical committee of experts from the peoples living together can also be a means of building understanding and good neighbourly relations between neighbouring nations. all this leads to the prevention of discrimination on the basis of nationality, mutual acceptance and the assumption of ethnic identity.
mr president, may i thank, among others, doris pack, for her great report. first, let me underline that i firmly believe in the european future and prospects of bosnia and herzegovina, along with its western balkan neighbours. i also welcome the recent appointment of mr bevanda as prime minister, which puts an end to 15 months of political crisis. however, though progress has been made, much remains to be done regarding fundamental reforms with a view to eu integration. i would like to insist here on the human and social rights issues. though human rights and protection of minorities are broadly ensured, constitutional changes still have to be made regarding compliance with the european convention on human rights as well as the effective implementation of major human rights conventions. moreover, though state-level anti-discrimination legislation has been put in place, its implementation remains very weak, particularly with regard to the protection of women against violence and the social protection of children. finally, i would like to highlight my concerns regarding the number of divided schools and mono-ethnic schools. i know that the scars of the past take time to heal, but i believe that much progress needs to be made in terms of inter-ethnic relations.
in writing. − i would like to remind honourable members that the issue of judicial training cooperation was raised at the hungarian initiative at eu level and was a priority in our presidency programme. the problem in this area is that, despite the acceleration of eu legislation, a significant proportion of national judges in the member states have a rather limited knowledge of eu sources of law and do not speak foreign languages to the extent that they know the legal language of other member states. as a first step, training should be made available to eu legal experts and a system should be established to ensure multilingual training. in the absence of new institutions, the starting point could be the creation of cooperation between existing national training institutions and a greater emphasis on the teaching of comparative and international legal studies. the european area of justice must be based on a common judicial culture, which in my view is based on mutual knowledge of national judicial systems, in addition to union law.
in writing. − i supported the motion for a resolution on 'child labour in the cocoa sector' as the protection of children is of paramount importance not only in europe but also worldwide. the european union must therefore use all possible means, whether it is to bring the international agreement under the roof, to develop economic alternatives for the countries concerned or, ultimately, to apply economic pressure. i believe that governments should use legal means to force the public to make schooling a primary obligation for children, since education is one of the foundations of development. where governments fail to do so on their own, it is the task of the international community to put pressure on governments to do so. i consider the withdrawal of the trade preferences contained in the resolution as part of the review of the eu's generalised system of preferences (gsp) and the legally binding and sanctioning clauses attached to trade agreements to be appropriate tools for this.
in writing. − diabetes, one of the most common of non-communicable diseases, is directly responsible for the death of 325 000 eu-citizens every year and the shocking decrease by 5-20 years of the life expectancy for 32 million people, earning its nickname as the silent assassin. diabetes and the related impaired glucose intolerance demands more than 10% of total national healthcare expenditures and researchers expect a 16,6% increase in the number of people living with diabetes by 2030 and consequently a growing burden on health care systems. despite these alarming figures, however, only 14 of the 27 eu member states have developed their national programmes to tackle the disease and its consequences and despite the initiatives of the austrian presidency in 2006, the european parliament and the un, there is still no eu level strategy to address the issue. the commission must develop a targeted strategy for the effective prevention, diagnosis, management and research of diabetes and to present specific recommendations for member states to support the development of national diabetes programmes. reliable data collection, standardized criteria for data processing and health education are also necessary, since diabetes can be effectively addressed by identifying the risk factors and promoting prevention strategies.
in writing. − i support the integration of the western balkan countries into the eu and expect the same from the representatives of the other member states, since their leaders committed themselves to their accession in the council as far back as 2003. the enlargement policy anticipates the process of social transformation, i.e. it contributes to the stability of the country. leaving the aspiring countries to their own devices poses a security risk in the region. in the case of the republic of macedonia, we should also remember that the commitment should bear fruit, otherwise the union's credibility will suffer great losses. the country fulfils the political conditions, as the commission has acknowledged. bilateral issues have no place in the accession process. the eu should seek to contribute to the reduction of tensions with the republic of macedonia, which has a high proportion of ethnic minorities of albanian origin. the macedonian public is still more in favour of accession than against, and it is not too late to start negotiations.
in writing. - i welcome the fact that macedonia was the first country to be granted candidate status. the role of the balkan region is of crucial importance for the european union and is particularly important for hungary. that's why we must not leave any country behind. in recent years, macedonia has made a number of efforts and has fought hard to become a member of the european union. the market economy is being developed in line with eu standards. the changes in the judiciary and public administration, the decentralisation of power and the efforts in the field of human rights all demonstrate the country's commitment to europe. it is important to mention that there are still ethnic problems, for example the situation of the bulgarian minority, and there is still work to be done in combating corruption and making public expenditure transparent, but the country is on the right track and i am confident that the european union will be a partner in taking all the necessary measures. i do find it sad, however, that a name dispute can prevent the future of a country from developing. we need a much broader approach than that.
in writing. − although the former yugoslav republic of macedonia took over the presidency of the decade for roma inclusion in the second half of last year, and some minimal progress has been reported in enhancing the school performance of roma children, the inclusion process of the country’s most vulnerable minority group remained unacceptably slow. there is an astonishing twenty-fold over-representation of roma children in special schools, the lack of personal documents and the extremely high unemployment still remains an unresolved problem and the percentage of children living under the poverty line even increased, reaching a shocking level of 37%. discrimination on the basis of ethnic origin is still wide-spread, especially affecting roma women and reports of police brutality and the use of excessive force against roma are a concern. it is necessary to promote dialogue between local authorities, judicial bodies, police and the roma communities in order to abolish discrimination, to improve confidence and to combat ethnic profiling. the general lack of coordination and evaluation mechanisms can only be remedied by joining the european framework for national roma inclusion strategies and elaborating a national long-term strategy for roma inclusion, including the necessary cross-sector, complex and territorially targeted development projects.

in writing. − hungary, as it has said so many times, is committed to the integration of the balkans into the european union. that is why it is welcome that a new federal government was formed in bosnia and herzegovina in february, 16 months after the elections. the government is in a difficult position. it's got to get three entities to talk and work together. ethnic and national differences still divide the country today. we must prevent all forms of extremism, because we must not become involved in ethnic disputes. we must not allow these contradictions to hold back the progress of the country. the rights of croats and other minorities living in the central part of the country are an essential condition for accession. it is important to transpose eu laws and rules more effectively into national legislation and to establish an institutional framework that promotes democracy and stability and european union integration. the adoption of the state aid and census laws and the government's priority to eu integration are welcome. however, it is necessary to amend the constitution in accordance with the european convention on human rights. now that serbia has been granted eu candidate status and croatia will join next year, the european union will share a border with bosnia and herzegovina. it is therefore important for the country to take the necessary steps that will give new hope and new impetus to the country's development and its integration into the european union. i am confident that the eu's balkan policy, including the candidate status of bosnia and herzegovina, will be successful.
in writing. − bosnia and herzegovina has developed its own national roma strategy under the framework of which it has implemented four action plans in the priority areas of the decade for roma inclusion. despite some progress in the fields of housing and education, however, the situation of the country’s roma communities in terms of health care and education still remains alarming. there are significant structural problems with regard to the sustainability and transparency of action plans, the coordination among stakeholders at state and entity level, the allocation of funds and the monitoring of projects, in brief: despite the promising beginnings, the decade initiative ran out of momentum. moreover, the state-level social inclusion strategy for 2008-13 – recognizing roma as a priority group – is still not adopted. the government, entities and local authorities must sign up for the european framework for national roma inclusion strategies and after the expiration of the current and unsuccessful strategy, bosnia and herzegovina should develop and submit to the commission its own complex roma inclusion strategy up to 2020 with special regard to improving the cooperation among bodies and policy networks, eliminating parallel, overlapping or conflicting efforts, and creating synergies.
