in writing. i welcome the fact that the report has taken into account to a large extent the opinion of the committee on agriculture and rural development which i prepared. european agriculture has a significant contribution to make to reducing the eu's carbon emissions, while creating new jobs, especially in rural areas. farmers can contribute to climate objectives by producing renewable energy, in particular biogas, while supplementing their income. the use of agricultural products for energy purposes must be such as not to endanger food security or cause food price increases. union policies need to be coordinated to increase energy efficiency and reduce emissions. therefore, only energy-efficient investments, in particular those aimed at the use of renewable energy, should be eligible for support from the rural development and other community financial funds in the future. one of the biggest problems farmers face is rising energy prices. increasing energy efficiency and the energy use of agricultural and forestry by-products could reduce their dependence on fossil fuels. this would not only help european farmers to meet climate protection objectives, but also significantly improve their competitiveness.
in writing. the 6th world water forum is being held in marseille in parallel with the european parliament's plenary session. in the 21st century, the world's dwindling freshwater resources are increasingly being competed for by geographical regions and economic sectors. it is particularly important to reduce water use in the agricultural sector, which accounts for almost 80% of global water consumption. this requires the use of economical irrigation technologies, the development of drought-tolerant crop varieties and the introduction of fair but cost-saving pricing. that is why, in the context of the reform of the common agricultural policy, i will propose that support be given to irrigation systems that promote water saving. desertification is a particularly big problem in hungary as well. on the already dry little island sand dunes, groundwater levels have dropped five meters in the past two decades alone. this is partly due to human intervention, and partly due to climate change. we need to increase the recycling of water for agricultural purposes across the union. to do this, we need to work closely with israel, where recycling is at 75%. this is well above the eu leader spain's barely 25% share, while other eu member states, such as those in the mediterranean region, are far from even this level. hungary also has a lot of work to do in terms of water recycling. eu legislation should also encourage efficient water management and recycling, as this is the only way to ensure that european companies are internationally competitive in this strategically important area.
in writing. − i supported the motion for a resolution of the european people's party on 'discriminatory websites and responses from governments', because all forms of discrimination and xenophobia are to be condemned. the dutch complaint is openly controversial in europe and encourages negative judgments based on national bias. this is incompatible with the values and objectives of the european union. i also agree with and emphasize the idea in the resolution that such an initiative could set a precedent throughout europe and even trigger similar initiatives in response. it is therefore very important that the website is shut down as soon as possible. the european union institutions, member states' governments and civil society organisations need to respond together to the economic, political and social challenges posed by migration within the union. citizens should be made aware of the benefits of migration within the member states and of the fact that, although each nation has a different culture and history, we all share the same european principles and values.
on behalf of the ppe group. – mr president, my congratulations to mr kacin. this is a thorough and cogent report. cooperation has been very good. serbia has been granted candidate status, and at the same time – this is important – elections are to be held in early may. so this report can serve as a guide for the next serbian government in its stance towards europe. a great deal has been done to make serbian accession feasible, and the country should receive due credit for all these efforts. however, as the report makes clear, this does not mean that all is done and dusted: far from it. there are many areas where the serbian system of government – and, for that matter, governance – demand a major overhaul before they meet the eu criteria. one of these areas is very serious. this is where legal provision is used, indeed abused, to eliminate competition and to stultify the market in favour of insiders. it is all the worse that this practice is undertaken in the name of the fight against organised crime. then there is kosovo. all candidate countries have to come to terms with their neighbours, whatever the antecedents. this means that serbia has to confront kosovo’s independence and abandon illusions that somehow, one day, kosovo will again be a part of the serbian state. it will not, however hard that may be to accept. thirdly, while serbia has taken a number of steps in the direction of providing equal status for its non-serbian minorities, its citizenship concept is not yet sufficiently inclusive to provide full rights to some non-serbian groups, notably the romanian speakers of the timok valley and the bulgarian minority. the model – the good model developed in vojvodina – of all minority councils should be extended to the rest of the country. the next government will have plenty on its plate.
mr. president, i have to go. we welcomed the fact that serbia has been granted candidate status, which should be followed by the opening of accession negotiations. we know this journey won't be easy. there is much to be done, but every step that is taken to meet the criteria, to build the rule of law, is somewhere in serbia that benefits people. it is an essential element of this report and i thank my colleague schöpflin and the rapporteur, mr kacin, for their openness and receptivity to the fact that, in view of the need to protect the rights of national minorities, this copenhagen criterion has been given due weight and attention in this report. i am particularly pleased that aspects of importance to the hungarian community in vojvodina have been included in the text in the form of expectations towards serbia, the observance of which can help these communities to prosper in their homeland and to have equal access to opportunities. it is important that serbia does not fall into the mistake of others, that only in order to join, only temporarily and in theory, it takes into account the interests of these national minorities, but makes it felt in practice, in everyday life, for those who live there. we'll have to keep an eye on that here.
mr. president, i have to go. first of all, i would like to thank mr jelko kacin for his report on enlargement. i would like to draw your attention in particular to the provisions of the document relating to the protection of minorities. the fulfilment of the copenhagen criteria and the relevant provisions of the lisbon treaty was a crucial precondition for serbia's final accession to the eu on 1 march. both slovakia and romania, which have minority policy deficits, can learn from the consistency of the european council in protecting serbian minorities, including romanians and vlachs. the council conclusions and the motion for a resolution on serbia itself are intended to strengthen collective minority rights, not least by providing for the continuous monitoring of serbia's minority policy. we hungarians support serbia's accession to the european union, but at the same time we demand justice for the mass killings of minorities in the past, and community rights and autonomy for our hungarian community, which still lives there. in defending the interests of the serbian hungarians, we take the example of romania, which stood firmly with the romanians of the timok valley.

the first item on the agenda is the commission statement on the distribution of food to the most deprived persons in the union.
my fellow members of parliament! the beneficiaries of the european union's food aid programme are not only the poor people who receive the food, but also the companies and businesses that receive these very large sums of money from public procurement. it doesn't matter who these companies are. i would urge my colleagues to look at the distribution of this money in their own member states. i did, and i got some pretty shocking results. hungary received nearly 4 billion forints in food aid, and a significant portion of this money was distributed among giant companies rather than small and medium-sized enterprises. just to give you some figures, gyermely corp. 660 million, diamant corp. 333 million forints. these two companies were among those that had been severely punished for cartel activity before. what is the interest of a member state in the fact that, while we are talking about supporting small and mediumsized enterprises, these giant mammoths are being given this public money? it would be very good if small farmers, small and medium-sized enterprises in any country of the european union did not feel that if they are not close to the fire, to power, to political and economic power, then they can only be losers, losers, as those farmers feel in hungary, for example, who fall off the public lands during the application process, because they can only be kissed, friends, just like the huge food aid money.
i'll close this discussion. the commission shall be assisted by the committee established by article 15 of regulation (eu) no 182/2011.
in writing. food aid for the most deprived citizens is a successful programme of the european union. under the program, thousands of tons of basic foodstuffs, such as flour and sugar, are distributed to those in need each year. the measure also ensures that consumers receive high-quality and safe products produced locally by european farmers. however, the future of the programme is in jeopardy as some member states want to reduce the eu budget and therefore want to end food aid. in our opinion, this is unacceptable. the protracted economic crisis has created a growing need to support european citizens in need. i therefore welcome the european commission's intention to maintain this measure in the new budgetary period starting in 2014.
in writing. there are concerns that the current eu food aid scheme will be phased out after the completion of the implementation of the 2013 annual plan and that the planned budgetary cuts threaten the future of the programme. the right to food is one of our fundamental rights, and the union is not abandoning those who need our help the most. it is our duty, based on the principle of solidarity, to provide support to those in need. we must act decisively to ensure that the food aid programme is maintained, as it is more important than ever in the current economic crisis.
in writing. the european parliament has defended the eu's food aid programme for the next two years. as a result, hungary can spend more than huf 4 billion of the eur 500 million allocated to aid to help those in need. last year alone, thanks to the union, more than 3500 tonnes of food were distributed to the most deprived hungarians. this is particularly important in my country, where nearly 100,000 people are hungry and another million people are at risk of quality hunger. in the midst of the economic crisis and austerity measures, 18 million citizens in 20 eu member states are expected to need eu aid in 2012. italy, the largest recipient of food aid, receives almost €100 million from community funds. the debate should not therefore be about whether food aid is given in the context of social or agricultural policy! the cap has always included a number of social elements. it is our common responsibility, in the spirit of european solidarity, to ensure that those in need have access to food in times of scarcity. the food programme, however, reinforces the eu's bottom-line view. i therefore urge those member states who oppose the food programme and the european commission not to put obstacles in the way of the eu food fund in the future. i and the european parliament are determined to continue the aid programme to help tackle social inequalities beyond 2014.
the following point is inserted after point a7-0058/2012 of the report of ms iliana ivanova, on behalf of the committee on budgetary control, on the proposal for the 2010 annual report of the european investment bank (eib) (2011/2186 ((ini))
in writing. the development of a comprehensive global control system is essential to ensure the detailed control of complex financial instruments that are constantly being developed by the eib and its partners. the report welcomes the bank's call for greater transparency and accountability in its more risky programmes. one of my amendments therefore provides that the eib should in any event be subject to prudential regulatory supervision to ensure that it maintains its aaa rating and to monitor the quality of the eib's financial position, the accurate measurement of its results and the observance of the rules of professional conduct. i also fully support the eib in its efforts to provide more support to smes, as they are the engine of the european economy. this report also highlights the eif's leading role in supporting the european union economy, and in particular smes. it highlights the weakness of the sme securitisation market and encourages the eif to continue its efforts to support the recovery. i certainly regard these proposals as a significant step forward. i believe that, in addition to all this, the eib should continue to support the europe 2020 strategy by multiplying the structural funds and by further using innovative financial instruments. it is undeniable, then, that this work has resulted in a balanced report. i congratulate the speaker and thank him for his thorough work.
in writing. in the current economic climate and in order to implement the europe 2020 strategy, the european investment bank is given a special role in the life of the union. as the strategy's core objective is to achieve economic growth - e.g. through job creation, sustainable development, innovation, etc. - projects financed by the eib, with a particular focus on small and medium-sized enterprises, which are the engine of the economy, are important elements. however, in order to operate with the necessary effectiveness and efficiency, thereby contributing to the eu's strategic objectives, due attention must be paid to performance measurement. this requires, on the one hand, that both the result, performance and countervailing indicators fit well into the strategy, covering the key strategic objectives. on the other hand, measures should be taken to measure the measurable indicators in a way that is clearly understandable to all. a proper, well-functioning and clear performance evaluation system increases transparency and accountability, which is essential. what can the commission do to ensure that the eib applies the well-functioning performance measurement system of the european court of auditors? is there a way for the court to develop a new performance measurement system in cooperation with the eib? what is the commission's view on the cost of measuring performance indicators and whether this is necessary?
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on constitutional affairs. − mr president, regrettably many problems still persist as regards the implementation of european citizens’ rights, including the right to move and reside freely within the eu and electoral rights. one of the greatest obstacles to exercising active union citizenship is the lack of awareness. we must intensify our efforts to provide useful and accessible information about eu citizenship rights. 2013 will be devoted to citizenship and we should maximise our efforts politically and financially to make the best use of it. as you know, the set of rights attached to eu citizenship is now complemented by a new tool, the european citizens’ initiative, which comes into force on 1 april and is very important. in that regard i would like to emphasise that the vălean report is not the place to determine which committee should be responsible for the hearings after successful ecis. this matter is being discussed at present in the committee concerned and will be submitted for discussion to parliament in due course. this is not only disregarding the distribution of competences between committees but is also a blatant attempt to bypass an open debate, which is not something this house should stand for.
in writing. − today we voted on a report that will have a particularly profound impact on the more than 800 million air passengers who travel through the airspace of the european union every year. this is a significant step forward, given that the relevant regulations have changed little in recent years and the level of aviation was lower at the time of their adoption. the report welcomes the need for greater legal certainty, clarity of interpretation and uniform application of the regulations across the union. this is also timely as the commission plans to review regulation 261/2004 next year. one of the most important steps is to publish information on passengers' rights in a clear, appropriate manner and in plain language at all key stages of the journey, starting from the moment when the passenger intends to book a ticket. it should be noted that the report also contributes to the future revision of other legislation, such as the package travel directive and the airport package.
in writing. − better and stricter regulation of derivatives markets should take account of the specific situation of firms which are required to continue hedging their financial and operational risks on favourable terms and in a tailor-made way with derivatives. as the commission has previously pointed out, otc derivatives in particular contribute to the financial turmoil by allowing leverage to increase and leading to an increasing dependency between market participants. this is why i support the guiding principle of this report, so that in the future, future legislative proposals on derivatives transactions follow a functional approach. the need for european regulation of derivatives transactions should therefore be emphasised. we therefore call on the commission to coordinate the process with europe's partners as much as possible in order to achieve uniform and internationally harmonised regulation. this should, of course, be done in a way that avoids regulatory arbitrage due to inadequate coordination. in closing, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and thank him for his thorough work and his openness. i think this work has resulted in a great report.
in writing. − i voted in favour of clarifying and improving the legal framework for administrative cooperation in the field of excise duties, as i believe that the commitment of member states to exchange information is not enough, as without well-constructed, automated and standardised it systems, data is exposed to risk and loss. this legislation and the emcs (electronic monitoring system) it contains also contribute significantly to the achievement of the economic objectives of a member state, as this harmonised reporting system allows for the comparison and full analysis of revenue data on the basis of a uniform methodology. it is also important to note that the report mentions that the exchange of information may take place in any language, which, in addition to facilitating the administrative obligations of each member state, certainly contributes to diversity and equality between member states. i myself have contributed to the creation of a data reporting system that does not increase the bureaucratic burden on businesses and member states, but rather promotes transparency and the smoother functioning of the internal market in accordance with eu data protection rules.
in writing. − hungary, as a committed partner of serbia, continues to support the country's european integration. i believe that the balkans and the region are of crucial importance for the european union and especially for hungary. of the former yugoslav states, slovenia is already a member of the eu, croatia has already decided to join, and macedonia and montenegro are also official candidates. hungary has worked on the european integration of the western balkans throughout its presidency of the european council and its commitment to this has not changed since then. that is why we considered it important to support serbia's candidate status. however, it is important to stress that the country must meet all the requirements imposed by the union, since the transposition of european law is essential to solve long-standing problems. for historical reasons, hungary has many ties to serbia, especially to vojvodina in its north. it is in the interest of our neighbourhood policy, our national policy and the hungarians of today that the country should join the community of the european union countries as soon as possible. i welcome the agreement reached between the serbian government and the representatives of the hungarian federation of vojvodina (vmsz), which allows the implementation of the law on asset redistribution and compensation. the aim remains to establish a fully clear legal situation which excludes any form of collective liability. it is welcome that belgrade is making great efforts to settle the serbian-kosovo relationship and that both sides are seeking a peaceful solution. these measures will give a major boost to the country's accession process. we encourage the serbian government to continue on the path it has started and to make every effort to meet the political and economic criteria for accession to the eu.
in writing. − the real stake of serbia's eu accession is to measure and properly exploit regional impacts. the regional approach is also justified by the fact that the union promised membership to all the western balkan states in 2003. however, most of them are not in a condition to begin fulfilling that promise. the diversity of levels of development is also a well-known phenomenon in serbia. added to this problem is the multifaceted political process of internal consolidation of the balkan countries, which perpetuates and even increases the inherited differences. in my view, the hungarians of the past have already proven on many occasions that they are committed supporters of eu accession. i am glad that serbia has been granted candidate status, but it is a bit bitter because it has been too long in the making. sadly, in recent months, the number of serbian supporters of eu membership has fallen below 50 percent, and the news from individual eu countries has contributed to the prevailing apathy. it should be noted that the hungarian community in the past was also keenly aware of what was happening around hungary. this also significantly exceeded the long-awaited eu accession. in the internal political dilemmas in hungary, the majority of the former hungarian community has been on the side of the government from the beginning. i hope that these problems will soon be solved, thus restoring the union's credibility and increasing its acceptance.
in writing. − hungary remains committed to supporting serbia's accession to the eu. i welcome the significant progress made in meeting the copenhagen criteria, which were essential for serbia to achieve eu candidate status. however, i would stress that these issues must be taken into account in the interim, not just for the sake of accession. it is in our unquestionable national interest to have a high level of recognition and guarantee of the collective rights of the hungarian community of vojvodina living in serbia. serbia should make further significant efforts to establish quality education in minority languages at the state and provincial level, which is necessary to preserve ethnic and cultural identity. in addition, the serbian authorities should provide the necessary financial resources for the exercise of cultural, educational and linguistic rights related to the law on national councils. the minority policy in serbia should be monitored continuously. it is reassuring, however, that despite the initial difficulties, the adopted rehabilitation law resolves the issues of collective guilt and that the law places greater emphasis on individual responsibility. in my view, serbia's further progress in the european integration process depends to a large extent on the protection of minorities, the maintenance of good neighbourly relations and regional cooperation.
in writing. − hungary consistently supports the european integration of the western balkans region, including kosovo. i would like to emphasize that hungary is seeking to deepen relations with kosovo in all areas. i believe that the number of people who recognize kosovo will grow dynamically. i also consider the dialogue between serbia and kosovo to be very important, as both countries are pursuing euro-atlantic integration. it is very important that no balkan country should be left behind in the western balkan process of european integration. acquiring eu membership is vital for kosovo as it is the only way to settle post-conflict relations. i am convinced that serbia and kosovo will conclude new agreements on, among other things, the free movement of persons, economic cooperation and the establishment of common customs offices for border crossers. this will of course require the establishment of new diplomatic relations and the conclusion of intergovernmental agreements. it is also important to emphasise that the country is home to many nationalities, and i welcome the fact that kosovo respects the rights of national minorities in terms of language and other rights.
in writing. − the balkans region is a key area for the european union, both economically and geopolitically. montenegro has made a number of efforts and struggled with determination to become a member of the european union in recent years. the country must be an integral part of the enlargement process and we must give it all the support we can. the country is progressing well towards meeting the copenhagen political criteria and has achieved satisfactory results overall. however, in order to make further progress, efforts to strengthen the rule of law, in particular with regard to high-level corruption and organised crime, should be maintained. i support the accession of montenegro.

thank you for your time, mr. president. a lot has been said in the debate, and i don't necessarily want to repeat it. first of all, i would like to thank my colleague, mr milan cabrnoch, for his excellent work and i would also like to point out that we members of parliament often receive questions from citizens about what we are doing here in parliament. if there is a good demonstration that the commission and parliament can do something useful together, then this apparently technical problem we are discussing is very good evidence that by meaningful, useful cooperation we are able to help our citizens who are free to move, free to enterprise in an enlarged europe, to retain the rights they have acquired, to enjoy the benefits of those rights. the particular merit of this debate, this consultation, this joint work is that it is based on the proposals of the commission's committee of experts, that it takes account of the experience of the member states in this respect and that parliament, its members, have also tried to find a solution to these apparently technical problems in the first reading. it's like it's been said before. new forms of enterprise, new forms of contract, new and more mobile work opportunities have emerged in the union. we struggle in many areas to create legal frameworks, legal rules to regulate these, to deal with them clearly. i think we have a really good example here that if we want to, we can and we can find good solutions. i find the new rules on self-employed airline staff particularly positive. i personally think that the formulation of the base space creates a new and creative opportunity. once again, i thank all of you in the commission, i thank my colleague milan for his work, and i hope it will be really useful. it is a matter of coordination, which we shall be discussing many times, and which must be brought up again and again, year after year.
i'd like to ask officer andor a question. in particular, today there are one million posted workers, which is less than 1% of the european workforce, or 0.7% to be precise. how do you see the commissioner as attributing the fact that the proportion of posted workers in europe is so low to the shortcomings of eu legislation, the previous directive and the previous regulation? has the commission carried out an impact assessment? if the amendment to the directive and the monti ii regulation were adopted, how much would this create new jobs, how much would it help economic growth, which is the biggest problem in europe today, that there is not enough economic growth. in this respect, what is the outcome of the commission's impact assessment?
thank you for your answer, officer. when we talk about social dumping, i often feel that there is a closure on the part of the old member states, and it would be very good if the debate on the polish plumber were not re-launched, if the debate on the old and new member states were not re-launched, because if i look at the proportion of workers sent abroad, as i said in the previous paragraphs, i think that social dumping is exaggerated here. how can we avoid another fault line between the new member states and the old member states after 2004?
the european commission's disability strategy, published at the end of 2010, includes a figure for employment, namely 50%. that number is 50% employment level. we're talking about central eastern europe, so half of that number is valid, so the employment rate is very low. unfortunately, there is no figure that can show whether disabled workers can take up employment in other eu member states. i don't think i'd be surprised to hear that they're taking jobs in very small numbers. why would i? because access and social services are not uniform across member states. my question is what does the european commission intend to do to achieve this goal? in particular, the commission intends to present this year the so-called european barriers package.
thank you for your answer. my second supplementary question relates to the european social fund. in recent years, about 4% of the basic resource has been devoted to improving the situation of people with disabilities. meanwhile, about 10% of europe's citizens live with a disability. so the fair argument is that at least 10% of the fund, 10% of the resources should be devoted to people with disabilities. is this the path the commission intends to take?
in writing. - i am voting in favour of the adoption of the irish protocol, which explains the treaty of lisbon in the light of the concerns of the irish people. the document states that the treaty of lisbon does not affect in any way the scope and applicability of the protection of the right to life, the right to family and the right to education provided for in the constitution of ireland, since, under the treaty on the functioning of the european union, those areas do not fall within the competence of the union. the european parliament has welcomed the fact that, by adopting the protocol, it has finally made clear that the regulation of abortion in the eu is a matter for the member states.
in writing. − in the european union, the outermost regions are a special autonomous group which should be given greater attention, not only because of their remoteness, but also because their integration into their own geographical environment should be in the common interest of the european community as a whole. i regret to note that the commission is planning withdrawals in its proposal for the multiannual financial framework for the period 2014-2020, which also concerns the additional resources for the outermost regions. however, i believe that the flexibility of cohesion policy instruments should be increased to enable the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy to be achieved, even in specific geographical and demographic circumstances, and that cohesion policy should aim at creating a sustainable, inclusive growth-oriented, territorial cohesion-based economy. cohesion policy must remain one of the main tools of european action and therefore play an important role in reducing inequalities in europe's regions, and in particular in the outermost regions. i am pleased to note that the commission intends to allocate a separate budget line for 'outermost regions and very sparsely populated regions' in the multiannual financial framework for the period 2014-2020, as this will create a clearer link between the support provided for these regions and their objectives. it is important to emphasise the need for territorial cooperation in the outermost regions and the continuation of related projects of common interest and, in this context, the better use of available aid.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report, as i consider it essential that the outermost regions of the union receive the same priority as the member states which form the core of the union. each eu policy, and cohesion policy in particular, can only succeed if it is applied consistently, in solidarity, to all equally, with the support of the little ones and the needy. just as the new member states needed help when they joined, and in many cases some of their regions still need support from the common funds, so too the different development opportunities for the outermost regions must be supported, be it in any element of the europe 2020 strategy or in the context of a more complete community coherence with this programme. the fact that these external regions are to be given a separate heading in the new budgetary cycle is an important step forward, and it is also right to bear in mind that they play a particularly important role in protecting the union's borders, in fostering economic and cultural links with third countries and in ensuring the territorial continuity of the single market.
in writing. − i would like to welcome the fact that the annual report on human rights in the world and the european union’s policy on the matter dedicates a full chapter to the human rights situation of women, taking a firm standpoint in condemning the gravest abuses of women’s fundamental rights, such as sexual violence. in this regard, the eu plan of action on gender equality and women’s empowerment in development for the period 2010-2015 and the commission’s women’s charter promoting gender equality at international level are very important steps. the council of europe’s convention on preventing and combating violence against women and domestic violence must also be promptly signed and ratified.

the following point is attached to the council and commission statements: call for concrete action to combat tax fraud and tax evasion [2012/2599 ((rsp) ].
the issue is extremely timely, as the prevention of tax evasion and the revenue generated from it is also extremely necessary. the crisis has confirmed this for us, and also that we must not only set requirements, but also continuously assess and monitor their fulfilment. if we do not do this, not only will we lose revenue, but also the confidence and the hope of european citizens and businesses in the future. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive on taxation of savings to include the taxation of savings deposits. in addition, it is important to me that tax avoidance from hybrid financial instruments is also mentioned. as the member responsible for the royalty and interest payments, i will also be dealing with this matter in particular.

in writing. our decisions on energy taxation today will seriously determine europe's competitiveness for decades to come. fundamental issues such as energy taxation must be addressed from all angles, including environmental, energy, industrial and even social policy. it is also essential that the tax reform leads to a tax policy that is cleaner, more transparent and free from over-administration than at present. a tax structure that promotes energy efficiency and low carbon emissions can not only strengthen the union budget but also help to finance the green energy development and research projects of the member states in the future. the same amount could also be used to support the efforts to make european nuclear energy safer. in addition, the integrated tax system can help in such endeavours as educating energy users, measuring and improving the efficiency of green energy and the necessary alternative energy sources, and not least supporting green small businesses. as a socialist representative, my priority is to ensure that the people of europe have access to affordable and safe energy. while we need sustainable development, renewable energy, efficient energy taxation and adequate energy security, we cannot forget the burden on the population for a moment.
blue-card question. – mr president, just a point of clarification. i agreed with you almost completely, especially taking into consideration the large-scale and serious violations of human rights committed by the government we are talking about, namely the us government. i just want to seek clarification on the issue of proportionality as opposed to purpose, because i think that for many people – especially those who are not lawyers – it might be disturbing to think that giving up some of our privacy and data protection rights might be proportionate to the noble aim of preventing terrorism. i think this is something we all agree upon. but the question here is: can this noble purpose really be achieved by the measures taken here? that is where i have a problem and that is why i am going to vote no today. i want to ask your opinion on the issue of proportionality as opposed to purpose. i want to ask you for a clarification on that.
blue-card question. – mr president, with all due respect, may i ask if, under normal circumstances, you would agree that a government – whether our own or another – has absolutely no business knowing where we travel, with whom, how often we do so, how much we spend on airline tickets, or whether we have a stoma, for example, or any other health issues? of course the government might have a justified interest in collecting data about this if they can protect lives with this information, but how exactly can they protect us from terrorism by collecting this data and restricting our privacy?
mr president the european parliament shall have the right of unanimity with regard to the convention on the exchange of air passenger data. that's how it was with the swift agreement, where we voted no. not only because of the problems of content, but also because we were not given the right information and the conditions set by the european parliament were not taken into account in the negotiations. partly as a consequence of the parliamentary 'no' at the time, the negotiations were now more transparent, there was a dialogue between the libe committee and the european commission. some of our expectations, as set out at the beginning of the process, have been incorporated into the draft agreement. nevertheless, the agreement is not satisfactory in several respects. i think, however, that the european parliament should approve the agreement. if we don't accept it, then the data will be passed on to the united states in the same way, without any kind of uniform guarantee for european citizens to have their data tracked, to have their data reviewed. it's what's happened before. bilateral agreements would govern data protection, with individual member states negotiating with even less lobbying power than the european commission now has. i do not think the draft agreement is good, but i am in favour of adopting it because it gives the european parliament the opportunity, through joint review and monitoring, to be informed about what is happening in the implementation of the agreement and, if necessary, to demand its suspension and renegotiation. passengers can track who has their data and what they do with it. it should provide legal certainty to european airlines. i urge the european commission to closely monitor the implementation of the agreement, the use of passenger data, the handling of sensitive data and to report back to parliament. if the implementation of the agreement is likely to lead to a breach of the protection of european citizens' data in practice, suspend and renegotiate the agreement.
my colleagues! how far will europe go? where is the limit in tolerating the united states to force europe to abandon its human rights standards by invoking the threat of terrorism? it started with the united states taking measures on its own soil that had no causal connection with the prevention of terrorism, because to keep people in prison for ten years without the right to defend themselves, tortured, in cruel conditions, without a trial, without a fair trial in the european sense, is unacceptable to us. yet we accepted that secret prisons were built on european soil and that this behavior was carried on there. now, this may seem like a small thing to talk about today, but i still think we have to draw the line somewhere.
in writing. the fight against terrorism and organised crime can hardly be effective without the tracking and processing of banking and passenger data. it is welcome that agreements have been reached between the eu and its allies on both tftp and pnr, which address the majority of the european parliament's concerns on privacy and data protection by incorporating guarantees for the prevention and remediation of possible violations into the agreements. i have repeatedly stressed the need for reciprocity in the us-eu relationship. in other words, the union should not only give but also receive information from the us as an equal partner in the prosecution of crime. it is questionable whether the eu needs its own pnr. a pnr system of its own would not only make the fight against terrorism more effective, but would also help to make reciprocity between the union and its allies a reality. it would be good if the commission finally made its vision of the future of the eu pnr clear. it would be beneficial for the union, the member states and the aviation industry to have the union pnr as a single european framework for processing data, ensuring equal access to information for member states. of course, and this convention provides a hopeful basis for this, it is essential that the guarantees of personality rights and data protection are also taken into account in the design of a possible eu pnr system.
in writing. − the prolonged economic and financial crisis is challenging the macroeconomic stability of some countries. the budgetary and debt crisis in the member states concerned has created exceptional situations that prevent them from implementing important projects under cohesion and structural policies. the banking sector is not very willing to provide financing, and private investors are not available. the severely affected countries are unable to generate growth and in some countries, investment has stopped due to a lack of private co-financing. the private sector must be involved in the implementation of infrastructure projects. europe needs growth, which can only be achieved if we ensure that the regions of the european union absorb the regional development fund and the cohesion fund as a risk-sharing instrument. synergies between the funds need to be strengthened, as this is the only way to achieve an efficient use. the rules on co-financing and public funding are extremely rigid. the increase in the co-financing rate to 95% is a help, but it is still not enough. the framework for the implementation of cohesion policies needs to be re-thought and, in cases where crisis-related problems arise, these risk-sharing options should be used. this legislative proposal will help not only greece but also many member states affected by the crisis. this risk-sharing instrument will allow member states to implement projects quickly and efficiently and to continue the implementation of projects already started. risk-sharing also means that the european commission and the european investment bank will be able to act together to ensure that private sector investment is also more effectively involved in the implementation of projects.
in writing. − the european union is currently facing a prolonged economic and financial crisis, which is affecting not only the macroeconomic stability of many member states but also access to finance across the union. this also threatens the implementation of cohesion policy programmes, as the liquidity problems of financial institutions limit the amounts of funding available to public and private actors implementing the projects concerned. i agree with the rapporteur that the liquidity shortage experienced by the financial sector is not limited to countries that have received financial assistance from the european financial stabilisation mechanism or the balance of payments mechanism. this is why managers of infrastructure and productive investments in all member states consider the shortage of funding to be one of the main problems. in my view, the possibility of extending the risk-sharing mechanisms to all member states will increase the volume of investment in growth and jobs across the union by using resources from the structural and cohesion funds that would otherwise not be used by the end of the current programming period. i have voted in favour of the report because i consider it important.
in writing. − the adopted report aims to introduce a voluntary common consolidated corporate tax base (ccctb) at eu level by harmonising the calculation and proportional distribution of the tax base, but without affecting the tax rate or national rules on reporting. its use can significantly increase the competitiveness of the european economy, transparency, tax fraud, tax avoidance and the reduction of double taxation, but i could not support the report with my vote because hungary also applies tax base adjustment items to enforce economic policy preference. thus, full harmonisation of the calculation of the corporate tax base is not in line with the current tax policy of the government and would have a negative impact on tax revenues, reduce gdp and undermine the competitiveness of the domestic corporate tax system.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report because it concerns a thorough review of the eu's rules on the taxation of energy products and encourages the use of renewable energy sources and lower co2 emissions, while addressing the imbalances that currently exist. the europe 2020 strategy and our energy and climate objectives require fair and transparent energy taxation to reduce our dependence on fossil fuels. this proposal provides a strong carbon price signal for businesses and consumers, and also provides an opportunity to shift the tax burden from labour to consumption, thereby favouring growth-friendly taxation. moreover, since tax minimums are uniform across the union, the current amendment to the directive will not create additional costs for operators or reduce competitiveness.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, minister, commissioner, i would like to thank you for your kind words. my fellow members of parliament! i welcome the position expressed by the council and the commission, which shows that the eu's accession to the convention on human rights and the text of the treaty on human rights are moving in the direction of providing added value for european citizens, as requested by my report to the committee on the interior and justice before the start of the negotiations, and as many of the elements of that report are reflected in the treaty. i should like to thank the commission in particular for keeping the members regularly informed of the state of the negotiations and for giving us the opportunity to express our views and make comments. the added value and effective enforcement aspects should also be taken into account when making the necessary compromises. at the same time, i do not recommend that european citizens have excessive expectations. it is very important to have precise information about what this accession will be about, because if not, then trust in the eu institutions will also begin. in seeking a compromise on the accession treaty, we must not forget the other side of the coin, the need for the strasbourg court reform, which was stressed from the outset. i hope that the brighton conference which began yesterday will provide answers to the problematic questions that have arisen in relation to this reform, which can be reassuring to all parties. accession can only be successful if it genuinely strengthens the european institutions already in place and the judicial systems of the member states, including all the existing instruments to promote the rights of citizens, not only in principle but also in practice, and this treaty is one such instrument.
madam president, please. the question arose as to what would be the added value of the union's accession to the convention. i would like to make it clear that this represents a new remedy for european citizens, who can also bring their complaints to the strasbourg court in the event of infringements of the union's fundamental rights, so there is added value. they could turn around if some member states did not block this connection. in this debate, we heard about which countries, primarily which countries. this is as much a cause for concern as some of the proposals made at the recent brighton conference on the reform of the strasbourg court. such a proposal would not be admissible before the court of human rights under the new admissibility criterion, namely that the member state court has already examined the case. it would seriously undermine the enjoyment of human rights and the right to a fair trial, in particular for citizens of states where the judicial system cannot be considered independent. we want to make sure that the overburden of the human rights court will not be reduced at the expense of the guarantee of human rights. we are all aware of the overload, but the solution is not to be found in the direction that the brighton conference has taken. let's hope that's not the final decision. what we can support is, indeed, what we have also heard here today, to provide adequate resources for the human rights court. the effects of the reforms of recent years must be expected, and the application of the convention on human rights and the quality of legislation in the member states must be improved. european governments must be committed to implementing the decisions of the court of human rights. if all this happens, the court of justice in strasbourg will certainly be less overburdened and will be able to continue to fulfil the role which we consider to be very important.
my fellow citizens! i started my career in the soviet union as a diplomat, where a lot of things were classified, were state secrets. i never thought that the classification of disadvantaged areas could be a secret issue in the european union. my colleagues have made all the important arguments in front of me, and i find this an absurd situation. in my country and in my smaller region, where i come from, the central part of hungary, the danube-tisza region, the sandbox, which the un world food organization, the fao has classified as a semi-desert, the farmers do not know that they will now be a disadvantaged area or not, although without irrigation these farmers will not survive. i therefore fully agree with mr dorfmann, my rapporteur, and all my colleagues that what the european council and the european commission are doing in the absence of the european council is a disgrace.
my fellow citizens! we've revived an old debate. i really like the bacon from south tyrol, but the bacon from hungary is also excellent. i would really like this discussion not to be about closing the trade gate for each other. moreover, this debate is somewhat redundant, since we have already had this debate in the context of quality policy. and let's not forget that the european union is the world's second largest exporter of agricultural products and the world's largest importer of agricultural products. mr dorfmann pointed out that local markets, local trade, local processing have their limits. i support this, because it means jobs in the agricultural sector, it reduces transport costs, but let's not be under any illusions. it would be good if we in this house could finally calculate what the actual environmental footprint of an agricultural product is, because it is not certain that if in hungary, where it is cold, a greenhouse uses a lot of energy, that the ecological footprint of vegetable production will be smaller than if it is imported from another continent, where i even include the transport costs. we can stop economic protectionism, we can help local production, but we can't stop global trade.
in writing. consuming locally produced food has many benefits for both consumers and farmers who produce it. these products are of a more reliable quality for local consumers, as they add the face of the farmer to the product, and the purchase is often based on personal knowledge. they contribute to the viability of the local rural economy by maintaining income. since most of the sales are made directly by the farmer in local markets, this eliminates the small and large food retailers who would otherwise receive a disproportionate share of the profits. and selling locally is good for the environment, because the goods don't travel hundreds, sometimes thousands of miles. this will reduce co2 emissions from the transport of agricultural products. one of fidesz's most important election promises was to support local smallholders, local markets. the government has already achieved a number of significant results in this area. the amendment to the public procurement act requires, for example, that schools, hospitals and other public institutions should give preference to local producers when purchasing food. at the same time, in order to protect local producers, it is necessary to define at european level the conditions and labelling rules for locally produced food.
in writing. in my opinion, it is considered to be a food that is produced entirely locally, to which no artificial preservatives are added, and which reaches the consumer in the same environment with its original natural characteristics. central, state support for the production of locally produced food could create self-sufficient regions that would contribute greatly to employment growth and, in the long run, radically reduce health expenditure in the region due to a healthy consumption of these foods. the precise origin of the finished product and of the raw material, broken down by locality, should be indicated on the packaging of these products.

member of the commission. − mr president, we welcome mr kaczmarek’s report and the report of the court of auditors, both of which show that the exercise of devolution of external assistance is bringing positive results. i believe that, thanks to devolution, eu aid is now being delivered better, closer and faster to the beneficiaries. of course, in order to fully exploit the potential of devolution we need to ensure staffing levels for the future. this is an area in which cooperation with parliament is very much needed. having said that, we are committed to improving what both the court of auditors report and mr kaczmarek’s report identified as the real issues. we should do more to measure the results of our aid in order to move towards a more result-oriented culture. given the restraints of human resources, we need to concentrate these where there is an identified need, and we have identified additional needs in africa and in neighbourhood delegations. as has been mentioned, the question of turnover is a real issue, given that 40% of our employees are contract agents with three-year contracts. increasing this ratio could create a real problem in the future. therefore, in order to mitigate the problem of contract agents and the turnover of agents, we are working to move the contracts of contract agents from the headquarters to delegations once their contracts are over, and we would like to see six-year contracts rather than shorter ones in the future. we call on the rapporteur and parliament to support our efforts to achieve this. the new external action service is very important for the future. we have agreed on working arrangements in order to divide the work. in addition, the delegation heads will have great responsibility: not only will they act as authorising officers, they will also be responsible for the consistency of the service. however – and this is still clear – the commission is, in principle, responsible for the implementation of the budget, and the commission is fully accountable to parliament in this area. to sum up, devolution is a positive development. we are ready to follow the advice and instructions set out in the court of auditors report and in mr kaczmarek’s report in order to make it even better in the future.

nyugati európai országaihoz való felzárkózást az európai felsőoktatási térség keretében. a jelentéstervezethez beérkezett 180 módosító indítvány is azt jelzi, hogy most a kontinensünket sújtó sokrétű válság idején az európai felsőoktatási rendszer megújítása korparancsnak tekinthető. ennek megfelelően a jelentés a felsőoktatási rendszerek modernizációja révén azokra a rendkívüli kihívásokra kíván feleletet adni, melyeket egyebek mellett az általános gazdasági válság, a költségvetési hiányok kényszere, a megnövekedett munkanélküliség vagy a tovább súlyosbodó demográfiai válság támasztanak. a felsőoktatás közös európai érték. magújítása csak az uniós, a tagállami és a regionális szint együttes szerepvállalásával képzelhető el azt is figyelembe véve, hogy a felsőoktatás meghatározó szerepet játszhat a társadalmi befogadás elősegítésében. a jelentés arra hívja fel a felsőfokú oktatási intézményeket, hogy az előbbiekkel, illetve külső partnereivel, a helyi önkormányzatokkal, a civil szervezetekkel és az üzleti szféra szereplőivel összefogva minden társadalmi csoportottal hátrányos helyzetben lévők számára is biztosítsák a felsőoktatási tanulmányi lehetőségeket. ennek összefüggésében az unió, illetve a parlament ezúton is fellép a diszkrimináció bármely formája ellen, és kiáll a kulturális és nyelvi sokszínűség alapvető értékei mellett. ennek megfelelően a jelentés arra ösztönzi a tagországokat, hogy különleges figyelemben és anyagi támogatásban részesítsék a nemzeti és etnikai, nyelvi kisebbségek szolgálatában álló felsőfokú intézményeket. kihagyok egy részt. hadd zárjam ismertetésemet a jövendő letéteményeseivel, az ifjúsággal. közismert, hogy egyes tagországokban, különösképpen a posztkommunista államok társadalmaiban igen magas a fiatalkorú munkanélküliek aránya. reményeink szerint a jelentés, illetve az uniós politika és intézkedések a márciusban elfogadott berlinguer jelentéssel együtt jelentős mértékben hozzájárulnak ahhoz, hogy az európa 2020 stratégia célkitűzéseinek megfelelően európai ifjúságunk méltó munkához és kenyérkeresethez jusson.

in writing. i congratulate my colleague, mr capitales, on his report which gives a complete picture of the state of european higher education. i would like to begin by highlighting the role of sport in education, the importance of which is clearly highlighted in several places in the report. i consider it imperative that the funding of european higher education remains stable in the face of the crisis, both from the member states and from the eu budget. i agree with the rapporteur that further support and development of the european institute of innovation and technology (eit) based in budapest should be ensured. i suggested that the commission develop a strategy to reduce regional disparities between western and central and eastern european higher education institutions. finally, to prevent students from ending their studies in debt and entering the labour market with excessive debt, i propose to review the double-edged effect of student loans.
in writing. i consider the report by mr tőke to be very important. we all know that it is in the interest of europe as a whole to acquire knowledge of a high quality, which is really useful and usable for present and future generations. today's higher education institutions must provide competitive knowledge, and they must do so while keeping abreast of the real needs of the labour market. in this context, i would like to highlight the issue of higher education in the mother tongue for national minorities. the motto of the european union is 'unity in diversity'. in order to preserve cultural and linguistic diversity, it is therefore necessary that these national minority communities be able to preserve their identity, represent their interests and defend their rights. but to do this, national minorities, like majority societies, need a well-educated leadership, an elite. and for such a reliable, competitive knowledge-based elite to represent the interests of communities, it is essential that higher education be of high quality, and not least, in the mother tongue, and that it be supported and provided by the majority society. majority societies should therefore pay particular attention to and support traditional higher education institutions of national, ethnic or linguistic minorities, especially those of endangered cultures and languages.
in writing. – i would like to warmly congratulate my colleague lászló tőkés for his report on modernising europe’s higher education systems, which rightly calls on member states and higher education institutions to widen access for students from all social backgrounds, since higher education has the potential to promote social inclusion and upward social mobility, benefiting disadvantaged communities in a complex way, with its positive socio-economic impacts reaching far beyond the individual concerned. the attendance of disadvantaged young people and especially of roma in higher education institutions is astoundingly low. aid mechanisms, such as scholarships and mentoring support, should be established therefore for young roma, to inspire them not only to obtain diplomas but also to enrol in higher education establishments. a system of perks should also be considered, which would increase the participation of roma youth in such institutions and improve their qualifications. it is also very important to keep track of the employment outcomes of graduates and to measure how higher education responds to labour market demands. the commission and member states therefore need to collect and publish statistical data regarding the correlation between different higher education degrees and employment opportunities.

in writing. with the rapid development of information technology, we are increasingly handling cases online or using our mobile phones. it is encouraging that in recent times several member states have taken advantage of the opportunities offered by technological innovation in the field of government services. it has become increasingly clear to decision-makers that the use of egovernment services is a major step forward both for the quality of life of eu citizens and for the efficient functioning of public administrations in the member states. in recent years, electronic administration has become increasingly widespread in hungary. the hungarian example has also proved one of the main messages of the report: citizens save time and money by using flexible and fast online administration. online customer gateways allow us to handle our business from home or the office, sparing us hours of paperwork and cluttered forms. the introduction of egovernment is supported by the business community as well as citizens, as it significantly reduces administrative burdens and thus improves the productivity of businesses. in addition, the public sector is the largest beneficiary of the spread of electronic administration. the electronic control system increases the efficiency of data management and reduces operational costs and errors. however, despite good examples in the member states and initial successes, we cannot afford to fall behind, as the development of information technology opens up new opportunities for improving efficiency.
the first item on the agenda is the report by ms nicole kiil-nielsen, drawn up on behalf of the committee on women's rights and equal opportunities, on the proposal for a directive on women and climate change (2011/2197 (ini)) (a7-0049/2012).
in writing. pollinators play a key role in the conservation of biodiversity, primarily wild and domesticated bees, which are responsible for pollinating 80% of european flowering plant species. as i pointed out in my report on 'health of bees and the future of the beekeeping sector', adopted last year, there are a number of reasons for the decline in honeybees over the years. the lack of veterinary medicinal products, the overuse of certain pesticides and their active substances, in particular neonicotinoids, certain intensive farming methods and the adverse effects of climate change all play a role. the main problem, however, is that there are insufficient eu funds for scientific research and data collection in the field of beekeeping at european level. our knowledge of european bee populations is limited and it is extremely difficult to generate scientific results on the basis of which responsible decisions can be made. i therefore welcome the fact that the report, in line with the previous report i have mentioned, calls on the european commission to significantly increase support for the beekeeping sector. bee health is in the interest of european farmers and environmentalists, and serves the interests of both the food industry and the environment. bees produce public goods through pollination and it is therefore appropriate that the new common agricultural policy, which will enter into force in 2014, should provide more support to the sector than before.

in writing. − i find the report on the modernisation of european higher education systems very welcome, as it tackles one of the most serious issues facing europe in a turbulent time - the disruption of higher education systems - which is also closely linked to the issue of youth unemployment and thus one of the biggest challenges facing europe. the report is good, because it rightly recognises the need to modernise higher education in today's changing times. on the other hand, the report rightly points out that it is not only the teaching methods, the learning structures and the training itself that are decisive and need to be reformed, but also the question of the financial support of the whole system, which cannot be treated in isolation and which fundamentally affects the quality of european education and training, that is, the supply of the european labour market. in the context of europe 2020, the key view on european higher education is that a system should be developed that does not place an additional burden on those who wish to attend higher education, but that functions as a system that rationalises its resources and uses them in response to real needs. universities need even more financial support from the european union to make their requirements more uniform, with due regard for student mobility and the mutual recognition of diplomas.

in writing. we have discussed the future of europe many times within these walls. the problem is not that we are putting this very complex issue on the agenda again and again, but that, as the french and greek elections show, we are unable to provide a perspective for european citizens, especially young people. it is particularly sad today, when we celebrate europe. but what are we celebrating when we often fail to defend european achievements with enough resolve, when we fail to agree on the values we once thought we shared, and when we fail to stand up for each other when we need to be truly united? "a good friend is known by his bad name", so the saying goes. unfortunately, the crisis has shown that we are not on the right track, because there are those who are given special treatment and others who are left behind. we cannot commit ourselves to the principles of more jobs, more economic growth and lifelong learning if one in five young europeans is unemployed and feels hopeless. it seems that we have created a fiscal compact for nothing, if there is a member state that has not signed it and there is one that would renegotiate the hard-won agreement. it is heartbreaking to have to go through all this 62 years after the eu was founded, but perhaps it is not too late to recognise that europe needs a complete change of direction.
in writing. the greek and french election results indicate that a significant proportion of european citizens do not feel that the european union is theirs. we need to hear them, because they also say that the eu is not doing enough to address the issues at hand. thus, the fact that almost one in five young europeans is unemployed, while youth unemployment rates in several countries reach 50 percent, is understated. this question should dominate this week's agenda. little is said about the fact that extremists are taking advantage of the disillusionment of young people to occupy key positions across europe. radical movements are listing eastern european citizens, double standards and insults to other countries are commonplace. there is less talk than there should be about unsustainable demographic trends. that europe has long been producing fewer children than it needs to sustain itself. and this leads to serious social tensions, supply chain disruptions. to get through this, we need to get back to basics. to the ones who created the union. first of all, we must strengthen the communities in accordance with the principles of christian democracy. second, we need to address the double standards and take action against extremists. and third, we need to restore the dignity of work. we need to remove the regulatory, legal and financial barriers that stand in the way of greater employment. anything else is symptomatic at best.
in writing. the past period has not been the golden age of europe. the negative effects of the global crisis are still being felt today. analysts are also in a difficult position: the depth of the recession is unprecedented in the history of modern society. the crisis has brought into sharper focus the contrast between the eurosceptic groups supporting confederation and those opposed to deeper integration. as regards greece, there is a risk that local elections will end with negative results, ignoring the ideals of europe. if the incoming greek government coalition does not support the tough government measures, it is questionable whether greece will receive the next tranche of the eu bailout, and thus could put the eurozone in even greater danger. the challenge for greece, as for europe as a whole, is the rise of eurosceptic nationalist parties, which are feared by young people and previous generations alike. europe must find a way out of the demographic crisis, not just the economic one. the previous social model is unsustainable: with an ageing society, young people see no future. eastern european aspirants in the west, westerners on other continents, in america, or in new zealand, imagine their future. i cannot stress enough the crucial importance for the future of europe of strengthening research and development, innovation and european industrial policy, demonstrating best practice and encouraging investment. we can't even consider five million young people out of work. for the sake of future generations, it is time to act!
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, the report claims that the arab spring is the greatest political transformation in the european neighbourhood since the fall of the berlin wall. this reminds me of zhou enlai, the famous former chinese prime minister, when he was asked for his assessment of the french revolution. he is said to have answered: ‘it is too early to tell’. democracy may have taken root in tunisia; there is more openness in morocco; there are encouraging signs in jordan. but what about bahrain? what about yemen? what about egypt? what about libya? it would be better to use slightly more cautious language. the report also claims that trade is an effective means of consolidating democracy and promoting stability. well, trade in itself is not an effective means of promoting democracy. it is important – no question about it, and we promote free trade – but trade alone can help dictatorships as well. trade does not necessarily lead to democracy. we need trade to be supplemented with institutions, the rule of law, constitutionality, equal opportunities and support for small and medium-sized enterprises in order to channel the fruits of trade to a wider segment of society. for democracy to take root there is a need for a middle class, which in turn can only flourish on broad-based economic growth – something which trade alone cannot guarantee. the report also calls for the reconsideration of odious debt. i am only familiar with big debt and small debt, but not odious debt. i remember very well when the berlin wall fell. no western state, no western country wanted to forgive communist debt, no matter how odious it must have been, so why do you not call on the italian state to take over all the debt? if mario monti were to agree to that, then your claim would be credible.
one of the major achievements of the union's wine reform is the single rule of origin. the question of quality european wines, however, cannot be a subject of dispute between states. several crossborder european wine regions have successfully resolved issues related to the winemaking process and protection of origin, such as the moselle wine region in the french-german-luxembourg area. this could be an example to follow in resolving the dispute over the use of the tokaji wine name and the method of production as soon as possible. this would serve the interests of both hungarian and slovak quality winemakers. unfortunately, the current situation is a dispute between the two countries. the socialist hungarian government has submitted a case to the european court of justice, but it would be very good if the slovak government, the hungarian government and the brussels commission agreed to protect the reputation of the king's wine, the king of wines.
