the committee on employment and social affairs has adopted an opinion. on 13 february 2012, we adopted the discharge for the financial year 2010 at an extraordinary meeting of the european parliament's social affairs committee. in the area of our competence, this means four european agencies, namely eurofund, cedefop, the agency for safety and health at work and the agency for the improvement of working conditions. we are pleased to note that the annual accounts reflect the actual financial position as at 31 december and that the procedures and cash flows for the year in question are in line with the agency's financial rules. our opinion, by the way, is in line with the court's. the same could be said for the transactions underlying the financial statements. at the same time, we stressed the need for closer cooperation in the design of future programmes and welcomed the commission's intention to monitor more closely the annual accounts of the member states, for example with regard to the european social fund, paying particular attention to the added value of european funds, and with regard to the globalisation fund, to look at the fact that only one third of the available funds have been called up. there is a lot of debate about this fund, but overall i have to say that we are satisfied, and this is the first year that we don't have to raise a serious objection about the management of the agencies.
the rapporteur is the committee on regional development. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, first, let me make a comment. today, the 43rd plenary session of the european parliament is debating the report on the final accounts for about 170 minutes, which is no less than the fact that we can spend just under 4 minutes per report on the use of the money of the european taxpayers. to put it in a slightly different way, in the language of numbers, the european parliament has the fantastic ability to audit every minute about one billion euros of resources used in the budget of the european union, in the budget of various agencies. i think that if we have this fantastic ability, this body should be called a kind of european audit firm. perhaps this is an unworthy time to discuss such a vast matter. as regards cohesion policy, the error rate in the area of cohesion policy has decreased compared to the previous programming period. i think it is important to say this because the question of the error rate is always framed as a sort of checkerboard against cohesion policy after the final tally. in the area of cohesion policy, the error rate is mainly due to disregard for public procurement rules and for payment entitlements. i think that underlines the need for simplification in every way.
on behalf of the s&d group. i would like to give the opinion of the european agencies from the point of view of the european socialists. we are talking about agencies, which are an important part of the european institutional structure. they were set up by governments in agreement with parliament to serve the people of europe by carrying out the tasks assigned to them. so these creations are not based on chance. each agency has been established through a political, policy co-decision process and their operation is sometimes quite complex. therefore, these organisations are too important to be used for power and political games, and proposing to abolish them could have serious political consequences. agencies play an important role and it is therefore essential that they are operated and managed as efficiently as possible in order to safeguard their reputation in the eyes of the citizens of the union. at the same time, the european agencies manage less than half a percent of the eu budget. they are subject to the same close scrutiny as any other union body during the final accounting. we believe that their control should be proportionate to their weight in the budget. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the budgetary authority is able to monitor the implementation of the budget in a way that is proportionate to the budgetary situation. we can't waste half our resources on cases that account for about half a percent of the budget. we had serious concerns this year with the reporter's very critical approach, because it was based on the assumption of guilt until proven guilty. instead, we believe that good faith and natural justice should be the basis until the opposite is proven. i think that our approach has been well justified by the speeches that have been made so far, because the opinions of the committees have been much more positive here in the plenary than the rapporteur's opinion, and i think that parliament as a whole can agree with that. now, a few points that have been consistently raised in the report: about potential conflicts of interest, particularly for agencies where industry or technical expertise is needed, how can we apply the most appropriate procedures and tools to prevent this. we therefore recommend, as the ombudsman has recommended, that special attention be paid to this issue. we strongly supported the court of auditors' special report on this subject and we strongly support the fact that the interinstitutional working party working within the council, the commission and parliament keeps this subject on the agenda. but that's no reason not to accept the 2010 final tally. so i would say that the reporting process is worth considering. other times, it is worthwhile to prepare a report with your colleagues from your own party and with the other parties from the other committees that will be better accepted in parliament.

in writing. − in the european union, the competitiveness of regions is in line with regional economic growth. the commission notes that the airport services agreement concluded between ryanair and ams was signed on 29 june 2010 and entered into force on 1 january 2010. the regional airport is now a 'must-have' investment location, with modern logistics and one of the most efficient means of development. where it doesn't exist or doesn't work properly, you can't expect to find a serious investor today. the commission notes that the airport services agreement concluded between ryanair and ams was signed on the same day. as these air carriers concentrate on lower costs in their operations, they prefer to use smaller airports with adequate infrastructure instead of large, crowded airports. from here, they transfer their passengers to nearby major cities. the method has worked in western europe, and there is already an example of such cooperation in my home town of debrecen. in conclusion, i believe that european regional airports and air travel should be seen as a key factor in creating an efficient and well-functioning eu transport network that stimulates trade and provides mobility for the wider public, which is why i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. − following the presentation of the court of auditors' annual report for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union in respect of the european commission in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the final report draws attention to the fact that in the area of cohesion policy the estimated error rate increased to 7,7% in 2010, which is higher than in 2009. the final report underlines, inter alia, that greater emphasis should be placed on more effective control of financial planning instruments in the future. at the end of march, the committee decided to grant the discharge, to close the accounts for 2010, and i supported the discharge in the vote in committee and in the vote in plenary.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union in the context of the closure procedure for the financial year 2010 concerning the court's special reports on the european commission for the year 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the court calls on the european commission to act more effectively vis-à-vis the member states and to immediately redeploy more officials to control state aid. it encourages the commission to consider whether it can use the lessons learned from the successful handling of the financial crisis to simplify its work in normal circumstances. i support the court's recommendations to further strengthen the commission's action in the framework of its supervisory and coordination powers. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the court of auditors' annual report for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for the european parliament in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the court found that all payments were free from material error during the audits and that no deficiencies were detected during the audit when assessing the compliance of the supervisory and control systems with the financial regulation. the internal audit service is welcomed to meet the highest international standards of internal audit practice. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for the eighth, ninth and tenth european development funds in the context of the closure procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. it is necessary for the european commission to obtain further information on the implementation of the edf at national and regional level and to ensure better visibility of all overseas activities supported by the union. however, it is of concern that in 2010 the supervisory and control systems were again only partially effective in ensuring the disbursement of edf payments, while the final report on the accounts states that the financial audit was conducted in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found in the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the court of auditors' annual report for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union in respect of the council in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. in its annual report for 2010, the court of auditors concluded that, on the basis of its audit work, the payments made by the institutions and bodies in respect of administrative and other expenditure for the financial year ended 31 december 2010 were, overall, free from material error. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. however, the council did not provide parliament and the committee with a number of documents necessary for the closure of the accounts procedure, without which the financial year 2010 cannot be closed. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to postpone the granting of the exemption. i voted in committee and in plenary to postpone the granting of the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union in respect of the european court of justice in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. in its annual report for 2010, the court of auditors did not raise any objections to the management of the court of justice in 2010. on the basis of its audit work, the court of auditors considered that the payments made by the institutions and bodies in respect of administrative and other expenditure for the financial year ended 31 december 2010 were, overall, free from material error, the most likely error rate being, in general, 0,4% in the area of administrative expenditure. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the european court of auditors in the context of the closure of the accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the report concludes that, in order to make the court's work more effective, a larger proportion of officials should be devoted exclusively to audit tasks. the auditors examined in particular whether financial operations are properly recorded and published, whether they are carried out lawfully and regularly, and whether the principles of economy, efficiency and effectiveness apply to these operations. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.

in writing. − following the presentation of the court of auditors' annual report for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the committee of the regions in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. in its 2010 annual report, the court of auditors revealed errors in the recruitment of a permanent staff member and in the procurement of an interpreting service. the committee of the regions provided satisfactory explanations on the issues related to the errors identified and took immediate steps to avoid such errors in the future. it is important that, in order to enhance transparency, a declaration of financial interest of all members of the institution should be made available on the internet and regularly updated. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.

in writing. − i voted in favour of the discharge for the 2010 budget of the european union agencies, as i am convinced that these agencies have been fundamentally frugal and, although many of them have made significant transfers, they have nevertheless made good use of their resources and have expanded their activities in 2010 to promote common policies. the report adopted in committee clearly distinguished between expenditure on financial performance, human resources, internal and external control, which are essential to ensure that the functioning of the union's professional bodies is transparent to all european citizens.

in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the european police college in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the european parliament has approved the closure of the accounts of the european police college for the financial year 2010. although the court found the transactions underlying the academy's annual accounts to be legal and regular, it also highlighted the lack of consistency in the procedure used to approve claims for reimbursement of costs relating to such activities in relation to the costs of organising courses and seminars. in particular, the lack of supporting documents raises questions in certain cases. it is important that the academy address these deficiencies as soon as possible and that similar failures do not occur in the future. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.

in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the european environment agency in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the final report highlights that the main problem with the european environment agency is that several procurement procedural errors occurred in the management of the agency in 2010. the report draws attention to the fact that the agency has recently discovered several conflicts of interest, and the panel did not recommend granting a discharge until these conflicts were resolved. at its meeting at the end of march, the committee decided to postpone the delivery of the final accounts, considering that further clarification of the agency's management in 2010 was required in order to receive the final accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to postpone the granting of the discharge.

in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the european institute for gender equality in the context of the closure of the accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the court of auditors concluded that it had obtained reasonable assurances that the annual accounts of the institute for the financial year 2010 were reliable and that the underlying transactions were legal and regular. the court of auditors confirmed that the annual accounts of the institute for the financial year 2010 are reliable and fair in all material respects and that the institute's financial position as at 31 december 2010 is correct and that its operations and cash flows for the financial year 2010 are in accordance with the financial regulation. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report on the promotion of gender equality, including the budget of the european institute for gender equality. i also voted in favour of not merging the abovementioned institution with the european union agency for fundamental rights, because i believe that, given that we have a separate policy on gender equality and the impact of differences between men and women, this issue cannot be dealt with purely from a budgetary point of view, that is to say, we must also include the functioning of the organisation concerned in a separate budget. only in this way will it be possible to explore the effects of gender equality expenditure and, in particular, to assess whether men and women benefit proportionally from the expenditure. at the same time, i agree that there needs to be a greater emphasis on the representation of control.

in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for artemis in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the discharge report emphasises that the joint undertaking should establish a procedure whereby non-member beneficiary countries pay part of their union contribution to the budget of the joint undertaking. furthermore, the evaluation and selection procedures should be modified to better align the supported projects with the european strategic objectives of the programme. the report also highlights that joint ventures are implemented in the framework of public-private partnerships, and that it is very important that conflicts of interest are properly addressed. it is essential that the joint undertakings, including artemis, inform the discharge authority adequately of the control mechanisms within their organisation that allow for the proper management and prevention of conflicts of interest. the final accounts report draws attention to the fact that the comprehensive review of the financial situation of the embedded computing systems did not reveal any serious deficiencies. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for the clean sky joint undertaking in the context of the closure procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the final report welcomes the adoption of a comprehensive strategic internal audit plan for the period 2010-2012; however, the rapporteur misses the fact that certain key processes such as ex-ante examination of cost claims and ex-post audits were only planned to start in 2011. the final audit report concluded that the financial and compliance reviews of the aeronautical and environmental projects did not reveal any serious deficiencies. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union in respect of the eniac joint undertaking in the context of the closure procedure for the financial year 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the joint undertaking should seek to encourage beneficiaries to identify strategically important projects and national programmes that could be implemented more effectively if managed at european level. the exemption report also highlights the need to involve additional national support organisations. the final accounts report draws attention to the fact that the financial audit was conducted in an orderly manner and did not reveal any major deficiencies. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for the joint undertaking for research on innovative medicines, in the context of the closure procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. with regard to the management of the joint undertaking, the final report highlighted that the functioning of the internal governance structures is not yet optimal, there is still a lack of proactive communication activities and the available human resources capacity is not being used to the required extent. in the case of the joint undertaking, it should be highlighted that several implementing measures have been introduced in respect of the operational processes in order to avoid conflicts of interest. all persons involved in the activities of the joint undertaking shall comply with the rules laid down in advance. the final report shall also invite the joint undertaking to develop an action plan containing concrete measures and deadlines to prevent conflicts of interest and to strengthen transparency and to make it available to the discharge authority at a later stage. the final report draws attention to the fact that the financial audit was conducted in an orderly manner and did not identify any major deficiencies in the area of the innovative medicines initiative. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for the european joint undertaking for the development of fusion energy and iter in the context of the closure procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the court of auditors stated that it was satisfied that the annual accounts for the financial year 2010 were reliable and that the underlying transactions were legal and regular. the final audit report draws attention to the fact that the financial audit was conducted in an orderly manner and did not reveal any major deficiencies in the areas examined. at the end of march, the committee decided to grant the discharge, to close the accounts for 2010, and i supported the discharge in the vote in committee and in the vote in plenary.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for the sesar joint undertaking in the context of the closure procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the report welcomes the fact that the court of auditors has found the accounts of the sesar joint undertaking for the financial year 2010 to be reliable and that the underlying financial transactions were legal and regular in all material respects. the rapporteur encourages the commission and the member states to strengthen the effectiveness of the application of the single european sky regulations in order to increase the efficiency of the investments made in the sesar project. the final audit report concludes that the financial and regularity reviews of the joint undertaking have not revealed any serious deficiencies. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to grant the discharge, closing the 2010 accounts. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − the annual report on the protection of the european union's financial interests and the fight against fraud will be drawn up in the context of the final accounts for 2010. as shadow rapporteur on the motion, i support the rapporteur's report, which gives a good account of the efforts made by the european commission, olaf and the member states in 2010 to protect the community's financial interests and to strengthen the sound use of community funds. the report shall also adequately identify areas where further action is needed and where action so far has not been successful. the number of irregularities detected increased by 2010 in almost all budgetary areas, with the exception of the pre-accession funds and traditional own resources. on the expenditure side of the overall budget, the proportion of irregularities attributable to fraud increased from 0,13% to 0,34%. in 2009, fraud cases amounted to only eur 180 million, while in 2010 this amount increased to eur 478 million. the irregularities reported occur most frequently at the implementation stage and to a greater extent at the selection and procurement stage. making the public procurement system more transparent and flexible could prevent a lot of mistakes. the commission has a duty to implement the reform of the public procurement system. i supported the vote on the report in committee and in plenary.
in writing. − as in previous years, this report contains the latest information on irregularities and suspected fraud reported by the member states. the report also examines a specific topic, agreed in advance by the commission and the member states, namely the risk of double funding in cohesion policy. in my view, the current rules do not always provide an adequate and sufficient means of detecting and preventing the misuse of eu funds, for example, in view of the different rules of the member states on the matter, in particular as regards the sanctioning of fraud. different rules on procedures, offences and penalties in different member states also constitute such an obstacle, hampering fraud investigations and criminal proceedings between member states. i would still consider it necessary to establish a uniform legislative framework in order to harmonise the member states' legislation. however, the question is whether the detailed rules which are the responsibility of the member states can be effectively harmonised, given the differences between them, and, above all, how much the member states concerned contribute to this. i believe that the rapporteur's findings are correct, that the commission's annual reports are based on inaccurate and incomparable data, and that this raises questions about the purpose of the reports in general. i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − in the context of the digital agenda for europe, the commission has set itself the objective of reducing the gap between roaming and domestic charges to almost zero by 2015. this objective can be further achieved by finding a solution that guarantees competition and the effective functioning of the roaming market beyond the deadline. efforts to spread broadband networks can also support the spread of substitutes for roaming services. the new regulation (roaming iii) also regulates roaming charges for calls and sms and introduces an important new element: the euro data rate for retail data roaming services. the proposal also introduces a number of structural measures to increase competition, while maintaining maximum prices in order to provide customers with a 'safety net' for the period during which competition itself reduces prices. it should be emphasised that from 1 july 2014 customers will be able to conclude a mobile roaming contract separate from the national roaming contract. this will improve the competitive situation in the roaming market and is one possible direction of development. in conclusion, the report is extremely timely and clearly in the interest of the citizens of the union, and i voted in favour of it.
in writing. − the regulation establishes a common approach to ensure that users of public mobile communications networks do not pay excessive prices for roaming services within the union when travelling within the union, compared to national tariffs. unfortunately, roaming charges are still unjustifiably higher than domestic prices and the cost-price relationship remains far from what it would be under genuine competitive conditions. high charges are a disincentive to the use of mobile devices for cross-border travel within the union. to ensure a functioning internal market and to protect consumers, we need to guarantee fair roaming charges for eu citizens travelling for leisure or work. i believe that we must achieve as soon as possible the complete elimination of the distinction between national and roaming tariffs. the proposal is a major step towards this objective, as it reduces the price of telephone calls and sms messages within the territory of other member states and forces market players to compete even in the case of data roaming.
in writing. - traditional media used to be the mouthpiece of a few. now, with the rise of the internet, and the advancement of technology, there are new ways of committing identity theft. the electronic media have become a major source of manifestations that push the boundaries of freedom of expression and often go beyond them. slander has become an international issue. unfortunately, when the regulation on non-contractual obligations (rome ii) was adopted, the protection of personal rights was excluded from its scope. in my view, the conflict-of-laws rules on tort law should be harmonised in the interests of transparency and predictability. while member states are free to determine the balance between the individual fundamental rights at their discretion, it is time, in the interests of legal certainty, to clarify at community level the law applicable to liability for infringements of privacy and personality rights.
in writing. − since the fall of the berlin wall, the arab spring has been seen as the most significant political transformation in europe's neighbourhood. this process provides a great opportunity to align the eu's trade and foreign policy interests with the fundamental values of human rights, democracy and a free society. it is recognised that the eu has exclusive competence in trade and investment policy, which allows the union to respond effectively to unrest. there is a growing feeling among civil societies in the southern mediterranean countries that the eu should take a more proactive role in promoting the political and economic transformation of the region. as the economic crisis of 2008 directly affected the main sectors that drive the economies of the southern mediterranean countries and as the turmoil of the arab spring in tunisia, egypt, syria and libya further exacerbated the economic decline of these countries, i consider it necessary to strengthen the links in order to create investment opportunities. this strategy should be implemented in full cooperation with the other policy areas. the european investment bank (eib) plays an important role in supporting small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) in the southern mediterranean region through the euromediterranean investment and partnership facility (emip). these companies need the necessary investments to innovate and restructure in a way that will enable them to exploit the opportunities offered by the eu's internal market. i also welcome the latest extension of the ebrd's mandate and hope that the bank's experience in eastern europe will bear fruit in the southern neighbourhood.
first of all, i would like to thank rapporteur busuttil for this excellent report, not only because it is a great honour, but also because i find it very timely and very useful, as it highlights the importance of a tool which allows us to deal more precisely and transparently with the internal market instruments and the tasks ahead of us. i agree with my colleagues who say that, despite its long history and its many benefits, the internal market is still not working perfectly. our ultimate aim is precisely to make this market increasingly beneficial for all european citizens, so that all players without exception can benefit from it. the results of the table in the report before us show a positive trend, and this is a very important step, but it is by no means a cause for complacency. it is time to take a closer look at the areas where we are still lagging behind and where further efforts are needed. this report helps us to do this and also shows that the favourable overall indicators should not obscure the details, the problems that still exist today. to mention one example, public procurement, which accounts for some 15-20% of european gdp. if we can fully open up these markets and apply european legislation properly , then governments can make significant savings for the future. we must be clear that europe will be more competitive if it pays sufficient attention to generating economic growth alongside strict fiscal policies. i would also like to stress the importance of strengthening solvit, which is also an initiative of the commission. it is very important that we see what deficits we have in the area of law enforcement. but that's not enough. we need to strengthen the one-stop shop, create more information portals and better coordination mechanisms, and we also need to eliminate overlapping tools. our task is therefore to give effect to the legislation in a way that meets people's expectations in terms of jobs, growth and security. i'm convinced that we don't need these scorecards to pick a task, but to understand exactly where we are right now. i welcome the progress made by the member states so far and encourage them - together with the rapporteur - to step up coordination and further increase transparency.

in writing. as the shadow rapporteur on the homeland security strategy, i have to thank mrs borsellino for the openness and flexibility with which she has taken the people's party proposals. our successful compromises prove that when we address the problems and challenges of homeland security on a professional basis, without prejudice, we can find common ground and rise above political differences. the internal security strategy is an important tool for effectively tackling many forms of crime. the focus is on combating different forms of international organised crime, such as mafia-type or 'white-collar' crime and money laundering, and on strengthening the european counter-terrorism. it is essential to develop a common threat assessment system and to better coordinate member states' practices for the effective protection of critical it infrastructures. cooperation between the public and private sectors, as well as between civilian and military actors within the union, is essential to ensure the cybersecurity of the eu. the newly created cyber security centre also serves this purpose. the definition of european critical infrastructures is an urgent task. the protection of the external borders, not least the prevention and control of natural and man-made disasters, requires joint action. in order to achieve these objectives, it is essential to allocate proportionate budgetary resources to them, and to ensure that the measures included in the internal security strategy are appropriately reflected in the 2014-2020 budget.

in writing. there can be no effective consumer protection when consumers continue to feel that they are being served by individual businesses, when they do not understand the terms of the contract or when they have little hope of protection in their legal dispute. at the same time, i believe that effective consumer protection cannot exist without cooperative and consumer-friendly businesses. i would like to point out that i will soon be submitting a written declaration to confirm this, in order to create a consumer-friendly, business-friendly trademark, because as consumer complaints show, consumers still feel the imbalance and the total vulnerability that exists between the consumer and the business side in the case of consumer interest. this can be done mainly, but not exclusively, by amending the legislation to a degree and quality that has not been achieved so far. last but not least, i cannot fail to mention consumer awareness, which is the basis for strong and effective consumer protection. without awareness, there is no prevention, and without prevention, there can be no protection. this is why it is necessary for consumers to know which organisations to turn to in the event of a legal dispute, from the education of young people to the assistance of the elderly. mrs perez's own-initiative report is exemplary, since it concerns a very sensitive and urgent area. i thank the rapporteur for his timely remarks and i am voting in favour of this report.

in writing. small and medium-sized enterprises play a very important role in the european economy. it is therefore very important to facilitate their access to the resources of the structural funds, which can provide them with investment opportunities and funds that can also mean their survival during the economic and financial crisis. the instruments and forms of support currently in place do not always reach the beneficiaries who really need them. unnecessary administrative burdens and the obligation to pre-finance remain a problem for the majority of smes. the definition of smes would need to be reconsidered, as the 2005 definition is considered to be outdated in several respects. small and medium-sized enterprises account for 99% of european companies, and their importance is shown by the fact that 85% of new european jobs were created by smes between 2005 and 2010. these businesses are clearly the key to growth and development in a europe emerging from the crisis, and it is therefore important that the commission should support, by all possible means, the access of smes to structural funds.

in writing. − the outcome of the vote made it clear that consumer protection is important for everyone, but effective consumer protection cannot be achieved without real action, as consumers continue to feel as vulnerable as before. the european parliament's continued consumer-friendliness is a positive trend and it is also welcome that meps have voted in favour of strengthening consumer protection. but as long as the terms of the banks' contracts remain incomprehensible, as long as they are limited by a code of conduct rather than by binding legislation in their unilateral contract modifications, there will continue to be a huge gap between consumers and these businesses. similarly, the eviction moratorium can only be considered as a temporary relief, since the distressed debtor will in any case be forced to leave his house or apartment after the expiry of the period, and consumers cannot expect their salaries to increase in proportion to the rate at which the repayment instalments of the loans may increase. at the same time, it is therefore necessary to strengthen the future role of conciliation bodies, which play a vital role in the simple, free and rapid settlement of consumer disputes. but this will only have an impact on consumer protection if all the substantive changes that have long been advocated by consumer organisations are implemented.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because i believe that if we are to succeed in creating a single internal market that is open to the world but can also build on its own internal strength, then european efforts must be matched by performance at member state level. as i said in my speech during the keynote debate, our job now is to enforce the law without fear or favour, and we must do so in the future. it is very important to meet citizens' expectations for jobs, growth and security. if we are to keep up with the changes, we need to make the regulation sufficiently flexible and we need to recognise that not everything can be controlled from brussels. once again, i thank the rapporteur, mr busuttil, for his humble work, which highlights the importance of a tool that allows us to deal more precisely and transparently with the instruments of the internal market and the tasks ahead of us.
in writing. − in turkey, all relevant ministries are working hard to launch projects that will make the legislation to improve women's living standards effective. more importantly, these ministries are working together on gender equality. however, i should also note that the turkish statistical office published a significant figure last year: among the five million turkish citizens over the age of 15, 84 percent of the illiterate population are women. the employment rate for women is not very encouraging either, with the current employment rate for women in the eu at 63.9%, compared to 26.1% for turks. a dual trend seems to be emerging in the way women are valued and judged in turkey: globalization, which allows them to be more fully fulfilled in their private and professional lives, and religious tradition, which continues to permeate the fabric of society. i congratulate the rapporteur on her timely findings and i also particularly welcome the fact that her report is based on secondary analysis and interviews. i hope that the report will be used by interested parties and that the positive trend outlined in the report will continue in the future.
in writing. − i voted for the amendment of the rules of procedure of the european parliament with regard to the implementation of the european citizens' initiative. i welcome the clarification of the rules of procedure of parliament. i believe it is important that in a body such as the european parliament, which is directly elected by the citizens, the institution of the citizens' initiative should be supported as effectively as possible. the european parliament can contribute most effectively to citizens' initiatives by organising a public hearing, which it must hold within three months of the submission of the initiative to the commission. i support the commission's participation in these public hearings and agree with the clarification of the circumstances of the hearing in the regulation. as a member of parliament, i support the idea of having the organisers of the initiative heard in our own constituency at public hearings. on the other hand, as a member of the committee on petitions, i recognise the special role and specific experience of this committee in relation to citizens' initiatives.
in writing. − on 1 april this year, the european citizens' initiative launched a new instrument of participatory democracy, giving eu citizens a genuine political initiative. citizens can thus be directly involved in eu legislation, further enhancing the eu's democratic legitimacy. since the regulation provides for the possibility for organisers to present a citizens' initiative at a public hearing if they succeed in collecting one million signatures, it is now necessary to amend the rules of procedure of the european parliament. to this end, the report shall provide a stable procedural framework for the smooth organisation and conduct of public hearings. the committee responsible for the subject shall be responsible for organising the public hearing in parliament with the assistance of the committee responsible for petitions. this is to ensure that citizens can refer important matters to the most appropriate forums. the committee on petitions has decades of experience in this field, but it is the members of the committee responsible who are really able to influence the future of the sectoral policy concerned.
in writing. economic growth has slowed or stagnated in most eu countries and even declined in a number of member states, including hungary. in europe, 25 million people are unemployed, half the young people in greece and spain, and one fifth in most of the member states are unable to find work. it is sad that future generations are increasingly looking outside europe for a way to prosper. unilateral restrictions not coupled with economic stimulus are narrow, affecting all but the richest segments of society and affecting all social groups severely. disillusionment, hopelessness and social exclusion are driving the tide of extremism, as the recent election results in greece show. opinion polls show a rise in populist, radical forces in many places. alarming data and facts prove that the crisis management so far has not led to results. if the pro-european forces do not win a majority in the june elections in greece, it could lead to disaster. the mediterranean country has the potential to attract member states from the european periphery and from the region of eastern and central europe. finally, the recognition is maturing in the governments of the member states, which have so far rejected the solutions long advocated by the left, that in addition to responsible fiscal policy, economic stimulus measures, deepening european integration and solidarity, and the elimination of internal imbalances in the euro area are needed to avoid even greater trouble. the concrete steps to achieve this will be decided at the summit on wednesday.
in writing. the french presidential elections and the recent changes of government in denmark, slovakia and romania mark a political turn, a breakthrough for the left. the question is, is there an economic turnaround? will the european union finally abandon its conservative economic policy, which is focused exclusively on deficit reduction? the situation in greece and the difficulties in spain, portugal and ireland clearly show that restrictions are not enough in themselves. the capacity of european societies to cope with the crisis is limited and it will take years, even decades, to eliminate the deficit. we need to find a new economic model that offers an alternative to the free market capitalism that is causing the crisis. it is worth mentioning why the scandinavian countries were able to effectively resist the crisis. in these countries, widespread social benefits are financed by high taxes, and the financial and banking systems are subject to strict regulations. european countries need to focus on investments for long-term growth, such as modernising infrastructure and energy networks, research and development, eradicating extreme poverty and improving education. the costs of these investments should not be taken into account in the deficit calculation under the stability and growth pact. we should introduce european project bonds to finance investments for growth.

in writing. the european union has no place for any form of negative discrimination and all forms of harassment must be combated. no acts of violence against sexual, ethnic or national minorities are permitted. this is the tangible essence, the foundation, of the protection of fundamental human rights. i, and the hungarian people's party delegation of which i am a member, have consistently condemned all such discrimination for years. at the same time, as someone who fights against this discrimination, i find it saddening and thought-provoking that a parliament which adopts a resolution against homophobia every six months has not adopted a resolution for five years against the very serious violations of the law and the humiliating discrimination against linguistic and national minorities which affects hundreds of thousands of people in many member states of the union. 10% of the eu population is affected by this type of discrimination on a daily basis. i would like to draw your attention to the fact that the motions for resolutions tabled by the socialist, liberal and green groups on the fight against homophobia are based on the same slip: in the case of hungary, amendments tabled in the municipal assembly of the capital are stigmatising a country, which has failed to vote on them in the local assembly. we know very well here in parliament what the fate of the rejected amendments is, what they mean: absolutely nothing. therefore, to brand hungary implies political ill will. the resolution, and other similar texts, make the ep's work seem less serious.

the next item is the report by marielle de sarnez, on behalf of the committee on international trade, on the trade imbalance between the eu and china (2010/2301 (ini)) (a7-0141/2012).
in writing. the eu's trade deficit with china is growing unabated. in 10 years, it more than tripled, jumping from 50 billion euros to 170 billion euros. this huge imbalance can only be partly explained by the fact that china is more competitive in labour costs. as indicated in the report, the asian country and the companies operating there engage in a number of practices that are contrary to international trade law and practice, thereby gaining further competitive advantage. since china joined the wto, it has been the biggest beneficiary, the real beneficiary of globalization. however, the wto brings not only benefits but also obligations. it is unacceptable that beijing supports export-producing companies in various ways, such as through export credit schemes. the usa has recently taken safeguard measures against chinese dumped products. the eu must protect european jobs and take similar action against illegal imports. for example, it is in the interest of the entire world economy that the chinese government does not artificially keep the yuan exchange rate low in the interest of exporting companies. european companies wishing to export to china, especially those who sell agricultural products such as meat, face a number of technical and legal obstacles that are unusual in international practice. the commission has not established that the chinese exporting producers are in a position to benefit from the measures in force.
i can only welcome the report before us because it finally clearly states that financial transactions were undervalued compared to other transactions and because it offers a real, tangible solution to the economic situation in crisis. this is the first step in a european crisis management that will not only be implemented at the level of international fiscal conventions, but will also have a practical impact. i regret that my new european tax cannot be implemented on a global scale, or even perhaps on a union-wide scale. however, it can still provide significant revenue to the european economy. enhanced cooperation is a good tool to help financial transactions develop in a more regulated, traceable way, thus excluding the possibility of a complete disconnection of the financial sector from the real economy once again. the report also has the merit of starting very correctly from the assumption that the financial world, which was one of the main causes of the crisis by its speculative and short-term subservient behaviour, did not help in managing the crisis, but rather - if you like - wanted to be the beneficiary of it. this contradiction is addressed in the report and transactions for which no transaction tax has been collected are professionally excluded, thereby increasing transparency and reducing the risk of tax avoidance and evasion. i therefore recommend that the report be adopted.
the early introduction of a european financial transaction tax is also of paramount importance to make clear to european citizens that the union is not placing the burden of the crisis on them but on the financial sector. a transaction tax at european level would not apply to the general public, but to high-value business transactions, speculative operations. it would be more equitable to share the burden of crisis management. in contrast, in hungary, the government is preparing to introduce new restrictions affecting the population under the banner of a transaction tax. this transaction tax has nothing to do with what we're discussing. this new tax, which will be effective in the future, will be charged on retail transactions, including cash withdrawals and deposits, bank transfers, credit card purchases. this is unacceptable! the union transaction tax applies to the financial sector, national and international oligarchs.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because it offers a tangible solution to the current economic situation. this is the first step in european crisis management, which will not only take place at the level of international fiscal conventions, but will also have practical effects. the report and thus the tax excluded pension funds, an important factor in preventing the savings of the population from being judged and regulated in the same way as the speculative financial transactions that triggered the financial crisis. furthermore, as this new european tax is unlikely to be introduced globally - even in europe, unfortunately - the report refers to enhanced cooperation as a novelty, which is a really good tool and can help to make financial transactions more regulated and traceable, thus preventing the possibility of a complete disconnection of the financial world from the real economy. the reporting shall correctly and professionally exclude transactions for which no transaction tax has been collected, thereby increasing transparency and reducing the risk of tax evasion and avoidance.
in writing. − china is currently one of the world's largest economic powers and the eu's second largest trading partner. i agree with the rapporteur that china's economic development and accession to the wto is not only beneficial but also a responsibility in terms of china's positive role in the world economic order. i voted for the report which states that there is an imbalance between the two regions, that there is a trade advantage for china through various means. however, china should also not forget that the lack of growth in europe is also a problem for the chinese economy. the report therefore recommends continuing reciprocity in trade. china is still not complying satisfactorily with wto rules and therefore the wto should not grant met to the country before 2016 without fulfilling all the criteria. european companies are prevented from accessing the national market by chinese intervention and chinese regulations are not clear and transparent. in public procurement, foreign investors in china cannot participate on an equal footing with their chinese competitors, which is contrary to the treatment of foreigners in european public procurement. the protection of intellectual property rights remains a concern in the country, and china should fight against counterfeiting and piracy.
we are now discussing a very good report based on excellent commission material. i would like to focus on water and waste management as a resource efficiency issue. 2012 is also the year of the water review. the union is far behind in terms of water conservation, in terms of reusing water, compared to israel, for example, and i think it is very important that we take steps to solve the water problems of the mediterranean region, but also of central europe, the danube basin. in waste management, i agree with those who want to ban the dumping of recyclable waste altogether. it is unfortunate that my country, hungary, is facing european ambitions in waste management. the right-wing government has completely abolished the existing waste management system, and the commission has unfortunately opened an infringement procedure.
the european youth unemployment rate is alarming, as has been mentioned many times today, and even more alarming when you look beyond the average figures. we see that disparities between member states have increased in this area, and that within member states there are also very large disparities in opportunity, especially at the expense of those with low levels of schooling. the feeling of youthful redundancy is a good breeding ground for the spread of extremist ideas and a cause of many human tragedies. we have urgent practical work to do, we have to use the opportunities offered by the internal market to make young people truly mobile, but we have to do it in a way that does not absorb well-qualified, highly qualified young people from countries with a better labour market. we must solve the transition from education to the labour market, we must not take resources away from education - as we see in many countries, these resources are being reduced - and we must pay special attention to disadvantaged young people, roma, disabled people, immigrant young people. it's only through a coordinated, targeted program that we can avoid an entire generation feeling lost, with all the consequences of this. (the opponent has consented to answer a question marked with a blue card under article 149 (8) of the ec treaty. )
it's a two-card answer. i think that the program that started about two years ago -- the microcredit program -- could be a great opportunity in this regard as well. it's exactly the kind of thing that helps people who don't have credit. and for people who are just starting out, and maybe they have an idea that they haven't tried yet, it's very difficult for banks to give them loans. i think if we develop this program in this way, it's going to be a very big help.
it's unbelievable that in 2012, more than 60 years after the birth of european integration, 55 years after the principle of equal pay for equal work was established, with a series of wars behind us, beyond economic crises and currently experiencing it, there is still more than a 16 percent pay gap between male and female workers. the bauer report is a fine piece of work. it thoroughly analyses problem areas such as job classification, social dialogue, possible sanctions, the introduction of collective redress, which i am particularly pleased to see. i believe that with publicity and increased transparency, this wage gap can be significantly reduced. quite simply, commissioner, i am thinking here of the need for economic organisations to include the statistical statement of the wage gap in their annual accounts and thus to ask for the public's help in closing this gap. i congratulate the rapporteur and support this report.
contracts must be respected, it's an old roman legal principle, and no one has ever questioned it, and this is true even if the crisis in many countries is creating difficult, complicated situations, serious domestic political debates. we have this in our own countries, and yet we are constantly pressuring each other to abide by the agreements we have made, whether they are internal agreements or external agreements. what happened here is not a casual breach of contract, but what appears to be a very deliberate departure from the contract. there are at least three points here that constitute a willful breach of contract. you can't discriminate between member countries, you discriminate between member countries. the 10% increase above the average is when the safeguard clause can come into force, and that 10% should be counted on the 27 member states, not on the 8 or the 4 or the ones we are going to cut. consequently, they're in breach of contract here, too. and the third violation of the treaty is that the established quota should be above the last governing quota, and here it is just the opposite, it is below it, instead of three thousand, it is set at two thousand. so that suggests a pretty serious awareness. and fourthly, there was no consultation before these measures were introduced. this is an element that calls into question good intentions, and i agree with my colleague mr busuttil that we expect a firm and clear position from the european commission, and that this swiss provision, this breach of the treaty, is unacceptable.
in writing. − the hungarian people's party delegation believes that there is no place for any negative discrimination in the european union, that all such harassment must be condemned and that action must be taken against it. no acts against sexual, ethnic, national or linguistic minorities shall be permitted. this is the essence and the foundation of the protection of fundamental human rights. our delegation nevertheless voted against some of the paragraphs put to a separate vote and abstained from the final vote on the draft decision because it contained factual errors, omissions and proposals which, under the principle of subsidiarity, are now clearly a matter for the member states. the legitimate fight against homophobia must be separated from the slippage of individual political groups.

in writing. − i find it sad that the noble and welcome goal of combating prejudice related to ethnic, religious or gender identity is being reduced to a tool of a political campaign by the political left, which is increasingly hostile, often violent and intolerant. my abstention from the closing vote does not mean that i do not fully agree that homophobia is an intolerant behavior that violates the dignity of our fellow human beings. it is unacceptable, however, that the resolution is riddled with slips, untruths and unabashed interference with legislation which is strictly a matter for the member states. i find it outrageous, for example, that the original text attempts to blame the shameful manifestations of a far-right party in hungary on the ruling party. the previous people's party resolution, known as the weber-bus-style, is in my opinion an exemplary vote in favour of protecting human dignity and opposing all forms of prejudice, but it is also tolerant and balanced, and does not contain any slippages and exaggerations that can be attributed to political marketing. i am very sorry that this resolution will not be put to the vote, because i would be proud of it even if, in the light of the mathematics of parliament, the resolution were to be voted down by the majority of the left.
in writing. − as a consequence of the protracted european economic crisis, unemployment among young europeans increased from 14.7% in 2008 to 22.1% in 2011. of those under 25, 5.5 million are unemployed. the aim of the plenary debate is to call on the european commission to take action on the future and employment opportunities of europe's young people and to highlight the seriousness of the situation. at the beginning of 2012, the european commission sent action groups to eight member states where youth unemployment is well above the eu average. the task of the action groups is to draw up action plans to promote youth employment. the french rapporteur reports to the european commission on the results achieved so far by the commission with the help of these action groups and asks the commissioner responsible what additional means and resources the commission intends to focus on job creation, in particular on tackling youth unemployment. i supported the rapporteur and the draft resolution which seeks to solve one of europe's most pressing problems.
in writing. − i supported the report, which aims to make recommendations to improve legislation to close the gender pay gap. it also proposes the establishment of a europe-wide strategy. the gender pay gap in the european union is still at an average of 16.4% and is showing an increasing trend in some member states. the reasons for this are complex and interrelated. i agree with the intention of the report to draw the commission's attention to the review of the 2006 directive on the implementation of the principle of equal opportunities and equal treatment of men and women in matters of employment and occupation. i consider the recommendations for change to be comprehensive and thorough. i consider it a remarkable initiative for the european parliament to introduce a 'european women and business' award to employers who support women and female managers and apply the principle of equal pay.
in writing. − following the european parliament's 2008 decision, member states have not done everything possible to ensure the principle of equal pay for men and women as required by the decision. the decision created the possibility of applying sanctions and penalties to employers who do not comply with the principle of equal pay. member states should use all the means laid down in this decision to reduce the gender pay gap. the aim of the bauer report is to call on the european commission to review and amend the existing directive in order to tackle discrimination against women as effectively as possible. the report makes specific proposals in nine different areas and calls on the commission to prepare by 15 february 2013 a revised draft directive that more effectively protects the rights of women workers, taking into account these recommendations. the pp group supports the report in the plenary vote.
in writing. - i have supported the bauer edit report as it is based on a principle already laid down in the treaty of rome. it must finally be made clear that just as no distinction between men and women is to be made in fundamental human rights, neither male nor female workers can be discriminated against in any matter, including matters of pay. the report before us is very comprehensive and thorough, and it addresses the issues that have been raised by the commission on several occasions, but which still have major shortcomings, and which are still problematic in this area, such as the classification of jobs, social dialogue and possible sanctions against certain employers. the inclusion of collective redress in the report's recommendations is particularly important, as it is a very novel recognition that, although it is not mandatory, it is increasingly, and now not only in the economic sphere, a redress that is recommended to all member states in order to make the fundamental european policies and values applicable in everyday life.
in writing. − i consider the swiss labour market restrictive measure to be discriminatory and my parliamentary colleagues strongly condemn it. our approach is clear: we believe that treaties should not be interpreted in a discriminatory way on the basis of nationality and that they should be respected even in difficult economic times. moreover, switzerland should not forget that union citizens are also a benefit to the swiss economy. i hope that it will be established urgently that switzerland cannot take such a selective measure after 30 april 2011, thereby accelerating the withdrawal of the decision, which has no legal or economic basis and cannot be justified by the number of eu citizens wishing to settle in switzerland.
in writing. the gsp aims to provide access to the eu market for developing countries that need it most, by applying preferential tariffs. however, the current regulation covers practically all non-european countries except the developed oecd countries. many of these countries have economies that are more competitive than the eu's or provide subsidies to exporting companies in breach of wto rules. in many cases, the competitive advantage is caused by supporting energy prices, which encourage higher co2 emissions. meanwhile, the eu is imposing significant additional costs on companies to meet climate objectives. these unjustified advantages harm not only the interests of european workers and european industry but also those of the truly needy states, as they force their less competitive products out of the market. the new system will result in the termination of the preferential treatment of certain highly competitive asian economies such as china and thailand after a transitional period. i've asked you to do this before. i welcome the commission's proposal to exclude from the preferential tariff preferences more competitive and richer countries such as saudi arabia, kuwait, russia and brazil. under the new system, the number of countries receiving preferential treatment will be reduced by more than half, from 176 to about 80. this will help ensure that developing countries that really need it can enjoy preferential market access.
mr. chairman, commissioner, fellow members of the house. i would like to address the role and importance of human rights in the eu's bilateral relations in connection with the report just presented. it is of the utmost importance that respect for and protection of human rights be an important element and an unavoidable condition of any cooperation agreement. this is particularly true when the human rights of eu citizens are violated by a particular country, and i am thinking here of bolivia, to take as an example the case of elder tóásó, who has been in pre-trial detention in bolivia since 16 april 2009, and this temporary status, which expired on 16 april, cannot be maintained under the current bolivian law. toásó has not been released to date. your right to a fair trial is being violated. i would therefore ask the relevant eu bodies to take human rights into account when concluding these conventions.
