in writing. as rapporteur for the draft opinion of the libe committee on the report, i would like to congratulate mr kalfin on his professional approach and balanced position. as is well known, cybersecurity is an important pillar of the union's internal security. as a popular opponent of the homeland security strategy, i have previously stated on several occasions that it is essential to develop a common threat assessment system and to better coordinate member states' practices for the effective protection of critical it infrastructures. cooperation between the public and private sectors, as well as between civilian and military actors within the union, is essential to ensure the cybersecurity of the eu. the newly created cyber security centre also serves this purpose. the definition of european critical infrastructures continues to be an urgent task. in order to achieve the objectives of the recently adopted internal security strategy, it is essential to allocate proportionate budgetary resources to them, and to ensure that the measures of the internal security strategy are appropriately reflected in the 2014-2020 budget.
the rapporteur. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i have a question for you. in a rapidly changing world, in a rapidly changing europe, energy needs to be provided to the eu population and businesses in the long term. in the age of uninterruptible power supply, digital economy and society, they've become even more important, if possible. it is a global challenge to ensure that energy does not lead to tension or conflict between countries in the world. at the global level, the european union is also competing with fast-growing, high-population economies. the eu is increasingly dependent on fossil-fuel-rich countries and transit countries. in addition to the eu's internal responses, market interconnection, efficiency gains and the full use of renewables, it is worthwhile to look beyond the eu's borders at our possible common policy. the following points are worth considering: although our current energy connections are mainly in the east and south, in the future all regions of the world will be connected to energy markets. so we need to develop our policy towards the five continents, the three oceans and the arctic circles. interconnected energy networks will increase the eu's competitiveness, but only if we create an institutional structure that prevents member states from being used against each other. we therefore recommend that the matter be discussed regularly at council meetings in the presence of the commissioner and the high representative. we also propose that a common database should help to conclude the best contracts and therefore invite the commission to draft the law. in the energy sector, investment and the speed of investment generation are of particular importance and should be optimised in decision-making. we propose that the european union commissioner for energy be given a larger and clearer mandate than the current one in negotiating certain issues in joint energy projects. in the energy market, we're not just talking about raw material dependence, we're talking about technology dependence. the european union is a leader in many areas. and that's true for legislation and for standardization. we need to get the neighbouring regions, and even the more distant ones, to look to our legislation, our decision-making mechanisms, as a model for themselves when they make such laws. the latter is particularly important in respect of environmental, safety and employment rules, because we see that sometimes european union companies are put at an unfair disadvantage vis-à-vis external actors if we fail to achieve a similar level of restraint. it is therefore very important that we continue to develop the rules through international organisations and ensure that they are properly implemented. we strongly support the commission's proposal, put forward by the commissioner for energy, and we would like to develop this further in our own work. i would like to thank the shadow rapporteurs for the active and effective debate, the many conversations, which we hope will ultimately lead to a greater energy input from the european union.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! a significant proportion of the european union's oil and gas supply comes from outside europe. as the union's external energy dependence is expected to increase, i support the idea that the eu regulatory framework for energy cooperation with third countries should be strengthened. in order to ensure effective advocacy, we need to take a united position in our negotiations with third countries. while eu member states negotiate with the major energy suppliers one by one, our strategic partners will pursue energy sharing diplomacy. i believe that the union's ability to act as a lobbyist could be developed in the future by concentrating on three areas: first, the differences between national energy markets should be reduced. this will be possible through the development of missing infrastructures and regulatory harmonisation. the single energy market is a guarantee that member states' interests are properly addressed and that a common position is reached in the field of energy. i would like to draw attention to the need for continuous coordination after the single market has been achieved. the establishment of effective coordination mechanisms could ensure continuous coordination between member states, thereby promoting the promotion of common interests in international negotiations. the third element is diversification. international negotiations should aim to ensure that the energy supply of the union and its member states is provided by several energy exporters at the same time. the diversification of suppliers and flexible contracts can help to prevent a complete power cut in the event of a major supplier's failure.
the committee on employment and social affairs has adopted an opinion. - mr president, members of the house. the european parliament's committee on social affairs and employment welcomes the initiative to coordinate the control of budgetary policies, in particular the provisions on the establishment of a common budgetary framework, and the proposals for increased surveillance of member states under the excessive deficit procedure. our commission considers it essential that the union's budgetary policy is fully aligned with the eu 2020 strategy, which aims to create new jobs and boost growth. the committee on social affairs and employment also considers it important that the budgetary autonomy of the member states be maintained, while budgetary policies are coordinated and controls are coordinated. finally, we consider important the provisions which propose to introduce a maximum level of public debt in the basic laws of each member state. we welcome the fact that several member states, including hungary, have already adopted such a provision in their new constitutions.
i would like to draw the attention of commissioner olli rehn and the rapporteurs to the fact that there is a disorder in the current regulations. the story is that the fiscal gold standard linked to the regulation under discussion only allows deficits to be exceeded within the euro area, not outside it. it is true that the three per cent and half per cent differ from one area to another, but if the rules do not specify how much more can be done for the euro area members with the growth stimulus package, how much more can be done, then this option is not available for the non-euro area member states and a larger deficit can be achieved for those in the euro area. it would be important to regulate precisely how far the growth stimulus for euro area members can go beyond half a percent.
the experience of the past months in managing the crisis has clearly shown that without fiscal discipline, we cannot talk about real economic growth or real economic performance. at the same time, the view that fiscal austerity is sufficient to manage the crisis is highly misleading. the truth is that nothing holds economies in a better grip than government debt, which paralyzes growth, cutting off the opportunity to stimulate growth and investment for years. the two reports before us set out minimum requirements. these minimum requirements are the basis for further development and therefore these reports must be supported. of course there will always be arguments and proposals for moving forward, but to do that, the previous points must be closed. it is our duty and responsibility, ladies and gentlemen, to ensure that this answer arrives in time.
in writing. the new legislative package adopted in 2011 to complement the sixth package on european economic governance aims to further strengthen fiscal surveillance in member states experiencing financial difficulties and to establish an effective crisis prevention mechanism. both new legislative proposals provide for provisions for the euro area member states. unfortunately, experience in recent years has shown that the difficulties of a single member state can have an impact on the economy of the union as a whole, and that a mechanism must be put in place to protect the other euro area member states from any negative spillover effects. we must not forget that the financial crisis affects not only the euro area but the union as a whole. however, we must emphasize that hungary is part of two great struggles at the same time, so we must assess the situation from our own perspective. on the one hand, we have to remedy the serious consequences of the wrong economic policies of the last eight years, and on the other hand, the euro crisis is also hurting our country. therefore, one of the most important provisions of the new fundamental law of hungary is the introduction of budgetary constraints. the purpose of this fiscal rule is to prevent our country from being indebted again. our first priority is to restore the country's economy, and this requires a continuous reduction in the national debt. the new constitution is a new beginning for hungary in this respect. thanks to the results of renewal, hungary is now able to stand on its own two feet.

madam president, i come from a country in which hundreds of thousands of people work and live in third-world circumstances, even though that country, hungary, is not in the third world: it is in the european union. these people work on the assembly lines of mainly foreign-owned companies, for about eur 250 a month. they work in warehouses and food stores, also for eur 250 a month. they can be fired overnight; they do not have any legal protection in terms of labour law. so my questions to the high representative are as follows – and i would very much appreciate it if, as an exception to her normal rules, she would give real answers to real questions. is this new eu representative for human rights going to examine cases of human rights violations within the european union? my second question is, do you regard labour rights as human rights and, if so, how are the mass violations of human rights in hungary going to be examined, and on the basis of what proceedings, by this new representative?
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − madam president, our second topic today, as you said, concerns the annual report on human right and democracy in the world in 2011. this report, which, i am pleased to say, is now on the council website, is set to be adopted in two weeks’ time. i am very happy to present the outline of this report to the house – on time – this year. the annual report shows how the eu really can make a difference when and where it matters most. the report covers 2011 but, if i might, i would like to give some up-to-date figures to illustrate what i mean. since the beginning of this year, we have made 36 declarations or statements on behalf of human rights defenders or country situations of concern. of course that does not include the countless number of démarches we conduct – for obvious reasons – behind closed doors. since january, we have also conducted 14 in-depth human rights discussions with countries around the globe, with another 22 still to come this year. from jordan to vietnam, from ukraine to the united states, from argentina to pakistan, the european union sits down in the spirit of partnership to see how we can, together, improve the human rights situation. the annual report details all of this action in 2011. but it does more: in line with the wishes of parliament, the report contains a section on freedom of religion or belief, which is so essential to the development of free societies. 2011 saw worrying developments in this connection, with intolerance spilling over from discrimination into violent attacks. also, for the first time, the report contains a section on international humanitarian law, which was the subject of various eu initiatives in 2011, culminating in the seven pledges made in november to the 31st international conference of the red cross and red crescent. as in previous years, the annual report catalogues our work across the whole range of thematic issues, bilateral and multilateral. it encapsulates the tremendous challenges to human rights and democracy in 2011, and how we responded. madam president, just a few examples to show that we stay consistently active: in bahrain, we have been working since the events of february 2011 to encourage all sides to engage in national reconciliation. i personally expressed support for the independent commission of inquiry and the implementation of its recommendations when i met with his majesty the king and with the foreign minister. we have kept a close eye on the case of mr al-khawaja and i have asked my officials to meet him in prison; this took place just before he ended his hunger strike. the eu is also following the fate of mr nabeel rajab, who has just been arrested yet again. in mongolia, the eu has been following the situation since the arrest of former president enkhbayar on corruption charges, his hunger strike and subsequent release. we are determined to support the democratic process in mongolia ahead of the elections on 28 june. in pakistan, the eu intervened in the case of mr behram khan, who has been on death row since 2003. on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, as the shadow rapporteur for the ppe group, i would like to thank the vice-president for her presence here today. in the spirit of the guiding values of the un charter, we take this report very seriously, and it is our priority to cooperate with the eeas for a comprehensive strategy. looking back at the past year, however, in spite of all our commitment, we have to face reality. regular and systematic human rights violations continue. people face repression around the world and innocent people die every day. yet, there is reason for hope. in spite of the aforementioned violations, we also witness increasing demand for human rights coming from the people, from brave individuals, inspired to action by the principle of human dignity. millions poured out into the streets to demand justice and freedom in the arab world; courageous people in china continue showing the world that they are not afraid. they want accountability, just as those tibetans who choose to sacrifice themselves in peaceful protest against china’s communist rule. human rights defenders and lawyers continue protecting those who want to practice their freedom. we are witnessing increasing non-violent resistance, reflecting self-consciousness among people – just as liu xiaobo wrote in 2006 in his essay ‘changing the regime by changing society’. his words are still of great inspiration today. our solidarity with entire communities that rise up in spite of intimidation is crucial. we cannot force change from the outside. it needs to come from the inside and we have to stay committed in order to catalyse this change now.

in writing. since november 2010, we have adopted three resolutions on tibet here in the european parliament. the fact that this matter is back on the agenda today indicates the seriousness of the situation. since 2009, 35 tibetan monks have set themselves on fire in their ultimate despair. the chinese authorities restrict the exercise of tibetan cultural and religious rights, access to education in the mother tongue, change the ethnic ratio of tibet through resettlement, and force tibetans to abandon their ancient way of life. this situation is untenable! increased attention is needed from all international bodies. it is true that tibet is an essential topic in the eu's dialogue with china, but it is not enough to talk about this issue, concrete steps must be taken. an effective and tangible solution must be found to protect the human rights of tibetans, including by applying pressure if necessary. i hope that the eu will be able to act more effectively in this area with the new eusr for human rights, who will be elected shortly. as the dalai lama himself told us at our solemn plenary session in brussels in 2008, "we want autonomy for tibet, not complete independence, and we will renounce the use of force". we must therefore give the tibetan community every assistance in its peaceful struggle for freedom and self-determination.
blue-card question. – i should like to ask mr hartong whether he has read the lisbon treaty, under which own resources are not a wish, but an obligation.
mr president, the new french president said that we need not only austerity but also growth. that is common sense. nobody wanted to have just austerity without growth. on a professional level, however, it is clear that governments have much less direct leverage on growth. austerity is a direct result of restrictive fiscal policies, but there is no such a thing as a growth policy. government action can have only a very indirect and stochastic impact on growth. in a market economy, most productive investments are undertaken by the private sector. it is only the private sector which can create sustainable jobs leading to increased output and tax revenues. the government can only facilitate this process by creating a business-friendly environment and investing in physical and human infrastructure. budgets are important tools to support private sector activity. the mff is considered as such. it can contribute to growth only if it concentrates resources on investments and initiatives to improve competitiveness and productivity. the mff should envisage more spending on research and development, education and infrastructure, and cut back significantly on current subsidies, including on agriculture. as i always emphasise, eu budgets are capital budgets and should not pretend to be similar to national budgets. they have to remain small and finance the future.

european citizens - and in hungary too - blame the union for the austerity measures that have been in place for years, and it is therefore extremely important that a strong cohesion policy is in place in the future, because if citizens see results in terms of job creation and investment under cohesion policy, this would strengthen confidence in the union. i consider it a big problem in the context of cohesion policy that my country could lose 1/5 of the funding, so it is very important for the "capping", the ceiling, not only to define the upper limit, but also to define the lower limit, what a member state could lose, because it is a 20-30% loss, which would be difficult for hungary to bear.
in writing. the rapid pace of recent events is not good. yesterday we were discussing the re-writing of the schengen agreement and the complete omission of the european parliament, and today we are talking about the creation of a kind of banking union, which is being referred to by aspirations and demands that have appeared in the european narrative, but whose precise practical depiction, impact and even consequences are unknown. i am glad that the council and commission's statement today is already marked by restraint, because, in the face of the heightened mood, there are doubts about the success of a european summit. however, i respectfully ask the members of the european council to reconsider their efforts so far and to commit to a higher level of integration only if it is accompanied by openness and preparedness across europe and, above all, if it serves the genuine interests of at least 500 million citizens, in addition to ensuring the long-term viability of the european banking system.
in writing. – as pointed out in the parliament's motion for a resolution, a better and more efficient use of the eu funds must be safeguarded, while ensuring that the eu budget is drafted in a transparent way. we also need sufficient budgetary flexibility in order to ensure that budgetary means are properly aligned with evolving circumstances and priorities. moreover, the eu budget should be a strong investment budget. we won't be able to exit this crisis and restore sustainable growth and employment without an ambitious budget that gives europe the means to achieve the targets set in the 2020 strategy. it should also guarantee fairness in distributing the resources and sustainability. therefore, it is important to ensure that cohesion policy continues to receive adequate funding. moreover, i am also worried by this new tendency within the european commission to reduce funding for citizenship programs. the decrease of funding also concerns gender-related issues, which is a very negative development in the light of the need to combat discrimination and promote equal opportunities policies. i strongly believe that fairness and sustainability of budgetary programming may only be achieved by means of introducing gender budgeting in the eu budget procedure.
in writing. the competitiveness chapter and its research and development lines are one of the most important instruments of the eu budget. the council should not burden these lines with the cost of major infrastructure investments. i would remind my colleagues that in the last period of the cfsp, the metaphor was that of 12 skinny cows beating each other. today, the european council cannot suffer the same fate. the 12 yellow stars should shine in the light of excellence and progress. we need a self-sustaining and pro-growth eu budget, in which iter (the european fusion reactor) and gmes (the european satellite observation system) should be financed outside the seven-year financial framework. however, if the council decides otherwise, it should attach financial conditions to it and not divert resources from r & d again! i believe that entrusting the council with the supervision of the payment of money is like entrusting cabbage to a goat. the decision of the council of ministers last week showed that the finance ministers can, with a single decision, taken in a narrow circle and with a narrow view, reduce the amount of research expenditure for the current year without the development ministers even knowing it. i therefore repeat: the council must not jeopardise europe's competitiveness!
in writing. for the first time, we are going to create a financial framework that is not a free agreement, but a constitutional obligation. we're limited in our options, and we can only say yes or no to the framework. the european council has also adopted a resolution on the situation in the middle east, which calls for the eu to take action to combat terrorism and to ensure that the situation in the middle east is not further complicated by the threat of terrorism. such a red line is the question of cohesion policy. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (emah) is based on the principle of equal treatment for all medicinal products. the eu funds allocated to cohesion policy must not be reduced, and at least the level of the 2007-2013 period must be maintained. this is a difficult task, since the european commission's proposal already envisages a reduction of almost 5% of the total budget and member states would only be able to call in 2.5% of their gdp. moreover, the calculation of the 2,5% ceiling is not based on future economic growth but on the period 2008-2010 during which hungary suffered a severe economic downturn under the leadership of the socialist governments.
on 28 november 2011, i was a member of the european parliament delegation of election observers to observe the congolese legislative and presidential elections in kinshasa. i personally monitored the vote. i can tell you from my own experience that the electoral process was marred by serious problems: irregularities and fraud. voter registration was incomplete, vote counting was opaque and violence occurred in some places. our election observation mission, after our stay in congo, has made recommendations for the provincial and local elections that have not yet taken place. i believe that it is essential that the legal framework for elections be respected, especially in a country with such a large population, where the mass turnout in the november elections is proof of the need for free elections. the publication of the results was accompanied by a great outcry in the streets of brussels, where the congolese in belgium expressed their displeasure with vandalism.
in writing. − iliana ivanova has been nominated by the bulgarian government as a member of the european court of auditors. since 2009, the mep has been a member of the european parliament: vice-chair of the committee on budgetary control, vice-chair of the special committee on the financial, economic and social crisis, vice-chair of the delegation for relations with the people's republic of china, member of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, and an alternate member of the committee on economic and monetary affairs. the bulgarian representative has already demonstrated his competence in the committee on budgetary control on several occasions, having been rapporteur for the following reports: the 2010 annual report of the european investment bank, the regulation on the hercule iii programme to promote activities in the field of the protection of the financial interests of the european union, the regulation on the financing, management and monitoring of the common agricultural policy. the bulgarian mep was responsible for the opinions on innovative financial instruments, the european financial stabilisation mechanism and the european stability mechanism in the committee. iliana ivanova was a municipal representative of sofia between 2007 and 2009, a member of the committee on budget and finance and the committee on european resources and environmental protection, previously worked in the united states, before which she was responsible for coordination with international financial institutions as a staff member of the bulgarian ministry of agriculture and food between 1999 and 2002. the ep's committee on budgetary control held a hearing on 4 june with iliana ivanova, following which a large majority of committee members voted in favour of the bulgarian candidate.
in writing. − in the light of the financial crisis, the main national audit institutions, the european court of auditors and the european parliament should cooperate more closely than ever to properly assess the quality of the use of eu funds. i voted in favour of the election of iliana ivanova because she was the one who initiated a hearing in the committee on budgetary control on budgetary control issues relating to the financial instruments between the institutions (efsm, esm, efsf). i think this could be the beginning of a new direction. as the audits of the new instruments to overcome the credit crisis were carried out in part by national audit institutions - some of them through the european court of auditors - and as it is the role of the european parliament to represent the democratic inquiry of the public in the process, it seems natural that all views should be heard in a joint discussion outlining the main challenges facing europe in relation to these stability mechanisms. in addition, my bulgarian colleague has won the respect of my colleagues for his diligent work, his excellent reports and his professional qualities, proving that he is fit to fill the post he has won. i congratulate mrs ivanova and wish her every success in luxembourg.
in writing. − the gsp aims to provide access to the eu market for developing countries that need it most, through the application of preferential tariffs. however, the current regulation covers practically all non-european countries except the developed oecd countries. many of these countries have economies that are more competitive than the eu's or provide subsidies to exporting companies in breach of wto rules. under the new regime, which will apply from 2014, the number of countries eligible for reduced duty rates will be reduced by less than half of the current level. this will ensure that only developing countries that are genuinely in need receive preferential treatment. that is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the two additional economic management packages which form part of the economic governance framework, because, in addition to further strengthening the timely monitoring of draft budgets submitted by euro area member states, and in particular by those member states experiencing financial difficulties, they also allow a preventive type of crisis prevention mechanism to function. this is to be achieved, inter alia, by requiring enhanced reporting in order to allow member states experiencing financial stability problems and excessive deficits to report on their budget deficits in a timely manner. the purpose of this mechanism is to prevent a negative financial situation from having a knock-on effect on the economy of the european union as a whole.
in writing. − as the mep responsible for the opinion on this report in the committee on employment and social affairs, i believe that the prolonged economic and financial crisis has put some member states in a very difficult situation, and that in order to overcome the imbalances in these member states, the european union intends to introduce in them a strengthened economic and budgetary surveillance which will ensure that the member state concerned is able to solve its economic and budgetary problems. however, it should not be forgotten that the introduction of new, more stringent control procedures could easily have side effects which could put the economy, society and the labour market of the member state in question in an impossible situation. the new procedures should be applied only with a broad view of the economic and social problems caused by the crisis. preventing the aggravation of existing problems, leading to the emergence of even greater budgetary problems or causing a slowdown in growth in the member state and, with it, in the union as a whole. i have followed this in my amendments to the report and in the vote in committee and in plenary. it is not possible to focus exclusively on the budgetary position of the member states, but it is necessary to take into account labour market and social impacts and to take into account all the union's objectives and all the union's founding documents, including the employment rate and poverty reduction objectives of the eu 2020 strategy. withdrawing development resources from countries in budgetary difficulties could lead to the preservation of differences between core and peripheral countries and could affect the growth prospects of the union as a whole. any unfounded suspension and sanction is contrary to the fundamental values of the european union, the idea of solidarity. in any event, member states should not be punished by withdrawals for macroeconomic imbalances, and sanctions can only be applied if the european commission has made a specific and thorough investigation to establish that the member state concerned has not fulfilled its obligations. in the absence of a thorough and careful examination of the problems, any proportionate suspension of community funds is unacceptable, since it is a blow to the beneficiaries in the member states who are the guarantors of economic recovery and employment growth.

in writing. − this report on the fiscal discipline of euro area member states facing excessive deficits is part of the common system of economic governance. indeed, i believe that, following the sixth governance package, the second governance package - which further clarifies the practical issues of budgetary discipline - is timely and essential if europe is to have a chance of getting its long-awaited economic growth off the ground. the text addresses important details, including not only additional reporting and reporting obligations for member states with serious budgetary imbalances, but also positive technical assistance affecting them, such as the commission's 'enhanced surveillance' or its timely recommendations in relation to an incorrect draft budget. i did not support the amendments relating to the individual stability bonds and the debt purchase fund because, in my view, they are not closely linked to the issue of european fiscal discipline and are, in many places, too elaborate and excessive. the report before us sets out minimum requirements and, although the final vote was not taken in order to allow the trialogue to proceed with the first reading agreement with the council and the commission, i supported the amended text itself, as i did in committee.

in writing. instead of tightening restrictions and raising taxes on the wealthiest sections of society, we need a european strategy for growth and jobs, one of the pillars of which is to make the most of the opportunities offered by the single market. the removal of barriers to the functioning of the internal market would not only bring savings to public finances but also give impetus to economic growth. most measures to complete the internal market do not require major investments, only political will and a prepared enforcement apparatus. however, the economic benefits of these measures can be measured in billions of euros if they stimulate intra-eu mobility, facilitate cross-border provision of services, facilitate access to finance for smes, stimulate innovation and ultimately create jobs. the proper functioning of the single market requires that european citizens, who are legally entitled to travel, work and reside freely within the territory of the european union, are not discriminated against in the exercise of those rights. it is the responsibility of member state governments and the european institutions to ensure that citizens are aware of their rights and obligations and are able to seek redress where necessary. we need to ensure that eu citizens working in another member state enjoy the same basic social rights as those who work in their own country. the freedom to provide services and economic activities must be accompanied by guarantees of social security.'

in writing. the 2010 red mud spill in hungary demonstrated that, however strict the rules on hazardous installations, accidents can occur in the event of negligent operation and lack of state control. it has also been established that compensation for damage caused by an accident is only guaranteed if the operator has full environmental insurance. i welcome the fact that mr áder has proposed that this should be made compulsory in the european union in his report. the landfill of industrial and mining waste cannot be considered a final solution. landfills should be minimised and recycling of the resulting waste should be pursued. the red mud could also be used to extract significant quantities of iron ore, chromium, copper, nickel and other precious metals. these procedures are not yet economically viable. the european union should therefore support research aimed at making the recycling of industrial and mining waste, including red mud, more efficient and economical
in writing. genital mutilation is one of the most grievous and harmful forms of gender-based violence affecting more than 140 million women globally and an unacceptably high number of 500 000 women in europe, with an estimated 180 000 girls being at risk of this destructive practice. intentionally damaging female genitals for non-medical reasons performed with primitive tools and without antiseptic precaution - is an obvious violation of fundamental rights and since it mostly affects girls younger than 15, a violation of the un convention on the rights of the child. no religious, cultural or traditional convention may justify its serious adverse consequences on girls' mental and physical integrity, including the irreparable damage of affected organs, painful sexual intercourse and childbirth, infection, shock, the transmission of hiv, tumours and complications after pregnancy and childbirth. effective sanctions need to be introduced against perpetrators, enabling law enforcement officials to mobilize all available instruments for combating this practice. taking into consideration the nature of genital mutilation, the close cooperation of national authorities and institutions with civil society, women's rights organisations, and representatives of affected communities - such as religious and community leaders - is essential for eradicating this form of violence against women.

in writing. − in order to effectively boost eu economic growth by creating the necessary political and economic conditions, the single market must be supported. this is necessary to ensure that it develops adequately and at the same time meets the changing conditions and challenges of the global economy. the internal market now comprises 27 member states and is therefore much more diverse than when it was created. it therefore needs to be transformed from a system of internal eu projects into an effective tool, thereby enabling the eu to become more competitive with the rest of the world. it will also contribute to strengthening territorial, economic and social cohesion and to achieving the objectives set out in the lisbon strategy. i believe that the european commission and the member states should develop their objectives for the future single market in terms of skills and employment. in addition to giving regions greater autonomy and accountability, this will enable programmes to increase skills to better adapt to the needs of regional and local labour markets, thus helping regions to cope with the specific challenges they face. the motion for a resolution contained these objectives and i therefore voted in favour of it.

in writing. − the european parliament adopted a resolution on the situation of women in the european union. i would like to begin my explanation by pointing out that the large number of immigrants coming to europe makes it wrong to think that this harmful habit is a problem of the third world! it is estimated that at least half a million women and girls in europe are living with genital mutilation. on the other hand, female genital mutilation is an abuse which violates the fundamental rights of the victim, such as the right to personal integrity or to physical and mental health. and since it is usually done on girls under 15, it is also considered child rape. the short and long-term adverse effects of the physical and psychological injury caused by the procedure are clearly demonstrated, not to mention the fact that the intervention can be fatal due to the lack of disinfection. i believe that the member states should establish a regulatory system to help women and girls to report cases of female genital mutilation.

in writing. − the scandal of poly implant prothèse using industrial silicone and low-quality breast implants indicates a serious malfunction of communication and data sharing among the national and eu level. during the commission’s review of the medical device directive (2007/47/ec) by the end of this year, a better system of guaranteeing the safety of medical technology needs to be developed, including reinforced eu-level market surveillance, introducing pre-market authorization for certain medical devices, developing an efficient tracking system for medical devices as well as establishing a single european database pooling information on available medical devices on the market. raising awareness is also a key element: patients and potential patients need to be informed about the potential risks of cosmetic surgery and that implants are not permanent and eventually they need to be replaced or removed. furthermore, a better regulation of advertising cosmetic surgery needs to be considered in order to ensure that patients are fully aware of the risks and there is a need for an eu-wide registration for breast implants, so that the endeavour to provide quick access to medical devices and the consequent will to increase profits would not overwrite patient safety.

the next item is the report by mr richard seeber, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on the implementation of eu legislation on water (a7-0192/2012).
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, it is very good that the european union has introduced strict legislation to protect our water resources, but we must add that practically no member state will be able to meet these targets by 2015. the report itself explains that this is a global problem that needs to be addressed locally, so to speak regionally. i would like to highlight a very important region in terms of water, the carpathian basin, which has developed over the centuries an organic water management system that is perfectly suited to the challenges facing europe today. unfortunately, this management of the carpathian basin ended with the treaty of trianon, and now, within the european union, there is perhaps an opportunity to rationalise and make the management of the carpathian basin operational again, since hungary, as an important water catchment area, receives some 114 cubic kilometres of water through our rivers, but we cannot always ensure the quality of the incoming water in the best possible way, whether in slovakia, romania, ukraine or austria, and this does not depend on hungary. so i ask both the european commission and the european parliament to look with a watchful eye to hungary and help us in this endeavour.
the excellent report by mr seeber shows that the european union is much more advanced in energy efficiency than in water conservation, and i suggest to mr potočnik that if buildings have an energy certificate, we should also introduce a water management certificate, because there is a lot of waste. you'd have to think about that. enable cohesion policy funds to finance renovations to improve water efficiency. it is good that we are financing the construction of irrigation systems under the common agricultural policy. we must ensure that this is the case not only for the new member states but also for the old member states, and i propose that special support be given to encourage the recycling of water for agriculture. in israel, the rate is 75%, in europe, cyprus and spain can only recycle 25%.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. the closing declaration of the recently concluded un rio+20 conference gave particular urgency to the 'blueprint process' in the union by identifying the issue of providing safe drinking water as one of the main areas of action for sustainable development in the future. there is much to be done, if we only consider the alarming un forecast that by 2025, two thirds of the world's population will be facing persistent water problems. in this light, i welcome the commission's initiative to undertake a water assessment of the european union, which will provide appropriate policy responses to current and future european water challenges in order to preserve our water resources. given the sheer size of the seeber report, i just want to make one point. it is very gratifying that the report deals with the issue of land use, since the question of arable land and freshwater cannot be dealt with separately. soil erosion in europe is very rapid, the quality of the soil is deteriorating, which also affects the quality of our surface and groundwater. as agriculture is known to be one of the largest water-using sectors, there is an urgent need to introduce integrated water management, i.e. to coordinate economic, social and environmental policies in a water-related manner. for example, if we continue to exploit arable land and do not ensure the natural replenishment of groundwater, some areas of europe could become deserts. to see how much of this is true today, take a look at spain, where some of the wetlands have already undergone irreversible ecological processes.
i want to talk about a small hungarian settlement, a hungarian settlement called ónod. we hungarians are very proud of this small town in the county of borsod-abaúj-zemplén, not only because 300 years ago there was a famous parliament here, but also because for 600 years there has been a fair in this town, the famous woolen fair. for 600 years, this fair has endured the turkish dula, the habsburg oppression, and even the decades of terror of communism, but it looks like it will no longer survive the european union, as news has surfaced that certain eu laws are not up to code. my fellow members, let us not lose sight of this community institution on the altar of globalism, where small farmers, producers, traders, small artisans can sell their wares and exchange their products. let's protect these communities. europe is richer, better off with the trade of the cloth.
there has been a great deal of confusion, commissioner dalli, about the new regulations in hungary regarding caged egg-laying hens. the general opinion among experts and the press is that it was invented by bureaucrats in brussels who may have only seen chickens in restaurants in their lifetime. the new member states were once again subject to a very disadvantageous system. while the old member states had 13 years, the new member states had only 8 years and a number of environmental investments had to be made in the poultry sector before and after accession. unfortunately, the double standard has been applied here too, and i believe that this expectation has not helped the prestige of the european union. it caused a lot of confusion, even though i consider animal welfare to be extremely important.
mr president, first of all let me thank the rapporteurs and my colleagues in the committee on internal market and consumer protection and the committee on transport and tourism for their work and their cooperation on this report. our common goal is to have the life-saving ecall system in all new cars by 2015. it is crucial that parliament supports the introduction of ecall. my wish is that the report should serve as a model for a legislative proposal by the commission in 2012. currently fewer than 1% of all passenger vehicles in the eu are equipped with an automatic emergency call system. the ecall device is estimated to cost less than eur 100 when new, and it could save as many as 2 500 lives a year. as shadow rapporteur on ecall, i strongly believe the system can be reachable all over europe only with mandatory deployment. the s&d group’s approach is that the eu-wide ecall service must be based on the 112 emergency number. we urge the commission to table legislation to make the ecall system compulsory by 2015. in addition, member states should actively cooperate on this issue. having free access to ecall should be a right for every eu citizen, as this public service would benefit all.
in writing. i would like to thank colleagues koch and sehnalova for the work they have done. the road safety and the speedier and more effective care of road accident victims, the protection of life, i think is a priority for all of us. i therefore welcome the mandatory introduction of the e-call system and ask the commission and the member states to create the legal and technical framework for this as soon as possible. i have also stressed in my amendments, and thanks to the vote in the committee, these amendments are now part of the core text that e-call services should be accessible to everyone. i therefore ask manufacturers and member states to take into account consumers with disabilities and special needs, including passengers, in the manufacturing and design process and to make their applications accessible to them.
mr president, six months ago i said here that we had high expectations of the danish presidency, not only because we had a presidency led by a woman but because of its ambitious agenda in a period where europe has multiple challenges to address. six months is a short period to meet such expectations, but recent developments such the outcome of last week’s european summit give us reason to believe that with the political will and the necessary unity much can be achieved. i am referring here to the investment package and the financial transactions tax. the danish presidency put investment, growth and competitiveness as priorities, and i believe that package should help meet those objectives. i would like to thank the danish government and especially minister wammen for their hard work. i would like to highlight the commitments that the danish presidency has made towards gender equality. progress has been made on some of them, such as increasing women’s participation in boardrooms. i regret, however, that despite the efforts of the presidency, no agreement could be found at the council level regarding the maternity leave directive. as prime minister thorning-schmidt underlined, it is our duty to take our countries through the crisis without abandoning our values and the european social model. let us not forget that.
it is a political success that after a long struggle they have taken the path towards building the union in the spirit of the founding fathers. it is a political failure that, contrary to the spirit of the founders, it is not offered to all citizens of the union. that's why the hungarian prime minister reported on the summit in parliament on monday: hungary won, the union succumbed to the hungarian economic policy. it is a disappointment that while the 80-year-old gyula horn of tomorrow has the strength to cut through the iron curtain in the shadow of two world systems, you are quietly assisting in the construction of a new iron curtain. it is disappointing that you, the leaders of the great powers that won the greatest political, economic change since the second world war, do not want to see the consequences. if the dream of a united europe is replaced by two world orders, the consequences could be tragic, unimaginable. our political responsibility to preserve and advance peace is universal and indivisible. that's what i want to bring to your attention after the summit.
in writing. the socialists welcome the decisions taken at the eu summit on the establishment of banking union, the intention to create monetary and political union and concrete measures to promote growth and job creation. the heads of state or government have finally shown their mettle and decided on a series of long overdue measures, which have been urged by the european parliament and, in particular, by the socialists since the crisis began. these include recognising that the fiscal consolidation must be accompanied by pro-growth economic policies that encourage investment. it is also welcome that a number of member states have voted in favour of taxing financial transactions at european level and indirectly encouraging investment to stimulate the economy. the closer economic, financial and political integration of the euro area member states must not lead to the fragmentation of the european union. it is in hungary's national interest to be part of the decision-making process that is putting the union on a new footing. the heads of state and government have finally committed themselves to crisis management. in addition to preserving the single currency, it is equally important to protect the union's fundamental values. closer political integration must be accompanied by an expansion of the powers of the community institutions, not only in the economic and financial spheres but also in the field of the functioning of democratic institutions, human rights and the rule of law. the european parliament, as the only directly elected institution of the union, is an integral part of this process.
in writing. we are right to be cautious in our assessment of last week's european council, as we thought that the eu's drift had stopped for once in december. reporters often overestimate the value of a meeting. it is crucial that the financial pact resulting from the current summit is complemented by the growth agreement, even if the amount is modest and the way it is used needs to be clarified. in the coming months we will have to deal with the issues, such as the introduction of eurobonds, which were not addressed at this summit. a decision on the introduction of a financial transaction tax at eu level should be taken. this would both create key resources for the eu budget and ensure the normal functioning of financial markets. either we will be able to create a completely new european union, or europe will be permanently marginalized in global competition. in the course of reforms, we must create political union, new democratic institutional decision-making frameworks, outside the economic and political union. it is very important that all 27 member states are involved in the reforms, and that none of them is made worse off by the growth pact. we should set up a special committee of the european parliament to consider questions relating to the future of the european union without delay. the european parliament should review the possibilities for strengthening the eu's democratic legitimacy and institutional decision-making framework. more europe, more community can only be achieved and consolidated by a whole new democratic legitimacy.

in writing. there has been considerable debate recently about some of the provisions of the anti-counterfeiting trade agreement (acta) concerning the new online piracy. many feel that these rules infringe on freedom of speech, freedom of expression, and the free flow of information. the hungarian people's party delegation believes that these citizens' and civil society concerns must be satisfactorily and reassuringly addressed, and we therefore supported the european commission's decision to ask the european court of justice to give an independent legal opinion on the compatibility of the international agreement with eu law and on the question of infringements of the right to liberty. in line with this move, the hungarian people's party group supported the european parliament delaying its decision until this opinion is delivered. according to the hungarian people's party delegation, the objections raised by numerous citizens and civil society organisations in relation to the convention should be taken into account. as there was no opportunity to clarify these, the delegation took the responsible decision to vote against the conclusion of the agreement in view of these concerns and legal uncertainty. according to the hungarian people's party delegation, it was a wrong concept to treat the fight against piracy and counterfeiting together from the outset. the delegation continues to urge action against counterfeiting. the influx of counterfeit foreign goods is putting tens of thousands of jobs at risk, and the counterfeiting of medicines is threatening the health of citizens.
officer, my fellow members of the house. congratulations to the reporter. you can hear from the previous speeches that he's got a tough job. the 2013 budget should contribute to solving europe's problems. perhaps there is complete agreement that unemployment is one of the most important of these problems, and that the decline in production is not independent of it. some people, by the way, see the bad state of the banks as the cancer of the problem, and they put the emphasis on the decline in lending, but in the midst of a number of failed bank consolidations and the various banking scandals that have broken out, the europeans would not accept that we try to find the breaking point at this point. however, chapters 1a and 1b of the european budget contain a number of programmes which are suitable for both job creation and production growth. the effectiveness of these programmes can be further enhanced if member states are flexible and do not stick to their pre-crisis programmes but carry out redirections. to make better use of the 2013 budget, member states should boldly prioritise programmes to support production and create jobs. the european commission should be more responsive in supporting such proposals. that would be real crisis management. if we dream of money that we haven't spent, and then we create new plans to spend it, then mountains are born, and gray mice are born, or not even born. instead of the myth of unspent money, the budgetary authority must face the fact that payment needs increase in the last year of the cycle and those programmes must be paid. in the hope of finding that solution, i propose that you give me this mandate.
officer, please! it's nice to see you again this year to talk about the competitiveness chapter. i would stress that since 2013 is the last year of this seven-year framework programme, it is very important that we spend our resources as efficiently as possible. i would like to make four points. we would like to see smes not cutting back on their investment, but rather strengthening this area, for example through eib support. we want the pilot project initiative of the project bond to be realised and successful, and then it could be available for similar infrastructure projects in the field of computer technology. we would like to stress, commissioner, that we welcome the iter project, but the financing of its additional costs must be managed in such a way that it does not hinder the successful implementation of other eu policies, in particular the 7th research framework programme. we solved that last year, and i hope this year they'll solve it. and i would like to emphasize that the set-plan still does not have the budgetary framework that is essential to make it happen. i think if we can make progress in these four areas, then the commissioner and i can smile at each other.
officer cioloş, my colleagues! following the excellent work of the rapporteur, mr capoulas santos, the draft will allow direct agricultural payments in the new member states to remain unchanged in 2013. we finally achieved in 2012 to equalise the level of support for the new and old member states, but this does not equalise the amount of support, and mr wojciechowski is absolutely right about that. i am very pleased that the rapporteur, mr capoulas santos, has understood the problem, which unfortunately is a big problem for the new member states, for those who have to switch from saps to sps from 2014, because they cannot take with them the benefits they have had under article 68 (1) (b) and (c) and the 'top up' under the national supplement. unfortunately, this was not included in the commission's draft, but the rapporteur, mr capoulas santos, understood this problem. the new member states, the 12 member states that are moving from saps to sps, cannot be treated in a disadvantageous way. i ask commissioner cioloş to take action also at commission level.
mr president, mr prime minister, mr commissioner, my fellow members of the house of representatives! the comments made in the last few minutes suggest that the toughest debates of the cypriot presidency will be over the long-term budget for 2014-2020. we're expecting some heated debate, as we're still a long way from an agreement. it is enough to think only of budgetary plans for cohesion policy. my fellow members of parliament! let me be perfectly clear. a substantial reduction in cohesion aid is unacceptable. it is unacceptable because cohesion policy is one of the most important pillars of european cooperation. and the principles that made it possible are more relevant today than ever. we cannot allow differences in development to undermine european cooperation. we cannot support short-term accounting considerations overriding long-term solidarity. we cannot support it because today, when the union is facing one of the most serious crises in its history, it is essential to maintain solidarity between the member states. my fellow members of parliament! the current plans for cohesion funds still contain serious imbalances and it is therefore unacceptable that support should be significantly reduced for countries such as hungary which are not expected to reach 75% of the eu's level of development by the end of the next period. however, there are other considerations which are against the withdrawal of cohesion funds. to manage the crisis, we need instruments - and i have said this many times in this house - that do not increase debt but increase jobs. cohesion policy meets both of these criteria. the protection of it would therefore be a clear success for the cypriot presidency. i therefore ask the prime minister, the commission and my colleagues to stand up for cohesion policy, to stand up for the principle of solidarity.
i have great confidence in the cypriot presidency, although its task will be extremely difficult in relation to the multiannual budget. i would like to draw your attention to a major problem, which is of particular importance for the new member states, which is the reduction of the amounts of cohesion funds for catch-up purposes. there is a particular problem that particularly affects my country: hungary is in danger of losing 20 or even 30% of its cohesion resources. it would therefore be very important for the cypriot presidency to establish a lower level of gdp in addition to the upper level. the danish presidency has made progress in building a safety net, but not enough. i therefore ask the cypriot presidency to record in a communiqué what the danish presidency has not done, namely that hungary cannot accept a reduction of cohesion funds by 20 to 30%. i need to find a fair solution for my country. thank you for your help and cooperation.
in writing. – i welcome that cyprus specifies the promotion of the european union's social agenda as a key point of its agenda and the protection of children as one of its priorities. poverty is becoming a more pressing issue in the eu and most countries, extreme poverty and deprivation are concentrated in marginalized communities, such as the roma, and children represent a large share of these populations, who are particularly vulnerable to deprivation. targeted and complex policy measures are needed to safeguard the institution of family in europe, to address the socio-demographic changes affecting family structures, to combat child poverty, and to change the employment patterns. eliminating child poverty however, is inseparable from the efforts to improve the situation of the families and we need immediate action to stop families falling behind, and rebuild our social and territorial cohesion, because it would be an unforgivable sin to loose further generations. it must be ensured that consolidation measures will not have further negative impacts on people at risk of social exclusion. marginalized communities demand a special focus, since they constitute a huge reserve of idle workforce and are disproportionately overrepresented among those living in poverty.
on behalf of the ppe group. – mr president, i am glad to see vice-president šefčovič in the chamber. the message of the epp in discussing the 2013 work programme is loud and clear: what we want to leave for the next generation of europeans is not debt, but job opportunities and the chance to go forward. as has been said, europe has been living on debts for too long. it is the easiest way to spend the money and resources of future generations. i think that we should stop doing this in europe. in order to provide prosperity for future generations europe should again be based not on debt but on work and the possibility of work. in that framework, job opportunities and the chance for the next generation to enter the labour market are among the most important measures in the commission’s work programme. but i would like to stress clearly, on behalf of the epp, that this should be the number one priority. we have to fight against youth unemployment. imagine people who are starting out in life with no prospect or possibility of working. this cannot go on. in order to provide opportunities and work for young people, we have to use all the instruments the european union has at hand, namely support for small and medium-sized enterprises, and micro-enterprises. we have to put in place – and we expect this from the commission’s legislative programme – clear targets and timetables in the areas of youth mobility, language skills and youth entrepreneurship. we have to use the full potential of the european union. we know that this is mostly the job of the member states, but the european union with its legislation has to help. we often congratulate ourselves on the freedom of movement of goods, services, persons and capital, but very often we overlook the very many obstacles which still prevent the chance to have this kind of freedom, especially in the area of services. the epp also calls for the completion of the digital single market. we have to create and enhance trust between consumers and businesses. in that area there are also many new possibilities. another of our major jobs is to reduce red tape. we should set a good example to member states but also do it at european level. we have to remove the administrative burden from citizens and businesses, as has been seen in the work programme, and our proposal is for a figure of 25% by 2015. year after year we discuss the commission’s work programme. sometimes we feel – and i come back to this every year – that it is a vain enterprise and exercise because, according to studies, normally fewer than 50% of the goals which have been set in the commission work programme appear in the next year’s commission proposals. i would propose that the committee on constitutional affairs draw up an own-initiative report on how we can enhance this figure. i am not against flexibility. especially at a time of crisis we should react quickly, but a plan is only worth anything if it is fulfilled. if it is not fulfilled, or less than half fulfilled, it is not an optimal result. in that sense i think that we have to work together in order to improve this figure. i trust in the commission being a force for stability in the institutional structures of the european union. we expect that from the commission. very often the european institutions are accused of not acting until after the event. the commission has the potential and the capacity, the administrative resources and all the instruments to lead this process and, even in times of crisis, not just to point to the future but also to be a clear point of reference and stability. on behalf of the epp, my party, and on behalf of the european parliament, i think we should support this kind of activity and enhance cooperation between these two institutions. we are not jealous if relations with other institutions like the council are also better. yesterday rebecca harms criticised the commission, and seemed to be jealous about that, but we are happy if relations between the council and the commission are good as well.
in writing. − given that the relationship between hungary and russia will determine in the long term both the economic development of the region and, if these relations are further deepened, the political stability of the region, i commend russia and support the report for its commitment to remove barriers to trade and investment and to grant more favourable treatment to service providers in 11 sectors. hungary is an eastern region of the european union, and has an interest in the agreement with russia because of its geographical location. in the framework of the trade part of the new bilateral agreement to be concluded between the european union and russia to replace the 1994 partnership and cooperation agreement, which is currently the basis for negotiations, it is appropriate to update the terms of the bilateral commitments of almost 20 years ago, in particular with a view to the russian side to take on further commitments under modes 3 and 4 of the trade in services and rights of establishment chapter. this chapter should also more strongly define the rights of european service providers established on russian territory. if these conditions are met, i can only support this report.
in writing. − hungary considers the automotive industry to be a highly important strategic sector, which is why i support this report, which is a very good reflection of the importance of this issue. in recent years, several large automotive companies have also moved their production to hungary, thus creating many jobs. for my part, i acknowledge the overall positive impact of the accession of the russian federation to the world trade organisation, as it also supports the legal provision of bilateral trade concessions. however, i regret that the russian accession package is tolerant of certain trade-distorting measures, such as the russian package of trade-related investment measures in the automotive sector, which are broadly prohibited by the wto trims agreement. these trade-related investment measures actually impose on car manufacturers factory closure in russia (which leads to discriminatory treatment of imported car parts and components and restricts imports), but link this obligation to special investment-related privileges (reduction or elimination of import duties) on the import of car parts. the maintenance of such mechanisms is detrimental to both the eu and russia. the union is at stake, with 2.3 million jobs in the automotive industry and 10.3 million indirect jobs. moreover, easier access to this market would be highly advantageous for the union producers, including hungary, as russia is a rapidly expanding market for car sales.
in writing. − i consider the agreement in the report to be a provisional agreement and look forward to its replacement by a new, sufficiently robust agreement on trade in raw materials between the eu and the russian federation. the list contained in the list, which russia interprets as not precluding the imposition of new or additional export duties on any non-listed critical raw material, the import of which is not of a material, existing or potential interest to the eu and which may be at risk of international supply difficulties, is not appropriate. however, i would stress that the language and wording of the agreement are not precise and may give rise to misunderstandings on certain issues. if russia, despite 'all efforts to its advantage', applies new or additional export duties on any of the raw materials listed in the agreement and does not accept the solutions proposed by the eu in its negotiations with the european commission, there are few tools at the eu's disposal to enforce this agreement and protect the eu's interests. russia has recently introduced a ban on animal products from all eu member states following the outbreaks of schmallenberg and bluetongue, although this ban is in clear violation of wto rules. this ban also indicates that russia's attitude has not changed much despite wto accession. i support the report because it highlights the importance of the issue.
