in writing. − as a member of the fidesz ep delegation, i also voted against the animal welfare strategy 2012-2015 in the european parliament's vote in strasbourg today. the parliament's report contains more references to the introduction of new mandatory animal welfare measures than the european commission's communication on which it is based. these are contrary to the interests of farmers, as they cause additional costs and energy consumption, thus further reducing the competitive disadvantage of european producers vis-à-vis imports, and are also detrimental to consumers, as they lead to higher food prices.
in writing. − the pp has the largest group in the european parliament and the position of our group is crucial in the votes in committee and in plenary, given its size. as vice-chair of the epp group since 2009, i have been responsible for the legislative work of the group, which includes both legislative coordination and consultation, and the development of the legislative work programme and strategy. furthermore, as 'chief whip', my duty is to ensure the integrity of the group's voting, which is reflected in the fact that i am the one who sets the position for the whole group of the people's party in the course of the votes in plenary. the aim of the people's party is to create a european unity with decisive political weight, based on common european values, traditions and culture. however, since the hungarian people's party group is actively working to promote the interests of our country, we also represent our own national interests in the european parliament, so the position of the hungarian fidesz-kdnp delegation may differ from the people's party position. in order to avoid any contradiction with my duties as vice-president, i shall not take part in the vote directly in cases where the group votes differently from the hungarian members in the roll-call votes. i do not press the ballot button, but i do raise my hand to indicate to the pp group the voting position that we had previously agreed on.
in writing. today, the debate is once again about the 'community hen egg', which is the eu's most important achievement in the eyes of european citizens, not to mention young people, nearly 90% of whom vote for the importance of free movement, according to a survey by the european youth parliament. the value of this acquis communautaire is being undermined by the practice of the old member states of increasingly regular checks on passengers arriving from the new member states, on buses. in the midst of the economic and financial crisis, when the need for community confidence is becoming more urgent, some member states are reinforcing mistrust. which shakes the foundations of the system. we saw the same loss of confidence in the council decision in early june. i therefore ask the council to do everything possible to ensure that the deadlock between parliament and the council is resolved as quickly as possible and that we can develop the governance of schengen together. it is also to welcome with confidence the two new accession countries, romania and bulgaria, to the schengen area in september.
in writing. the draft amendment requested by the court is intended, as in the case of the hungarian judicial reform, to improve the efficiency of the functioning of the bodies constituting the court of justice and, consequently, to reduce the duration of proceedings. as in our country, this is not a one-off change, but rather a way of working with flexibility to meet the challenges of the future. the amendments concern primarily the composition of the grand chamber, the creation of the office of vice-president of the court of justice, the increase in the number of judges at the general court from 27 to 39 and the possibility of making use of alternate judges at the civil service tribunal. we all recognise the urgent need for a structural solution to reduce rapidly the number of cases currently pending before the general court and to limit the time taken to deal with them. in order to ensure the efficient functioning of the judiciary, some amendments should enter into force as soon as possible, even before the partial renewal in the autumn.
mr president, mr commissioner, and my fellow members of parliament. the un conference on sustainable development was expected by many - including the members of the european parliament - to be far more than the scale of the problem to be solved. the alarming consequences of unsustainable exploitation of our planet are now well known, and the technology and knowledge to reverse the processes are available. unfortunately, political will is still lacking, and action often stalls. the implementation of the ambitious goals agreed at rio remains therefore our shared responsibility. in this respect, there is still much work to be done. speaking at the rio+20 conference, mr president of the republic áder jános, who represented hungary and was my predecessor here in the european parliament, said that sustainable development is about us and our children, about our lives and our future prospects. it is common knowledge that water is the source of life, the greatest treasure of mankind, yet it is shocking that the united nations predicts that by 2025, two-thirds of the world's population will be facing persistent water problems. that's a shocker, please. imagine, my fellow members, a situation where 10 to 100 million children will not have access to safe drinking water, and as a result, child mortality rates will continue to worsen on this earth. we can't afford to be so irresponsible. i feel that politicians and business leaders need to take immediate action in this area. shockingly, half the world's hospital beds are now occupied by people who are sick from poor drinking water, and nearly two million children under the age of five are victims of drinking water and wastewater problems every year. and we haven't talked about the people, especially in the third world, who can't even get to the hospital to get any kind of hospital care. they die without being counted in the statistics. the european union has long recognised the strategic importance of water and is at the forefront of the path towards sustainable water management. however, the union alone is not enough to solve the global water problems on its own. it is therefore in our vital interest to have effective international cooperation on water policy before the lack of it can trigger waves of migration and threaten us with wars over water. i want you to think, my fellow congressmen, of george miller's 1979 film, mad max. i don't want my kids growing up in a world like that, so i feel like it's a last-minute thing, and everybody has to act.
mr president, when assessing the outcome of the rio+20 conference on sustainable development, there is not much place for optimism. it is more than clear that the austerity measures implied by the current economic crisis are being matched by a recession in human rights and ambitious environmental policies. the outcome document does recognise the importance of the green economy and stresses a number of vital issues, such as the need to improve gender equality and engage civil society. however, the overall commitments are worryingly minimal and disappointing, not least in terms of lacking a timeframe for the implementation of the objectives. in this respect, the outcome document can hardly be seen as an instrument for the achievement of a green and socially inclusive economy and for growth. moreover, it is highly regrettable that while the participating countries recognise the need to integrate reproductive health into national strategies, the language of reproductive rights was not expressed in the outcome document. in short, there is some limited added value in the 700-plus commitments voiced at the conference but they do not translate into a concrete action plan.
in writing. the rio+20 summit has been highlighted by the various manifestations of the situation worldwide, including growing social discontent and global climate change. the high-level conference was expected to address the most pressing global challenges and develop concrete strategies to address challenges that have eluded us for the past two decades. however, despite the efforts of thousands of civil society organisations, there is little hope of a political implementation of the summit declaration. in my view, the so-called 'the future we want' final document merely restated previous agreements, but omitted the formulation of new objectives and a commitment to those objectives. it includes short-term plans at the national level, but it does not take into account the limits of the planet's sustainability. although the document is a political resolution on sustainability, it is dominated by the paradigm of economic growth. i myself believe that this change is necessary at all levels, applying the principle of subsidiarity. however, the role of global and international decision-making is vital to bring about the necessary change and to develop appropriate responses to the growing economic, social and environmental challenges. the ep is also calling for a reduction in the use of all types of raw materials. i believe that this is the only way to effectively reduce today's wasteful consumption and to mitigate the impacts of unsustainable resource use on both nature and human communities.
in writing. − the court of justice of the european union has requested an amendment to its statute because it considers that urgent structural reform is needed to deal with the extraordinary workload it faces and to limit the time it takes to hear cases. the court is aware that a significant number of cases currently pending before the general court require urgent resolution. similar to the recent judicial reform in hungary, the draft amendments requested by the court are intended to improve the efficiency of the court's functioning and to reduce the length of proceedings. it is not enough to stem the tide of new cases, but strong action is also needed to address the backlog of pending cases. the court considers that the necessary efficiency and consistency in the court's case-law can only be urgently ensured by an increase in the number of judges. there is a general consensus among the institutions on the need for these amendments. however, while there is agreement in principle on increasing the number of members of the general court, the method of their appointment has not yet been defined. given that the council was unable to reach a political agreement on the distribution of the 12 newly created judicial seats among the member states, the relevant part was omitted from the regulation, thus allowing the remaining points of the reform to be adopted and the organisational changes to take effect before the partial structural change in the autumn.
in writing. − the civil service tribunal has only seven judges, and its functioning is therefore seriously affected if one of its members becomes ill and is unable to perform his duties for a prolonged period. the court therefore proposes that the statute provide for the possibility of supplementing the civil service tribunal with substitute judges whose assistance could be called upon in the event of the permanent incapacity of a judge. in cases where a judge is prevented from attending the hearing of cases for more than three months for reasons of ill-health, the judge concerned is not in a state of total incapacity. in order to ensure that the european civil service tribunal is able to carry out its tasks with the greatest possible efficiency at all times, it has become necessary to amend the statute of the court of justice. the use of substitutes is a reasonable solution to a practical problem which can cause considerable disruption to the work of the civil service tribunal. the proposed amendments are purely technical in nature but can significantly contribute to the provision of procedural guarantees in the case-law of the court of justice.
in writing. − i supported the report because the proposed instrument offers an opportunity to mobilise important investment instruments for transport, telecommunications and energy infrastructure projects, which are needed for growth, innovation, competitiveness and job creation. the implementation of the planned projects will make the benefits of european integration tangible to citizens, thus strengthening the european idea. however, this instrument also has risks, mainly arising from the assumption of losses that may be incurred in the planned investment projects. while the european commission's legislative proposal clearly sets out the maximum amount of possible risk-taking for the eu budget, the european investment bank (eib) has a different situation: the fact that the eib and investors enter into contracts for each project and that the eib prepares a risk-sharing plan for all projects implies that the eib does not have to take on any additional risks. the eib should therefore avoid any adverse impact on the eib's creditworthiness and reputation, as well as on the design and implementation of projects traditionally supported by the eib, from the emergence of specific risks.
madam president, madam commissioner, i have to ask you to leave. first of all, i want to congratulate the speaker, because he did a great job, and he successfully closed this very long process. i'm all for developing standards, but i'm all for speeding them up. however, i would like to point out that this process can in no way be at the expense of quality. i therefore believe that the main objective should be to speed up the administrative part of the process, which requires measures to be implemented jointly by the standard-setting body and the commission, while maintaining, of course, the public consensus and the public interest. i believe that the commission can do a lot to achieve this, for example by speeding up the start and finish phases of work, including the signing of mandates and contracts, the certification of performance and the facilitation of payments. i agree with the speaker that we can and should build on the existing system, and there are many opportunities for improvement within it. i agree that the problem of access to standards cannot be solved by the question of the cost of standards, and i also welcomed the fact that we had made a call to the national standards board. in other words, to reduce costs by means of special charges, for example by offering standard packages at a reduced price. i support and consider it very important to involve smes and other stakeholder organisations in the standardisation process. i just want to say, in closing, that there has never been a more significant obstacle to your participation. i see the reason for their passivity in other things, such as their very limited financial resources, and i also believe that their special expertise is sometimes lacking. i believe that we should focus on these areas and find ways of increasing their motivation. overall, the rapporteur has succeeded in developing a proposal that can also support europe's competitiveness, innovation and sustainable development. in addition to all this, and because of the great work that has been done, i recommend that the report be adopted, and i myself support it.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the hungarian government introduced daily physical education in the first and fourth grades of primary schools and in the fifth grade as well, starting from 2 september 2012. that may not sound like much of a success at first. i have to say that my own personal political career, over eight years, has been devoted to highlighting the importance of this area. and this was to be taken seriously because only 9% of the hungarian population exercises regularly, which is the last percentage in the european union. even more shocking, only 6 percent of children exercise regularly. this means that our children's future is at stake, for they may grow up to have serious health problems because of a lack of regular exercise. to be able to run this process and to serve as a model for the european union, we need the help of the european union, as we are missing 3-400 gyms, which we expect to build with the support of the union and our fellow representatives.

i congratulate mr turmes and would like to mention the example of hungary. that the previous socialist-liberal government helped renovate 280 thousand panel houses in four years, mostly from eu funds, from the hungarian state-self-government part, and citizens had to contribute only 25-30% to improve the energy efficiency of panel houses. and this shows, this is a concrete example, that energy efficiency is the best way to go. i agree with both commissioner oettinger and the rapporteur. it's a pity the current government didn't follow through. i ask mr oettinger and the house to allocate resources for this purpose between 2014 and 2020 to improve energy efficiency .
in writing. among the eu's 20-20-20 targets, the energy efficiency target is currently the most lagging behind. many initiatives are already moving in the right direction, but the results still dwarf the untapped potential. europe recognised its backwardness and realised that, in the midst of the economic crisis, it could not afford the luxury of neglecting energy efficiency. these investments not only reduce our energy bills, but they immediately boost our economy. however, our energy efficiency targets in the directive will remain noisy targets until the funding issues are resolved. often the problem is not with the determination but with obtaining the necessary funds for the renovation and the preparation of the self-part. i therefore consider it a great step forward that an innovative financing instrument has been included in the directive as a hungarian initiative. the proposal allows the state to sell excess allowances from non-industrial emitters and use the proceeds to achieve energy efficiency objectives. the hungarian proposal provides for the possibility of using the proceeds from the sale of allowances to replace the own contribution to energy efficiency programmes. thus, despite the economic crisis, a large number of investments can be made that achieve spectacular energy savings.
in writing. i believe that it is of the utmost importance for the future of europe that we make significant progress on energy efficiency in the period up to 2020. in times of crisis such as the present, the promotion of energy efficiency is of key interest to europe, as billions of euros could be saved on energy imports by reducing external energy dependence, not to mention increasing energy security, which has always been a priority for the sector. this agreement commits member states to renew 3% of central government buildings annually, in addition to the 20% efficiency target by 2020, and sets a minimum annual energy savings of 1,5% for the union in energy savings bills. the advantage of the agreement is that it addresses the issue of energy efficiency in several ways, so that its impact can be felt in both the public and private sectors. as a socialist representative, i consider it particularly important to stress that the implementation of the project would help to create new jobs and encourage invoicing based on real consumption, thus promoting the interests of disadvantaged groups. in the interests of hungary, i wholeheartedly support the objective of the draft directive to encourage member states to pay more attention to the operation of heating and air-conditioning systems in buildings, since in hungary 1/5 of the population still lives in panel dwellings, where this problem is particularly acute. in my view, if we can make real progress on energy efficiency in the near future, europe's recovery from the crisis can be significantly accelerated.
i voted in favour of the report, which essentially exposes the rampage that the united states is carrying out against fundamental human rights in the war on terrorism, involving many european countries. i have had experience of how this can lead to bizarre and frightening results, including in hungary: the budaházy case, which i have already mentioned here. budaházy is a well-known opposition leader in hungary. he has been the subject of criminal proceedings for more than four years for a gross violation of the fundamental procedural guarantees. he spent two and a half years in pre-trial detention, which the european court of human rights in strasbourg found to be unlawful and the hungarian state is obliged to pay compensation to budaházy. despite this, they continue to house arrest this opposition figure. and in reference to what? the threat of terrorism! of course, we must reject this by a wide margin and put an end to the hypocrisy.
in writing. − in view of the rapidly evolving technologies and innovation, it is particularly important to establish uniform standards at european level. in order to ensure the competitiveness of the european union and the placing of safe products on the market, uniform standards should be valid and applicable in all member states. i agree with the need to speed up the development of standards, but it must be a fundamental consideration that this should not in any way be at the expense of quality. the main objective should be to speed up the administrative part of the process, with measures to be implemented jointly by standard-setters and the commission. european standardisation also has great potential for supporting legislation and public policies. i appreciate that the report recognises this opportunity and stresses the need to move towards new areas of standards development such as services. as mrs comi has pointed out, the new european model must promote european innovation and sustainable development, which i fully agree with. standardization, complemented by the principle of mutual recognition, as we have seen, plays a central role in the proper functioning of the internal market. it also contributes to the protection of the health and safety of european consumers and to the protection of the environment. it is therefore important that we support this report, which i did by voting in favour of it.
in writing. − i was pleased to vote in favour of the report, as this proposal is a good example of how much criticised eu legislation can respond quickly to the challenges of practical life, especially when it comes to protecting public health. in response to the case, the european commission immediately identified remaining weaknesses in pharmacovigilance and proposed the necessary amendments, which were agreed by the council and parliament and could therefore enter into force as early as 2013.
in writing. − while emissions from land-based sources, such as industrial emissions and those from road transport, have been successfully reduced, the increasing pollution from maritime transport cannot be left out of eu regulation. as the reduction of sulphur in marine fuels is clearly a matter of public health concern throughout europe, i consider it very important, as a representative of a landlocked country, that stricter limits be set by the directive. however, i also voted in favour of the report because it creates a level playing field by applying uniform air quality standards in the union for both land and sea pollution.
in writing. − the current state of harmonisation of european legislation shows that there are significant differences between national administrative procedures and national data protection laws. in my view, the internal market information system (imi) should therefore be designed in such a way that when users of all 27 member states exchange personal data through it, they comply with their national legislation, including national data protection legislation. however, data subjects should also be assured that their data will be protected consistently, regardless of whether the data are transmitted to other member states through imi. ensuring consistency while respecting diversity is a key challenge for the deployment of the technical and legal infrastructure of imi. however, unnecessary complexity and fragmentation should be avoided. overall, i welcome the procedure laid down by mr bielan as it clearly defines the scope of imi and ensures transparency, while allowing flexibility in cases where imi is used for further exchanges of information in the future. i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. − the european parliament voted today by a large majority on this report, which clearly regulates the common system of taxation of interest and royalty payments, minimising the possibility of tax evasion, tax fraud and double non-taxation. the adoption of the document, which i voted in favour of, will allow member states to make substantial revenue, which has been lost, by reducing the potential for certain tax evasion activities. the parliament's report adopted today on the revision of the council directive on a common system of taxation applicable to interest and royalty payments aims to regulate the system of interest and royalty payments applicable to associated enterprises in different member states, while at the same time increasing clarity and accountability.
in writing. − the parliament's report on the revision of the council directive on a common system of taxation applicable to interest and royalty payments, adopted today, aims to regulate the system of interest and royalty payments applicable to associated enterprises in different member states, while increasing clarity and accountability. it is important to note, however, that the decisions of the member states on the taxation issue remain decisive and the principle of subsidiarity remains valid. however, for complex issues, even those involving several member states, such as interest and royalty payments, a european framework is needed to ensure that tax payments are made in a transparent manner and within a regulated framework, on the basis of uniform criteria and commonly agreed criteria. the report therefore addresses important issues and aims to further reduce the potential for tax avoidance, double non-taxation and tax evasion, which is why it should be supported.
in writing. − the european parliament adopted a motion for a resolution on the european commission's work programme for 2013 at today's plenary session. the text of the motion fully reflects the objectives of the pp group, and thus focuses on programmes to stimulate growth and create jobs. these objectives are in line with the measures taken by the hungarian government over the last two years. the re-launch of europe's economy will depend to a large extent on the future direction of the european commission's activities, which the european parliament can influence by defining the european commission's annual legislative programme. the resolution calls on the commission to take decisive action to recover from the crisis as soon as possible, identifying in a separate annex the sectors where progress is urgently needed. the resolution stresses that the commission should pay even more attention to legislative initiatives to promote job creation in the coming year. i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution.
in writing. − i support the adoption of the resolution on the preparation of the european commission's work programme for 2013. the commission is called upon to take the measures set out in the proposal to recover from the crisis as quickly as possible, by specifying in a separate annex the sectors where progress is urgently needed. we do not insist that the legislative initiatives to promote job creation should be at the top of the commission's legislative programme for next year. we have a responsibility to future generations. let us not leave a legacy of debt to future generations, but let us create a europe of work for them. we therefore call on the commission to give priority to the fight against youth unemployment and to help by all means to kick-start economic growth. these objectives are in line with the measures taken by the hungarian government over the last two years.
in writing. − the report looks at current trends in voluntary and unpaid tissue and cell donation in the member states and makes recommendations to improve the quality of life of people across europe and to increase the flow of donations between member states. it states as a principle that donations should be voluntary and be made on a non-profit basis in a transparent environment. the report gives priority to the donation of cord blood for the extraction of stem cells, as there is growing evidence that these stem cells can be used to treat diseases, including childhood diseases. it notes, however, that despite this, only 1% of births in the eu are stored with stem cells derived from umbilical cord blood. the report therefore encourages mothers to donate cord blood and tissues at birth to public or private banks; urges information campaigns; expects the establishment of at least one public cord blood bank per member state; encourages close cooperation between private and public cell banks to increase the availability and exchange of cord blood and tissues at both national and eu level; and stresses the need to define and enforce eu-level standards for both private and public cell banks. for all these reasons, i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − in the european union, there are 14.6 million jobs directly or indirectly linked to the protection of biodiversity and the restoration of natural resources and forests. women can be among the top winners of a greener and more socially inclusive economy. they will have easier access to job opportunities (e.g. in renewable energy) and higher incomes, and will be able to legally work. in industrialized countries, only 8 to 12 percent of the workforce is employed in the 10 to 15 industries that account for 70 to 80 percent of carbon emissions. only a fraction of these people are likely to lose their jobs if they embrace policies to green existing businesses and promote employment. i consider the objectives set out in the report to be extremely important and have therefore voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − eco-industry and environment-related industries – a significant and quickly growing portion of the eu economy currently representing 2.1% of eu gdp – offer an important and continuously expanding opportunity to enhance labour market participation, both in high-skill and low-skill sectors. eco-industries and related services – including air pollution control, waste water management, solid waste management, soil remediation, renewable energies, and recycling – sustain 3.5 million full-time jobs at present and around 75%. unfortunately there is a disturbing lack of reliable, comprehensive and comparable data on how the green economy influences the labour market, especially from the aspect of its impact on equal opportunities and equality. the european commission and national statistical authorities must therefore develop a proper disaggregated database and set of indicators to measure the actual and potential impact of the green economy on territorial and social cohesion. furthermore, a set of strategic recommendations and policy instruments needs also to be defined in order to effectively respond to the possible changes in the level and/or structure of employment.
in writing. − more than four-fifths of women in the eu labour market find work in the service sector, and quite surprisingly – mostly due to widespread stereotypes concerning what is considered to be a feminine job, or an occupation suitable for women – more than half of them find an occupation within less than 8% of all available professions. similarly to the whole of the labour market, most of them face a gender pay gap, difficulties in reconciling work with family life, and the glass ceiling blocking their advancement. unfortunately, precarious work is a persistent feature of the european union's labour market and women represent a great proportion of undeclared workers who are mainly engaged in domestic work and care. the european commission and the member states need to develop comprehensive and effective strategies in order to combat precarious employment as well as to support the development of the formal domestic and care services sector.
in writing. − the achievement of the knowledge economy objectives is jeopardised if the restrictions imposed in response to the economic crisis have a significant impact on education and training systems. the quality of funding is compromised by the fragmentation of funding at both member state and union level. if we invest more in quality early childhood education, it will reduce costs in the further stages of education. i voted for the report because i recognize the priority of education policy. i support the european harmonisation of the dual vocational training system, and i believe that practical training should be given a greater role in higher education. the report draws attention to the need to encourage learners and teachers in vocational education and training systems to make more effective use of the opportunities offered by mobility. a stable european economy requires quality work and european professionals with complex knowledge. this requires the removal of obstacles to mobility, which are still a problem in the european education and training area.
in writing. − i welcome the commission's intention to strengthen cooperation in education and training in the 'education and training 2020' strategy work cycle (2012-2012) in order to successfully implement the europe 2020 strategy. i welcome the strengthening of the fight against early school leaving, the implementation of lifelong learning, and the efforts to improve transnational mobility and the european skills base. it is important to mention, however, that there are areas which, in my opinion, do not receive enough attention, but their importance is not in dispute. these include language learning/language proficiency, which is one of the biggest potential obstacles to being able to work anywhere in the european union. language skills are useful in general, but multilingualism is a vital part of how we operate in the european union. ensuring universal access to quality early childhood education is also vital for the future of europe and can contribute to the achievement of the two priority objectives of the europe 2020 strategy: to contribute to reducing the school drop-out rate below 10% and to help at least 20 million people to move out of poverty and social exclusion.
in writing. − as the recent report of eu education ministers and the commission points out, more efforts are necessary for reaching the eu2020 headline target for tackling early school leaving (esl). the absence of early childhood education has been identified by several studies as one of the main reasons behind esl and, despite the recommendation of the european commission network on childcare to invest at least 1% of gdps on early education, the vast majority of eu member states still lag behind this level and the most vulnerable are most likely to be left out, or to enrol late and, even if they attend pre-school, the necessary services appropriate for their age and development are not secured. in the most disadvantaged micro-regions there is no pre-school service available, despite the usually high number of children and the fact that early childhood development plays an essential role in developing the necessary cognitive, emotional, social, and physical attributes (the absence of which increases the risk of esl and low educational performance), or option of redirecting disadvantaged children into special schools. poor performing pupils are also much more likely to fall victim to high risk behaviours, such as drug, criminality, or early parenthood.
in writing. − the report by mr cavada is an important milestone in the development of the european audiovisual sector. the internet is now the number one source of information for young people, reaching out to those who are interested as a new channel. the potential of digital recording also holds inestimable value for culture. i supported the report because it comprehensively addresses the problems of the legal framework for the audiovisual sector, the need for the legal offering of works, the opportunities for online use in their distribution and the dangers of piracy. he's a supporter of cultural diversity, which i've often stressed. i think that one of the important points of the report is that it emphasises the need to promote media literacy, as the media have a great social responsibility. on the other hand, i support the idea that public archives should take advantage of the tools of digitisation, thereby also ensuring the widest possible access to these cultural treasures.

in writing. i welcome this extremely thorough and comprehensive report. i would like to make two minor additional remarks on the sections dealing with security policy. the objective of promoting the fight against terrorism and organised crime by all means at its disposal should be given greater weight in the strategic and budgetary priorities. an important condition for this action is close and continuous cooperation between the external and internal security agencies, which leaves a legacy on the external security side. my second comment concerns the relationship with russia. the report rightly states that a condition for a good partnership with russia is respect for human rights in russia. however, i did not include in the report the demand that the russian state should not only help verbally but also in practice in the fight against russian and russian-speaking organized crime. i can give you an example of russian cybercrime, which is now operating as an international mafia. many analysts believe that russian and russian-speaking cybercriminals are in some cases receiving covert support from russian state bodies. i believe that one of the essential conditions for a partnership with russia is that we should see more sincere and effective cooperation from russia in law enforcement.

in writing. – i would like to congratulate the rapporteurs of this very important report reinforcing the need to adopt a comprehensive european legal framework offering all victims of crime the widest protection within the territory of the eu. this framework must follow a victim-centred approach – meaning that all categories of potential victims must be identified, targeted and protected, with special attention to children and other groups at risk – and must also cover related policies of social affairs and social inclusion, as well as effective methods for the social rehabilitation of victims, including measures related to social security systems and the labour market. specific measures are necessary in order to prevent extortion and retaliation under the full assurance of anonymity, paying special attention to the situation of victims with specific needs. awareness-raising campaigns must be organised involving civil and non-governmental organisations, women's organisations and the media, and it is also necessary to conduct detailed researches on the latest criminal trends, with special focus on the involvement of at-risk groups analysing all the factors that lead to the victimisation of such groups.

in my view, a collective sense of belonging to europe is one of the foundations of the union's stability. one of the symbolic expressions of this sense of belonging is europe's cultural heritage, which includes the artistic works of all the member states. works whose copyright holders cannot be identified are also included in this category, i.e. orphan works. ensuring that such works are made available to the public throughout europe and harmonising the different legal practices in the member states is essential to safeguard europe's cultural heritage. i think it's unacceptable for a work to be lost because there is no person or rights management organization that would authorize its use as a copyright holder. that is why i support the proposal that works whose copyright owners cannot be identified despite a long and careful search can be digitised and thus made available online throughout europe.
in writing. the great advantage of this agreement is that it provides legal certainty to public institutions, which will then be more empowered to take up these works. i welcome the fact that this proposal also includes the possibility for rightholders to request that their works not be regarded as works, while at the same time ensuring that public institutions are also adequately protected. i think the reason they don't use these works is because they're afraid that they're going to have to pay a million-dollar fine without the copyright owner's consent. it is not uncommon for an institution to have 70% of its collection made up of such works. i would also like to point out that i personally did not consider the legal policy approach of the european commission to be constructive, but rather a partly negative reaction to the book digitisation project launched by google. in this way, the model envisaged by the directive would certainly be able to compete effectively with the us company in the short and medium term, but in the long term it would again be pushed into the background by the failure to develop a regulatory environment that would allow creative uses in the digital environment. i believe that the rapporteur recognises this possibility too: he has steered the original proposal in the right direction, stressing that we need to move into new areas in the legislative field as well. as mrs geringer de oedenberg points out, the new european model should help to develop a simplified procedure. i couldn't agree more.
mr president, first of all let me thank the rapporteurs. well done! as pointed out in this report, the modified legislative environment introduced by the treaty of lisbon needs to be better reflected in european legislation, as provided for in the smart regulation agenda. better lawmaking means simplified, smarter and more transparent legislation, taking into account europe’s multilayered governance. in that regard, i believe the closer involvement of national and regional parliaments in our legislative process needs to be reinforced, especially in the light of the principle of subsidiarity. we have at our disposal very useful tools in relation to national contributions to eu law, such as the ipex platform. however, the follow-up and the ex-post control could and should be enhanced: firstly through an improved statement from the commission of the ground for its initiative (subsidiarity or proportionality), and secondly through an extended timeframe for national parliaments to carry out subsidiarity checks. finally, i would like to underline the urgent need for an update of the 2003 interinstitutional agreement on better lawmaking, and i look forward to discussing the upcoming commission progress report on the smart regulation agenda.
in writing. the interinstitutional agreement on better law-making adopted in 2003 should be adapted as soon as possible to the new legislative environment created by the treaty of lisbon, in particular as regards the tables of conformity and the demarcation line between delegated and implementing acts. we cannot allow the council to blur the clear distinction between delegated and implementing acts and we urge the commission to ensure the proper application of these two articles. the agreement between the three institutions is aimed primarily at enhancing the coordination of the legislative process, while respecting the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality. i would like to point out that, in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, the union acts in areas outside its exclusive competence only when and to the extent that the objectives of the proposed action are better achieved at union level than at national, regional or local level. it is therefore essential that the community institutions enable national parliaments to scrutinise legislative proposals by ensuring that the commission provides comprehensive and detailed reasons for its decisions on subsidiarity and proportionality.

in writing. − the aim of this agreement is to guarantee the equivalence of the treatment of classified data by all eu institutions. transparency and access to information are essential prerequisites for the democratic legitimacy of the eu, namely the european parliament. the framework agreement between parliament and the commission contains detailed provisions on the transmission of confidential information to parliament. however, in order to make effective use of the prerogatives and powers of the european parliament, which have been considerably extended by the treaty of lisbon, parliament also needs access to classified council data. i am voting in favour of the adoption of the agreement because it lays down the rules governing the transmission to the european parliament and the handling of classified information by the council relating to matters other than the common foreign and security policy which are relevant to the exercise of the european parliament's powers and duties.
in writing. − the pillars of the atlantic strategy: spatial planning policy and industrial policy will lead to the development of a macro-regional strategy, which will involve local authorities, public and private actors, eu organisations, transnational and international actors, to develop a complex, integrated and effective strategy, the implementation of which will lead to a comprehensive innovation of the atlantic region (including the channel and the coastal regions of the irish sea) from 2014 onwards, with particular regard to the development of smes, the employment of young people and the boost to transatlantic transport. the atlantic is a dynamic maritime area, the western gateway to the european union, and therefore of strategic importance for transatlantic traffic. it is also a peripheral region of the union, which, if removed, could link the atlantic regions to europe, transport, energy and information networks, regional development and the strengthening of land-sea links.
in writing. − the european parliament today took an important step towards strengthening the position of eu member states vis-à-vis large energy exporters and reducing their vulnerability. the document adopted today in plenary calls for member states to share more information on intergovernmental agreements in the field of energy supply in the future. the exchange of information facilitates the coordination of action by eu governments vis-à-vis the major energy exporters, thereby allowing member states to access energy on better terms. most eu member states ensure their long-term energy supply through intergovernmental agreements with third countries. but when it comes to energy supply negotiations, the european union of 500 million people is not the world's largest energy market. the security of energy supply and the lower price of energy in the union are guaranteed by closer cooperation and joint action between member states. knowledge of existing intergovernmental agreements and coordination of negotiation strategies will strengthen the position of member states vis-à-vis the major energy exporters, allowing them to obtain energy at better prices. strengthening the negotiating position of the member states is also an issue for hungary, as our long-term gas contract expires in 2015. it is crucial for the security of our energy supply and the competitiveness of our industry that we succeed in extending the gas contract on favorable terms. today's decision strengthens our country's negotiating position.
in writing. − a more efficient exchange of information between member states is important for eu countries to negotiate more successfully with major energy exporters. knowledge of existing intergovernmental agreements helps to coordinate the actions of eu governments, so that member states can access energy on better terms. the agreement at first reading voted on today is an important step towards harmonising negotiating strategies and strengthening member states' positions. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament and the council should be given the opportunity to discuss the future of the eu's external relations. the early strengthening of the member states' negotiating position is crucial for the union's energy supply and competitiveness, and i therefore voted in favour of approving the agreement between the ep and the council and setting up the legislative framework immediately.
in writing. − social enterprises have become increasingly important in economic and cohesion policies in recent years. different organisations have made and promised many different initiatives. i believe that these businesses can play a key role as drivers of social innovation. the experience of social cooperatives in bringing people into employment is a clear example of this, but beyond the legal form, social innovation also comes from new forms of service provision, the creation of new innovative products and services that meet new social needs. the european institutions must therefore act consistently and coordinate actions on social enterprises, innovation and social change. i believe that social enterprises should be supported, firstly because they can introduce new methods of delivering services and measures that improve people's quality of life, and secondly because they help to create new products that meet new social needs. it is also important to promote a more 'subsidiarity-friendly' attitude of public institutions, to introduce policies with targeted incentives and to implement the initiatives of business associations, which have proved to be essential for the growth of social enterprises. i therefore voted in favour of this report, as overall the proposals contained in it could improve access to finance and the regulatory framework.
in writing. − the adoption of the report will make it easier for venture capital funds to raise funds for start-ups across europe. the approach is simple: if they meet the requirements, each of the qualifying fund managers can raise funds under the european venture capital fund designation throughout the eu. they will no longer have to meet complex requirements that differ from one member state to another. the uniform rules will potentially allow venture capital funds to raise more capital and to grow. larger venture capital funds mean a larger volume of capital available to individual companies and allow the funds to specialise in stand-alone sectors such as information technology, biotechnology or healthcare. this will ultimately help smes to improve their competitiveness on the world market.
in writing. − a prior authorisation is required for copyrighted works to be made publicly available in online digital libraries. consequently, the use of so-called orphan works, i.e. where the identity or whereabouts of the copyright holder is unknown, is almost impossible, since the necessary authorisation for publication cannot be obtained in the absence of the copyright holder. at present, the member states of the european union can only digitise works whose copyright holders cannot be identified within their own territory. i am voting in favour of the report, so that we can finally establish a basis for the treatment of books, newspapers, magazines, films, music, television works which are protected by copyright but whose owners cannot be identified. this will allow such orphan works to be made available online throughout europe for cultural and educational purposes without prior authorisation.
in writing. − we expect accountability for the euro area's common banking supervision. that is what is said in the resolution which i myself voted for on thursday. the document calls on member states to guarantee the democratic accountability of the proposed single european banking supervision by means of 'quality rules', but states that, based on the current views of member states, it is feared that the actual rules will send a 'yearly message' and perpetuate the inefficiency. i hope for a system that stabilises the euro area, because that is in our interest, but without making the situation worse for banks outside the euro area. unfortunately, some measures that strengthen the stability of the euro area (such as the divestment of eastern subsidiaries to allow more credit to be made at home) may have a sensitive impact on non-euro area members. it is therefore also important to stress that the resolution stresses the importance of developing a mechanism to address the spillover effects of the establishment of the euro area banking union on the eu member states that are not members of the euro area. i believe that the only good system is one which deals with this extremely difficult situation by applying uniform rules in a single european union, which creates equal rights and obligations for those inside and outside the area.

author. - mr president, it must be the lady. the story of today's debate actually has three actors: azerbaijan, armenia and hungary, and it moves on two tracks: legal and political. of the three actors, hungary played a marginal role, as it only followed international legal norms. in light of this, i find the left amendments incomprehensible, because they are heating up a frozen conflict to condemn hungary. it's a well-known left-wing bulldozing technique. azerbaijan, by contrast, is clearly violating the spirit of the rules by immediately pardoning the transferred safarov. it is not surprising that armenia is attacking azerbaijan, but the anti-hungarian outbursts are a sign of a heightened emotional state. this is worrying because an overzealous armenian reaction and a dubious move by azerbaijan could put the already fragile security of the region at risk. on behalf of the s&d group. - mr president! my fellow members of parliament! according to the hungarian government, these are legal acts between two sovereign countries. but if that's the case, why did the people's party take the initiative to discuss urgent humanitarian issues here in the strasbourg parliament? since the people's party took this initiative, the socialists disagreed. murder, please, is murder. the verdict is the verdict. in the case of transfers between two countries, it is a common responsibility to ensure this on the basis of mutual guarantees. that's the responsibility we're looking for. international organisations, including the european parliament, need to do a lot of work to build trust in the region and bring it back to the level it was before the incident. there is no alternative to a peaceful, negotiated process in the region. i sincerely hope that bilateral relations will return to normal as soon as possible.
mr president, punish or extradite? this is the fundamental principle of international criminal law in matters of mutual cooperation in criminal justice matters between states. in this case, hungary used a combination of these two. we enforced eight years of mr safarov’s sentence and then extradited him – at the request of azerbaijan – and allowed his transfer to azerbaijan. as a criminal lawyer i think there should have been more careful consideration in terms of making sure that azerbaijan did not use article 12 of the relevant european convention and did not grant a pardon or amnesty to safarov. as a hungarian criminal lawyer i am not proud of this particular decision, and i think there is room for improvement. having said that, the european union is not a role model for us. the european union should not instruct us in matters of international cooperation in criminal cases when there is a shameful, shocking case pending in the european union in which we are on the receiving end of a miscarriage of justice, and you had better solve that case before you instruct us. it is the tobin case. have you ever heard about this case? if not, that is really sad; you should have heard about it. an irish banker in hungary killed two hungarian children in an extreme case of reckless driving. he was sentence to three years’ imprisonment by a hungarian court. he is now in ireland, in his country of citizenship, and do you know what? ireland refuses both to extradite him to hungary and to enforce his sentence. what does the european union do? it sits back and keeps silent. why is it that when we are on the receiving end of these miscarriages of justice you never raise your voices? ms hall said earlier this afternoon that there should be no cherry picking in matters of human rights. please use this principle when hungary is on the agenda. on behalf of the s&d group. the aim of the year '13 is to draw attention to the rights of european citizens, the rights they have. but the year will only be a success if european citizens see that they can really exercise their rights and that the european institutions take their own principles and values seriously. can citizens really be free to enjoy the rights to work in another country with the recognition of their qualifications? does the right to non-discrimination really apply to everyone, including those who seek to thrive as roma in another member state? does the european union take the guarantee of democratic rights seriously and does it speak up when democracy is undermined in a member state and people are prevented from exercising their democratic rights? they want to know if they can exercise their social rights, if the principle of solidarity works. are the people most affected by the crisis getting help? will solidarity be reflected in the post-2014 budget? if we can make progress in those areas, i think we'll have a successful year.

the following joint debate item union development policy: report by charles goerens on behalf of the committee on development on the 'programme of change: a proposal for the future of the union's development policy' (2012/2002 (ini) (a7-0234/2012) and on the oral question to the commission on the european year of development 2015 (http://www.europarl.europa.eu/sget/docs.docs.type?huqo&reference=o&o-2012-reference=167000&language=t" \blank" b36_7_3/2012) by philippe boulland, european people's party (christian democrats) and thijs berman, on behalf of the group of the party of european socialists (s&d) in the european parliament.
air transport is a key sector of the european union's economy. it is also because it provides jobs for more than 3 million people and contributes more than eur 140 billion to our gdp. however, these airlines are facing a difficult struggle as they are exposed to the unequal competition conditions imposed by the rapidly expanding middle eastern air carriers. european airlines have been criticizing gulf airlines for years, both because of the high level of state subsidies and because these companies do not have to pay corporate taxes. in contrast, european airlines are not eligible for state aid, are subject to corporate tax and are not eligible for european export credit guarantees. these factors together create an unequal competitive situation which i would like to call on the european commission to remedy immediately.
the main thrust and content of president barroso's opening address was set by the 'more europe' initiative. in this union, it seems unthinkable that there should be any hierarchical ranking or ranking in respect of the observance and enforcement of the principles. at the same time, it is inconceivable that the legal practice and legal systems of the member states would create or even revise legislation that is contrary to eu fundamental law. and that, ladies and gentlemen, whether you believe it or not, is what happened on september 20, 2007, with resolution 1483/2007 of the national council of the slovak republic. that decision confirmed, declared inviolable and unalterable the decrees of bénes. in view of this, however, it is particularly interesting to regard this as a document of the second world war, since it was not approved in 1945 but in 2007. therefore, the argument that historical documents are mentioned is unfounded and amateurish, since historical documents belong in the archives and history books, not in the current and valid legal system. i don't know if my colleagues in the house are aware that 13 decrees require enforcement on the basis of racial segregation, discrimination, as well as the nuremberg laws. that is why slovakia should deregulate these rules.
tomorrow is the 56th anniversary of the outbreak of the 1956 revolution. october 23 is not only a holiday for hungarians, but for all freedom-loving people. this year's celebration is double, as we are also commemorating the 120th anniversary of the birth of mindszenty józsef. to the cardinal who was imprisoned by both the fascist and communist dictatorships. the ideals that gave mindszenty strength were those of freedom, tradition, community and genuine european ideas. these were the ideals of the statesmen who were then working in the western half of europe to lift up a continent that was exhausted by war. de gasperi, schumann, monnet and adenauer believed in the same christian europe as the hungarian cardinal. these ideals and this faith kept the tortured priest going and lifted europe out of its seemingly hopeless situation. hope seems to be fading again from our continent today. sometimes it's worth looking back at our ancestors to find a way out. to those whose moral and public example has served as a pillar of a united europe.
in writing . small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) form the backbone of the european union's economy, providing more than two thirds of jobs and more than half of total eu turnover in the private sector. however, smes are the part of the economy that has been most affected by the economic crisis. many small and medium-sized enterprises are struggling to survive. in my view, three areas should be highlighted which i believe have the potential to significantly improve the position of smes on the eu market. the first area is to facilitate access to financial instruments and reduce administrative burdens. they represent the greatest burden for the crisis-hit enterprises. in order to overcome obstacles, we need to move step by step and therefore legislators should be encouraged to take account of the interests of smes at the earliest stage of legislation. the second area is improving entrepreneurship. new businesses are a real alternative to unemployment and offer young people who are out of work a way out of the crisis. smes also have an important role to play in providing adequate training and education to facilitate access to work. the third area is to improve access to eu and global markets. only a quarter of european smes are active outside their home country in the single market, so supporting both export and import promotion is particularly important.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, i fully agree with mrs foster in her report that the european commission and the european union are taking too long to introduce the new regulation and we could lose that position in europe, that innovative situation which can solve a lot of income and job creation later. let's not forget that this restructuring and the new programme will have a 419 billion euro impact on the european union's gdp, create 328 000 new jobs and, in addition, reduce co2 emissions by 50 million tonnes. so these are very important numbers. one thing that must be kept in mind during restructuring and reorganisation is that reducing the training and financing of the workforce is not the only way to achieve cost-effectiveness, as the central european region can already earn about half of the european average in this area. it could be very dangerous for their migration if we continue to decline.
i would like to begin by congratulating my colleague, mr bach, who was the rapporteur for this item. i fully agree that we must try and do everything we can to make information and transparency in the field of passenger rights more transparent, but we must also try to achieve coordination of national legislation. the idea of creating a single european database is a very good one, and the information already available in the national databases, the practices that some member states use at their own level, can be generalized to the eu member states. there is one very important issue that i would like to raise, and that is the issue of tourism. in addition to providing transport as a public service of increasingly high quality, and to maintaining the leading role of tourism in europe in the world, it is also particularly important to ensure that passenger rights are enforced at the highest level possible and that these services are as transparent as possible.
in writing. i also congratulate my colleague bach, who has done an excellent job. i would like to thank him in particular for supporting my 19 amendments, all of which are aimed at making life easier for disabled european citizens. i spoke, among other things, of the equal treatment of disabled people in transport, the physical accessibility of transport facilities and stations, the elimination of barriers to information and communication, and the consideration of the requirements of design for all. i believe it is important to promote sign language services using video technology and the use of web-based services to ensure the involvement of deaf and hard of hearing users and speech impaired users in transport. at the same time, it is essential to establish a common protocol for the training of staff in the different transport sectors and for safety requirements. the latter is especially true of air travel regulations, where individual airlines arbitrarily judge how many deaf or wheelchair users can be carried on their airline's aircraft. this is unacceptable because it will cause a lot of financial and moral damage. i therefore believe that, although at present legislation on the rights of passengers on buses and ships has not yet entered into force, it is essential that a horizontal legislation covering all modes of transport should be introduced in the areas of compensation, reimbursement, transport of passengers with disabilities and information flows. the right to move freely and without restrictions is one of the keys to europe's future, and this is inconceivable without a single regulation on passenger rights!

in writing. the european parliament is firmly in favour of not reducing the eu budget for the period 2014-2020, as insufficient resources would jeopardise the implementation of the new eu growth strategy, europe 2020, and economic growth, among other things. i would like to emphasise that not only the general budget, but also the regional policy budget, cannot be reduced in the 2014-2020 budgetary period. cohesion policy is a major contribution to the europe 2020 strategy and is essential to overcome the economic crisis. the union has always considered investment to be a key pillar of 'smart' growth, a strategic tool for competitiveness and a key element of european solidarity. its funding should therefore be maintained at least at the level of the period 2007-13. in light of all this, i find it unacceptable that, on the basis of the commission's proposal, hungary would be the only eu member state where gdp per capita would remain below 75% of the eu average throughout the period 2014-2020, while its catch-up aid could fall by almost 30% compared to the current period. i consider that the low growth ceiling and the growth forecast used by the commission to determine the level of aid are unfair and contrary to the spirit of the eu treaty, because they hit the countries with the lowest growth rates the hardest and thus preserve their decline in the long term.
in writing. the next seven-year budget of the european union must reflect the increased scope and ambition of the community: closer cooperation and economic, financial and political union cannot be financed with fewer resources. the budget should be geared to growth, employment, competitiveness and the catch-up of less developed regions. the european budget cuts would inevitably undermine growth and competitiveness, the mutually reinforced objectives without which we would be unable to emerge from the crisis. in many member states, community aid alone is already the basis for economic expansion. cutting spending would further widen the gap between developed and less developed regions, between the wealthy and the poor, undermining one of the most important principles of community, solidarity. it is therefore important that we make it clear in our resolution that the european parliament is opposed to the freezing or reduction of the budget at the current level. the socialist group's demand in parliament that a quarter of cohesion funding from the next eu budget starting in 2014 be allocated to projects promoting employment, social inclusion and poverty reduction is crucial. i also believe it is essential to provide more generous support from the eu budget for the period 2014-2020 to programmes promoting human rights and fulfilling the rights of european citizenship. we must firmly reject any attempt to create a future budget for the euro area at the expense of the twenty-weeks.
mr. president, i have to ask you... facts are extremely stubborn things. the latest eurostat figures clearly show that our crisis management is not adequate. however, the decision-making process at the current summit remains fragile. decisions are not born, only direction is born. i also consider it important that the new banking supervision will be able to start operating on the basis of the existing eu institutions and treaties and that the ecb's monetary policy and supervisory tasks will be clearly separated in the future. we've wasted a lot of time, but that's no reason to make irresponsible decisions about oversight right now, based on a lack of time. those that will disadvantage non-euro area members or those who commit to voluntarily joining the new supervisory regime. it is a well-known fact - we have said it many times, we will admit it - that the crisis knows no borders. it is therefore clear to me that crisis management must also be transnational, taking account of national interests, of course. so saving the euro requires the fullest integration. for us, who are openly committed to joining the euro in the future, it is unacceptable that some people talk about a multi-circle europe in banking supervision. it is a fundamental value of european solidarity that is being contradicted by those who say this. he doesn't understand the word of time, and he refuses to learn the bitter lessons of the past.

in writing. – i would like to welcome that the european council meeting of 18-19 october clearly declared that boosting employment and social inclusion remains a priority of the utmost importance and called on member states to step up their efforts to tackle the social consequences of the crisis and to fight poverty and social exclusion. the stumbling of the world economy and the debt crisis strangling our union have accelerated those harmful tendencies, due to which fewer and fewer people are able to maintain a proper standard of living and for more and more european citizens even everyday subsistence represents an insoluble challenge. the european council will devote a special meeting in november to reaching agreement on the next multiannual financial framework, which will be a crucial factor in improving the hopeless situation of the poorest europeans, the marginalised millions of our union. the commission and the parliament have introduced proposals to give priority to the integration of the most disadvantaged communities and i truly hope that the fourteen governments of our political group will promote these amendments unanimously in the council, and enable regional policy to help the most deprived and most marginalised 30 million europeans.
in writing. although the euro area countries have committed themselves not to create their own future budget at the expense of the 27th budget, the legitimacy of an independent euro area budget has been recognised. the commission notes that the bank of greece has not provided any information on the amount of the capital injections, but that the bank of greece has provided information on the amount of the capital injections and the amount of the capital injections. meanwhile, euro area countries can provide financial support on a contractual basis for structural reforms in less competitive members, while this option is no longer available to non-euro area countries. finally, it is becoming increasingly common to adapt the rules of the single market to the needs of the euro area, simply because the non-euro area countries are in the minority (eu17-eu10). small, open economies like hungary, which are stuck outside the zone, have a primary interest in participating in the deepening integration. if it is locked out, it will be less and less able to finance its public debt, attract the capital needed to operate the banking system and develop, and increase its competitiveness. the orbán government should finally give up its freedom-fighting economic policy, which is in sharp contrast to european tendencies. it will result in the loss of the hungarian economy, the country's permanent backwardness.
in writing. − the european parliament, in its own reading, sets the level of commitments at eur 151.1 billion and the level of payments at eur 137.9 billion for 2013. this corresponds to 1,13% and 1,03% of community gni respectively. this would mean that parliament would give €1.5 billion more in commitments and €5.3 billion more in payments to the community in 2013 than the council. the vote resulted in the adoption of amendments that are important for hungary and would provide more resources for research and development, the eu's neighbourhood policy, the strengthening of border controls and the school milk programme. the increase in the ceilings has helped hungary to save the cohesion fund investments that typically concern the central hungarian region and cross-border cooperation.
in writing. − i support the european parliament's efforts to prevent the reduction of cohesion aid. the council's proposal to cut back aid resources is unacceptable, since the current economic situation is the one that most urgently requires the strengthening of cohesion. we cannot allow differences in development to undermine european cooperation. we cannot support the reduction of subsidies, because today, when the union is experiencing one of the most serious crises in its history, it is essential to maintain solidarity between the member states.
in writing. − the european parliament is firmly in favour of not reducing the eu budget for the period 2014-2020, as insufficient resources would jeopardise the implementation of the new eu growth strategy, europe 2020, and economic growth, among other things. i would like to emphasise that not only the general budget, but also the regional policy budget, cannot be reduced in the 2014-2020 budgetary period. it is of great importance to the friends of cohesion policy, including hungary, that the interim report confirms that eu cohesion policy is a strategic instrument for investment, sustainable growth and competitiveness, and a key pillar of european solidarity. the european parliament insists that this area be supported by a stable, solid and sustainable financial framework and that funding be maintained at least at the level of the 2007-2013 period. in light of all this, i find it unacceptable that, on the basis of the commission's proposal, hungary would be the only eu member state where gdp per capita would remain below 75% of the eu average throughout the period 2014-2020, while its catch-up aid could fall by almost 30% compared to the current period. i consider that the low growth ceiling and the growth forecast used by the commission to determine the level of aid are unfair and contrary to the spirit of the eu treaty, because they hit the countries with the lowest growth rates the hardest and thus preserve their decline in the long term.
in writing. − the next multiannual financial framework will most probably prove decisive for the european union’s objectives regarding social and territorial cohesion, and particularly in the social inclusion of marginalised communities. i would like to welcome the fact that the commission has introduced several instruments aiming at the complex development of underdeveloped regions, in line with the requirement for territorial targeting of the european framework for national roma inclusion strategies and i also welcome the proposal to earmark 5% of the erdf resources of each member state for integrated urban strategies. the draft general provisions on the european regional development fund, the european social fund and the cohesion fund establish the integrated territorial investment, which enables member states to allocate funding from several priority axes of the operational programmes for the purposes of multi-dimensional and cross-sectoral intervention. in this context the maximum extent of place-based approach should be considered, in order to unlock unused local capabilities. furthermore, the community-led local development, as a set of multi-fund financed strategies with an integrated bottom-up approach may play a significant role in the inclusion of marginalised communities, including roma, when properly used and may represent an effective tool for the involvement of the concerned communities themselves.
in writing. − the new financial regulation contains, for the first time, provisions on financial planning instruments such as project bonds, specifying in which cases and for what purposes they may be used. in the future, the european commission will be able to create multi-donor aid funds, which are already essential tools for international cooperation and assistance. as one of the world's largest donors, the eu has not been able to contribute to existing aid funds such as the un or the world bank. this will then become possible, which will enhance the union's visibility in the field of international assistance. the reporting obligations of the member states for the structural funds have been extended by the regulation and the new regulation provides for a more precise definition, which allows for more effective controls. it is welcome that the new regulation will allow member states to benefit from the possibility of simplifying union control in exchange for guarantees of the correct use of union funds. it is important to emphasise that the regulation will make the european commission and the european administration work faster, as it will set strict rules on the various payments and the time required to decide on the support for each project.

september. obviously in this time of crisis we have to be more aware of programming, but i would also like to stress as we stressed in the resolution that the commission and parliament have to be flexible and react quickly to the difficult situation. i would like to enumerate some of the points which our group, the european people's party, takes as a condition for the support of the
