which is a clear distinction in the new lisbon treaty. this must help our controlling role over the commission's work and, obviously, the work of the other institutions of the european union. we also support the commission when it is ready to further reduce the regulatory burden or to identify the regulatory areas which are really important from the point of view of the crisis. obviously we cannot say in general that we should deregulate everything. there are areas which the crisis has shown need more regulation, but the regulatory burden as such should be reduced. we also support the commission when it is ready to improve the implementation and the rate and timing of eu legislation. if we are making laws here in parliament, it is in vain if the member states or the application of that legislation is not affected. we would also like to support the political ambition and vision of the european union in the global world, especially in the light of what president barroso said at the september part-session in his speech on the state of the union. obviously we, as members of parliament who are directly elected, are accountable for the european union's action and lack of action, so we rightly demand added value. we are also ready to respond quickly to different situations and to use the fast-track procedure whenever it is needed for the good of european citizens. but we are also responsible for checking and controlling the commission and other european institutions with regard to the efficient use of taxpayers' money. we would also like to support the commission's line in the area of cohesion policy. you mentioned youth unemployment, which was also part of our discussion last time, and also the employability of elderly people and supporting mobility. i would also party, as is the full implementation of the internal market with a binding calendar. my last point stems from this: when we are doing something and the commission work programme should be exactly about that showing a roadmap and a binding calendar of how we are going to proceed the next year it should be with flexibility and reactivity, because our responsibility does not stop at fulfilling pre-designed patterns but also extends to creating an innovative reaction to the different elements and events of the crisis. i would like to express, with these conditions, support for the commission work programme on behalf of the european people's party.
in writing. the current crisis calls for stronger leadership than ever at the level of the european union: it is also clear that the recession cannot be successfully managed by relying on the policies that led to the crisis. i welcome the commission's work programme for 2013 because its elements represent what i see as an innovative, different approach to the past, and because it is based on two major pillars of structural reforms for recovery: restarting growth and ensuring budgetary discipline. i also welcome the commission's position in the document 'a year of job-creating recovery' and am pleased to see that the policy changes towards job creation seem to be taking place. i support the commission's efforts to improve governance mechanisms, in particular the partnership principle applied in the design, implementation and evaluation of the national reform programmes. in the spirit of european solidarity, i welcome the commission's intention to seek to prevent the council from obstructing the operation of the eu solidarity fund, which helps member states to deal with unforeseen disasters, by preparing a new proposal. i would like to make a similar point, namely that the transfer of unused structural funds to the new growth agenda should respect the principle of subsidiarity and involve local and regional authorities. i therefore support the commission's work programme and call for its decisive and effective implementation.

madam president, europe must protect its citizens. last week, hungarian historian andrea varga was brutally assaulted in bucharest by an unknown person on the street. andrea varga is a noted researcher of the communist past. he is currently involved in the restitution of hungarian church property confiscated during the communist dictatorship. in 2009, the european commission commissioned the romanian delegation to organise a prestigious exhibition on system change in eastern and central europe in bucharest. in the final vote on the report on the european year of citizens, i consider it important to draw attention to the case of andrea varga and to ask the romanian authorities and the european parliament to protect him as a european citizen. europe must protect its citizens!
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the european food safety authority in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the final report drew parliament's attention to the fact that the agency had recently discovered a number of conflicts of interest. in order to clarify these and to promote more efficient and cost-effective management of the agency, the committee did not propose granting the exemption in the spring. the second discharge report was presented to the committee at its meeting at the end of september, where the committee found sufficient assurance that the agency would be run in accordance with the rules in the future. the panel decided to grant the exemption. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the european medicines agency in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the final report drew attention to the fact that the agency had recently discovered several conflicts of interest, and in order to clarify these and to promote more efficient and economical management of the agency, the committee did not recommend granting the discharge in the spring. the second discharge report was presented to the committee at its meeting at the end of september, where the committee found sufficient assurance that the agency would be run in accordance with the rules in the future. the panel decided to grant the waiver at its september meeting. i voted in committee and in plenary to grant the discharge.
in writing. − i supported the report on the european year of citizens (2013) because the european union needs the voice of its citizens, especially in today's more difficult economic circumstances. as a member of the european parliament, as an eu actor directly elected by citizens, i consider it important to ensure that the exercise of citizens' rights is protected. in hungary, i am also witnessing the process of increasing the number of eu citizens leaving their own member state to settle in my country. such foreign workers should be aware of their rights and the union should provide them with the maximum assistance in 2013. the effectiveness of the citizens' initiative has been discussed many times in parliament, and i have myself said that i support direct initiatives from such citizens. as a member of the committee on culture and education, i should like to make a special point that the concept of citizenship of the union promotes a sense of respect for the common european values and contributes to cultural diversity.
in writing. − on the commission's proposal, 2013 will be the european year of the citizen. the initiative aims to draw the attention of the citizens of the member states to the exercise of their rights and obligations within the union, in particular with regard to freedom of movement within the union and non-discrimination. the vast majority of european citizens are not aware of the rights and obligations they have as citizens of the union, through conventions and initiatives such as the charter of fundamental rights of the european union and the stockholm programme. statistics compiled by eurobarometer in 2009 showed that some 11.9 million european citizens work outside their country of birth, and 35% of the eu population have considered trying their luck abroad. however, one in five people think that there are numerous obstacles to overcome in order to achieve this. the main reasons for this are lack of information, linguistic differences and difficulties.
in writing. i would like to welcome the initiative to dedicate 2013 to european citizens and the twenty years of existence of european citizenship. such a thematic term may provide remedies to the shortcomings of practicing this right, namely the lack of widespread knowledge and awareness regarding the benefits that european citizenship may provide to all european citizens, students, workers, or pensioners. it must be made clear that these benefits are not the privileges of the elite, but are the fruits of one of our most important community acquisitions that everyone is entitled to without discrimination. we therefore expect information and awareness campaigns to be launched, that target all citizens of the eu, with a special regard to vulnerable groups, who due to their socio-economic status encounter serious difficulties when exercising their right to move, reside and work freely everywhere within the european union.
in writing. − the finalisation of the single european sky legislation would allow europe's airspace to be used in the most efficient way possible, both for the economy, for growth and job creation and for the protection of the environment. the most appropriate means to achieve this is the development of a new generation european air traffic services system (sesar) which could successfully replace outdated technologies and the associated safety and operational risks. the greatest obstacle to the creation of a single european sky is the existence and operation of existing national air traffic control service providers, which operate across national borders. in the future, the current system is expected to lead to congestion as european airspace is about to reach its maximum capacity and will indirectly affect the quality of air transport services provided to the public. the creation of a single european sky should therefore be supported.
in writing. − the previous commission communications contained proposals for all modes of transport. the draft single regulation on passenger rights covering the different modes of transport must be feasible in the medium term, which is essential for the harmonisation of the different modes of transport. the regulation addresses air transport problems by clarifying passenger rights and airline liability issues such as denied boarding, long delays, flight cancellations, loss of baggage and the development of airport infrastructure access. the 2009 ec regulation has significantly strengthened the rights of rail passengers, but as the union rules were designed with the specificities of travel in mind, it has given member states the possibility to exclude certain services from the application of passenger rights. this requires the development of new guidelines to strengthen passenger rights, taking into account the differences between modes of transport.
in writing. − the extension of passenger rights is a key element in strengthening the freedoms of the union, as it contributes to a better exploitation of the right to free movement. unfortunately, eu citizens are not sufficiently aware of their rights, so it is important that we give them the knowledge. i think that citizens should also be made aware that they are protected in the event of a single event, such as the failure of an airline or the shutdown of a volcano. increasing passenger rights can also increase passenger confidence in different forms of travel, as there is no economic growth without this confidence. mr bach's report is exemplary in that it proposes solutions to a number of current problems. i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. − following the presentation of the court of auditors' annual report for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union in respect of the council in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. in its annual report for 2010, the court of auditors concluded that, on the basis of its audit work, the payments made by the institutions and bodies in respect of administrative and other expenditure for the financial year ended 31 december 2010 were, overall, free from material error. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. however, the council did not provide parliament and the committee with a number of documents necessary for the closure of the accounts procedure, without which the financial year 2010 cannot be closed. the panel decided at its meeting at the end of march to postpone the granting of the exemption. the second closure report for september also gave an opinion similar to the spring report. the vote in the specialised committee was to reject the council's discharge. i did not support the council's granting of the discharge in the committee vote or in the plenary vote.
in writing. − following the presentation of the annual report of the european court of auditors for the financial year 2010, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the european environment agency in the context of the closure of accounts procedure for 2010. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the final report highlights that the main problem with the european environment agency is that several procurement procedural errors occurred in the management of the agency in 2010. the report draws attention to the fact that the agency has recently discovered several conflicts of interest. pending clarification of these, the exemption was not recommended by the panel. at its meeting at the end of march, the committee decided to postpone the final report. it considers that further clarifications on the agency's management in 2010 are necessary in order to provide the agency with a final report. the situation has remained essentially unchanged since spring. in my view, the agency should have made more efforts to restore orderly management and proper operation. i voted in committee and in plenary to reject the discharge.
in writing. - it is clear that israel will apply this agreement on its territory. a compromise solution could be that this protocol does not specifically describe the areas to be covered. this is called "constructive indeterminacy". the eu's position is that a palestinian state should be established with east jerusalem as its capital in the gaza strip and the west bank. this is a political position that lacks any basis in international law. palestine never existed in history and the palestinian people are a political projection that lacks ethnic foundations. the only way to create a palestinian state is for the plo to reach an agreement with israel, but it is not willing to do so. the eu should not be affected in its activities at all. the eu wrongly claims that east jerusalem and the west bank are 'occupied palestinian territory'. the mandate for palestine treaty provides for the establishment of closed jewish settlements in jerusalem and west of the jordan river, including the west bank. the settlements established by israel are thus not contrary to international law, but only to the political and legal decisions of the eu, which are in turn contrary to international law. i repeat, the eu should not be involved in this debate. especially not because he's wrong. ashton's objection to israeli construction is unfounded.
in writing. − more than half of the money spent on aid to the world's poor countries currently comes from the european union and its member states, and therefore the union's development policy must be treated as a matter of paramount importance. i believe that the report presented by the rapporteur, mr goerens, is innovative in many respects and contributes to the fundamental objective of eu development policy to eradicate poverty in a sustainable way. i myself agree that the eu's trade promotion support should not only focus on promoting exports from developing countries, but also aim at local and regional trade facilitation. in order to promote inclusive growth and aid effectiveness, there is great hope for the innovation that loans can only be credited as official development assistance in donor countries if, on ex-post analysis, they have actually contributed to poverty reduction. the report also highlights the need to strengthen entrepreneurship and private sector development in developing countries, and the importance of creating a good business environment for smes and micro-enterprises and their access to credit. it is a pleasure to note that the report addresses and seeks to address the need for stronger coordination of development policy actions by member states and other eu actors through the establishment of an independent analyst-advisory group. i therefore supported the development policy report because of its innovative initiatives.
in writing. − the commission has so far presented three communications: industrial policy: boosting competitiveness, small enterprises, the big world a new partnership to help smes seize global opportunities and minimising the regulatory burden on smes adapting eu legislation to the needs of microenterprises. the three pillars were designed to help smes in the eu survive and strengthen their competitiveness. there are some 23 million smes in the eu, employing some 75 million people, or 70% of all eu jobs. further strengthening the competitiveness of smes requires economic restructuring, enhancing industrial innovation, resource efficiency and creating a business environment that enables european smes to compete globally. the governance model of the enterprise europe network is an essential tool for this and to further reduce the administrative and management burden on smes and facilitate the provision of tailor-made support. it is also essential to establish contacts with chambers of commerce and other interested parties.
in writing. − while the commission and the member states have made real efforts to improve the competitive position of smes, further action is still needed. i continue to attach importance to any initiative that ensures that eu instruments to support smes are used more effectively or that contributes to the smooth running of this business community. the sme test, which was mentioned in yesterday's debate, is a good example of the latter, and it allows a preliminary examination of the impact of legislation on smes. i should also mention the operation of the eu sme portals proposed by mr rübig, which would allow european programmes for smes to operate more efficiently. a well-functioning single market creates a competitive environment in which smes are still at a significant competitive disadvantage, in large part because they lack sufficient information about business opportunities and in part because a lack of language skills is a major obstacle. i therefore believe that an eu strategy for 'better' regulation is needed and that the ambitious proposals put forward by mr rübig are essential to make the european union more competitive and attractive for investors and producers, which is why i voted in favour of this report!
in writing. − almost three quarters of all jobs in the european union are provided by small and medium enterprises and, furthermore, 85% percent of newly established work places between 2002 and 2010 were created by this sector. it can be clearly seen that not only do smes play a key role in the european economy but enhancing their competitiveness and helping them engage more people may also vitally contribute to the recovery from the economic crisis and may reset the eu economy on a trajectory of growth. the union should develop and launch tailor-made programmes in order to boost the entrepreneurial potential of eu citizens, especially targeting vulnerable groups excluded from the labour market, for whom self-employment as an alternative may provide a way out of poverty and marginalisation. in this regard, i would like to welcome the commission’s successfully implemented plan to reduce the administrative burdens by 25% this year.
in writing. − while some 98% of us-eu trade is free of problems and substantial obstacles, disputes are a serious strain on the atmosphere of bilateral relations. despite frequent high-level consultations, there are still a number of issues to be resolved and some new cases are emerging. i welcome the fact that mr moreira has made a number of recommendations to improve the development and integration of our knowledge-based economies and to promote innovative technologies. the proposals include, for example, identifying priority areas for research cooperation, deepening policy dialogue and promoting higher education exchange programmes. i therefore voted in favour of this report, as i believe it is a response to our commitments made at previous summits to develop a strategy and practical proposals for a more open and efficient overseas market.
in writing. japan is the european union's third most important trading partner. the conclusion of a free trade agreement would bring numerous benefits to both parties. europe could increase its exports and create new jobs. hungary is particularly interested in the opening of free trade negotiations, as this could significantly facilitate hungarian agricultural exports to the far eastern country. the agreement will allow the japanese automotive industry to make further investments in europe and hungary, thus creating new jobs.
the creation of the single market was a huge step for european citizens. i believe that the legislators in 1986, when they created the package of legislation, followed a guiding principle. the first is unhindered access to funds, goods and services, and at the same time the free movement of persons. however, let us not forget that there are still 80 million eu citizens who are partially or totally excluded from these. it is of paramount importance that the single market is made accessible to persons with disabilities. whatever the disability, it is the obstacles that are immediately associated with it. these must be dismantled as soon as possible, for they are the product of society, not of our own disability. mrs. bastos pointed out and made suggestions for the transmission of the clearance. for that, i thank you. i take this opportunity to reiterate the need for a european accessibility package in the current economic crisis. because people with disabilities do matter!
2012 is the 20th anniversary of the single market. this anniversary is a good opportunity to think together and to overcome the obstacles that prevent us from exploiting the full potential of the internal market. but as we celebrate, we must not forget that the main beneficiaries of the single market are citizens and businesses. it's our job as legislators to do that on their behalf. so our task does not end with creating good legislation, it must include good implementation, and also create synergies across borders between the different national public administration systems. i would like to stress that the internal market is the best available tool for restoring economic growth and that it is therefore very important that all single market issues are dealt with as a matter of priority. i congratulate the rapporteur on her excellent work and i also welcome the efforts made by the european commission in this area so far. however, i believe that this process should be continued. even greater commitment is needed in the area of enforcement. we have made significant efforts to implement quick and efficient information portals, but parallels have also emerged which will hamper this work. it is very important that in the future we define our objectives using a consumer approach. i would like nothing to prevent member states from treating this issue as a top priority in the future.
in writing. as the itre expert committee responsible for the opinion, allow me to briefly highlight the concerns of citizens in the energy and telecommunications sectors and to suggest solutions to improve the situation of smes. in the energy sector, consumer dissatisfaction is mainly due to the complexity of energy bills and the lack of answers to why energy prices have recently risen. the regulation is also very permissive in several member states, so that energy suppliers are not obliged to provide consumers with adequate information. in order to ensure adequate protection of consumers' rights, member states should transpose union legislation as soon as possible. in the telecommunications sector, the main problem is the quality of the internet service, and it is also difficult to compare prices and service packages. the role of regulatory authorities and consumer organisations should be strengthened and transparency ensured in order to monitor service quality and prices. small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) have problems accessing national and eu funding. smes have difficulty accessing the information on the application and do not have employees who are experienced in writing applications. the situation of smes would thus be greatly helped if member states introduced a so-called one-stop shop for the mobilisation of union and central funding.
in writing . the single market, celebrating its 20th birthday, has changed the way european citizens live, work and travel, and has become an integral part of our lives. the opportunity for enterprises to enter european markets has been opened up. without the single market, there is no european union, it is the foundation of prosperity that makes europe stronger. we must do everything we can to ensure that the internal market continues to develop so that our children can live in a safe europe in 20 years' time, with greater access to its values. what do we have to do about it? we need to open our minds to the unimpeded flow of creativity and knowledge that leads to innovation and growth. we have connected transport networks, and now we must also connect energy and telecommunications networks, to finally connect 500 million europeans. we must create a united europe that not only our grandchildren will see, but that the young generation entering the workforce now will not feel like a lost generation. my dear colleagues, let us dream big, let us dream of a united and prosperous europe.
in writing. i think there is not a young adult in europe today who has not heard of the erasmus programme, which offers higher education students the opportunity to study in another country for a longer or shorter period. the fact that more than 200 000 students took part in this popular higher education programme in the last academic year 2010/2011 alone is proof that erasmus is one of the european union's most successful achievements, with positive effects far beyond the realm of higher education. however, it appears that the funding of the programme is now in jeopardy due to recent eu budgetary cuts. although the european commission will propose to supplement the 2012 budget with the necessary funds to run erasmus, if the member states and parliament cannot agree on additional payments, there will be no funds to cover students' studies for the next academic year. i know that the prolonged economic crisis of our time and the resulting budgetary restrictions affect more or less all the countries of the union, but i ask the member states to support the commission's proposal. without proper training for the professionals of the future, we have no chance of recovering from the crisis.
in writing. − the stabilisation and association agreement between the ec and serbia and the interim agreement allowing for the early application of the provisions on trade and trade-related matters contained therein were signed in luxembourg on 29 april 2008. the former is still in the process of ratification. the proposed regulation provides, inter alia, for price quotas for fish and fishery products, trade defence measures and customs tariff regulation. several agreements with the western balkan countries have been concluded in the past and are part of the trade omnibus regulation i. they aim to harmonise existing regulations in order to ensure the smooth implementation of the common commercial policy. this is the only way to ensure that the european parliament's position on the subject is consistent and coherent. the agreement is another important step towards our southern neighbour becoming a full member of the eu.
in writing. − the decision will give our citizens more tangible help from the union when they are in a third country. the adoption of the report also represents a major step forward in the protection of hungarian citizens, as it will help them in situations where they need it most anywhere in the world outside the union, because their situation and return would otherwise be impossible due to an accident, an attack, a natural disaster or even the loss of documents. that is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − the adoption and future application of the draft directive on consular protection could be proof that solidarity between countries still exists and can be mobilised within the union. this directive strengthens the protection of our citizens in situations where they feel most vulnerable. in the most difficult moments, being part of the european union gives them a chance. the directive we have just voted on requires that in the event of lost documents, arrest, accident or illness, those member states which have representation in a particular region, in any remote part of the world, must provide the same assistance to unrepresented union citizens as to their own nationals. they must also provide protection where the citizen concerned would have to make a disproportionate effort to reach his own diplomatic mission (a journey of more than one day). i regard this as a major step forward in the protection of hungarian citizens in situations where they are most in need of protection anywhere in the world outside the union, because their situation and return to their home country would be rendered impossible by an accident, an attack, a natural disaster or even the loss of documents. we are also interested in the part of the directive that increases the role of the european external action service and the eu delegations and the coordination of consular protection, i am thinking here of the current case of the bolivian prisoner of conscience, el príncipe tomás. this is an important gesture by parliament, the council and the commission towards their own citizens.
in writing. − eu citizens travel outside the eu more than 90 million times a year, but only the united states, china and russia have diplomatic representation in all 27 eu countries. these figures also reflect the high priority of consular protection for union citizens. the report adopted today, in my view, represents a further step forward in this area, as it will make it easier for citizens who are not represented by a member state in a non-eu country to have access to consular protection. in line with the idea of a greater europe, i welcome the involvement of the european external action service and eu delegations in consular tasks, with responsibility for coordinating cooperation between member states in crisis situations and for evacuation. this eliminates the unfair situation that one member state bears the entire burden of coordination in such cases. the proposal also simplifies access to financial assistance in crisis situations and makes it easier for the donor country to recover its costs. the document is therefore based on european values and solidarity between european nations and i believe it is an important step towards european citizenship, which is why i voted in favour of adopting the report.
in writing. − the right of unrepresented eu citizens to consular protection abroad under the same conditions as member state nationals is one of the union’s most important achievements and is symbolic of its solidarity and its common identity towards third countries. moreover, the provisions for extending consular protection – including the broad definition of being unprotected, the clarification as to when the embassy or consulate of the citizen’s own member state is non-accessible, and the inclusion of protection for third-country family members of eu citizens, to the same extent as member states provide it to the third-country family members of their own nationals – bring great practical advantages for individuals.
in writing. − the european union is currently experiencing its worst economic crisis since its creation. unfortunately, in some cases, this crisis is threatening the very foundations of the european economy. i would like to stress, however, that the internal market remains the best available tool for restoring growth in the economies of the member states, and that it is therefore very important that all single market issues are dealt with as a matter of priority. i welcome the efforts made so far by the european commission in this area, but i believe that more commitment is needed in the future to implement european legislation. it is gratifying to note that in recent years a number of instruments have been developed to further stimulate the completion of the single market and to measure the contribution of individual member states to this objective. the internal market scoreboard is one such tool, for example, which encourages member states to improve the quality and timeliness of the transposition of internal market directives. so the question is, what further action do we need? i myself would vote for more decisive but realistic targets for transposition and implementation. the aim should therefore be to achieve the lowest possible transposition deficit, the shortest possible transposition delays and the highest possible quality of transposition. mrs bastos' report highlights the concerns of our citizens and businesses, and it is our task now to respond to them and to address these problems as quickly as possible. i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the resolution because the conclusion of a free trade agreement would bring numerous benefits to both parties. europe could increase its exports and create new jobs. hungary is particularly interested in the opening of free trade negotiations, as this could significantly facilitate hungarian agricultural exports to the far east. the agreement will allow the japanese automotive industry to make further investments in europe and hungary, thus creating new jobs.
in writing. − japan is one of the world's largest economies and the european union's third most important trading partner. the conclusion of a free trade agreement would bring numerous benefits to both parties. europe could increase its exports and create new jobs. hungary is particularly interested in the opening of free trade negotiations, as this could significantly facilitate hungarian agricultural exports to the far eastern country. the agreement will allow the japanese automotive industry to make further investments in europe and hungary, thus creating new jobs. the suzuki plant in esztergom would have access to japanese automotive parts with lower or duty-free duties, thus increasing its competitiveness vis-à-vis other car manufacturers. for all these reasons, i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. − the world is changing at lightning speed if we take demographic changes into account: in less than ten years, the world's population has increased by 1 billion, and will reach 9 billion by 2030. however, the higher intensity of world population growth will extinguish slower economic growth in its processes. globalization is contributing to this, as it has created a balance of power between states, but within states the gap between rich and poor is widening. this is most evident in the development of brazil, china and india. the changes in africa are promising, with more countries holding free elections, more accepting of the concept of political economy. the african union is taking serious steps to manage problems at the regional level, but it is already clear that the millennium development goals of 2000 will not be achieved by 2015. europe, as the largest donor to the 'transformation programme', therefore has an obligation to speed up the implementation of the programme and increase its effectiveness. for some emerging economies, the most pressing problem is not the production of goods but their equitable distribution. the five main objectives set out in the proposal are primarily intended to combat poverty, taking into account new actors such as china and india or new phenomena such as the extremely high birth rate.
in writing. − the european union has a major role to play in development policy, with the main objective of reducing poverty and, in the long term, eradicating it, and can make a major contribution to this if it makes good use of the fundamental synergies between eu policies. the european union's action in the field of development cooperation is based on cooperation between member states and international actors, on the need to ensure consistency between european policies and development objectives, and on the principle of aid effectiveness. i myself agree with the part of the draft report which says that only 3.4% of the commission's development aid budget is spent on direct food interventions, whereas malnutrition kills some 2.6 million children a year. it is a pleasure to see that the report highlights the corporate social responsibility obligations of companies to encourage employers to apply social standards that go beyond the current provisions. i still disagree with the rapporteur that, contrary to the commission's promise, it has not proposed to extend the european parliament's powers of scrutiny over the european development fund. i believe that the implementation of the recommendations in the report would be more effective in promoting policy coherence for development, and i therefore supported the vote on it.

mr president, economic and monetary union cannot be viable and sustainable without two indispensable ingredients: fiscal discipline and democratic legitimacy. permanently strong fiscal discipline should result in structurally balanced budgets across the business cycle, in order to avoid debt traps. at the same time, for fiscal discipline to hold, solid democratic legitimacy is necessary, based on an enlightened political culture of civic responsibility. at this stage in the development of the eu, fiscal policy has to remain national because full democratic legitimacy exists only at national level. setting budgets, imposing taxes and redistributing income are inalienable components of national sovereignty. that includes also the right of states to go bankrupt, while other states should maintain the right of refraining from bailing out bankrupt ones. for market capitalism and liberal democracy to survive in this century, bankruptcy should remain a credible threat not only for enterprises but also for central and sub-sovereign governments. fiscal socialism, the demutualisation of debt without commensurate mutualisation of national sovereignty, is untenable and unacceptable. it will only destroy the remainder of european fiscal discipline and further weaken democratic legitimacy.

report or the content of the report. the report before us, ladies and gentlemen, is a response to the van rompuy report and is an integral part of the package of measures to strengthen economic and monetary union. i am convinced that, in addition to the observance of coordinated economic policies and fiscal restraint in the member states, it is essential that the union has at its disposal a system of supervision that is accurate, capable of covering, predicting and, where appropriate, managing systemic risks. this new regulation should therefore not only create a more predictable regulatory environment but hopefully also restore the financial market confidence as well as the distorted financing relationship between banks and sovereign debt. the completion of all the elements of banking union would reduce the cost of financing for euro area banks, which would have a positive impact on their lending activity on a cross-cutting basis, thus giving a new impetus to europe's economy. it is therefore in our common interest that european banking supervision should be established, but it is not at all important what role the new system now being established will play for those member states which wish to cooperate but have not yet adopted the single currency. i have therefore sought in my amendments to give priority to balancing the obligations and rights of these member states and to creating a financial guarantee which would mitigate or eliminate the distortive effects of the banking union. after initial debates and disagreements, the draft report has found broad support in our committee, which i am glad to see, and that this debate is not coming to an end but is continuing, as we can put forward our more detailed, more practical clarifying opinion in two more legislative packages.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the report by mr zwiefka, as it raises a number of questions of legal certainty. i myself believe that the effectiveness of jurisdictional agreements should be improved, as this will also avoid the use of 'abusive tactics'. the report shall also cover enforceable judgments given in the member state in which the original proceedings were instituted. here, the aim should be to make the enforceable judgment enforceable in other eu member states without any intermediate proceedings. in my view, the effectiveness of arbitration agreements also needs to be improved in order to give full effect to the will of the parties. i am confident that the implementation of the recommendations in this report will significantly increase legal certainty.
in writing. − the adoption of the brussels i regulation is of decisive importance for the creation of a single european judicial area. the purpose of the regulation is to establish closer cooperation between the authorities of the member states by removing obstacles resulting from differences in legal and administrative systems, thereby facilitating the exercise of the right to a fair trial. the revision of the brussels i regulation is a success story for european consumers, businesses and the internal market as a whole. the new rules will benefit citizens and businesses by promoting legal certainty and making decisions more predictable. the abolition of the exequatur procedure is a significant achievement. this will allow a judgment enforceable in the member state of origin to be enforceable in other eu member states without intermediate proceedings, thus speeding up the free movement of judgments. it enables quick, fair and efficient dispute settlement, thereby facilitating cross-border trade by smes and contributing to growth.
in writing. − given that the retail and wholesale distribution of chemicals is risky and a matter of concern from a safety policy point of view, i strongly support efforts to tighten licensing. it is important to note, however, that the remedial action will only succeed if the final result also provides certain risk benefits for the consumer. in the light of the situation of farm workers, it is therefore necessary to recognise that the scope of activities subject to authorisation should be regulated at the level of the distributor. in this context, i welcome any effort to promote the harmonisation of the functioning of national authorisation offices, in addition to the strict notification requirements and the continuous operational supervision, thereby contributing to the development of bilateral consumer legal certainty.

in writing. − accidents in recent years, such as the oil spill in the gulf of mexico, have reiterated the need to ensure that offshore oil and gas operations are conducted under strict safety rules and controls. as more and more new fossil fuel exploration is taking place in the european union, it is important that european regulation keeps pace with these trends. on these grounds, i voted in favour of the recommendation to ratify the mediterranean protocol, as it also confirms the union's commitment to this issue at international level. however, it should be noted that the rules can only be complete if they not only state the 'polluter pays' principle but also require adequate financial guarantees to ensure that the polluter is actually able to pay for the damage caused by an industrial disaster.
in writing. − the report is to be supported, as it is very important that, in addition to fiscal reforms and budgetary cuts, member states should be able to apply policies that are fundamentally based on economic growth and job creation. however, as a result of the economic and financial crisis, these objectives require priority social investment and employment-friendly measures at union level. it is therefore essential to support the idea that budgetary and economic policy cooperation and surveillance should be supplemented in future by additional employment and social policy elements, together with, of course, scoreboards to monitor the correct use and the appropriate investment performance, and a more targeted use of the structural funds.
in writing. − beside the short term objective of re-establishing the stability and sustainability of european public finances, consolidation measures need to be matched with a definite focus on social investment and employment policy. targeted social investments are needed on the one hand to help families and individuals adapt to the changing economic environment, and on the other hand to contribute to the creation of sustainable jobs and supporting all european citizens that are excluded from the labour market – with special regard to the young and the long term unemployed – in returning to work. social investments have proven to be excellent tools for matching skills available in education and training with the demands of the labour market as well as for reconciling the goals of economic and social policy. the exchange of good practices in this field should therefore be fostered by the european commission and member states should consider tax advantages for social enterprises and for investors in social businesses. furthermore, social enterprises should be made an investment priority within the framework of the european regional development fund and european social fund.
in writing. - i agree with the report in principle. from a hungarian point of view, i think it is important to highlight the point which aims to increase the resources to be spent on promotion and information measures. in order to increase efficiency, it is appropriate to restrict the use of these cap resources to publicly owned and controlled entities. i voted in favour of the report for all these reasons.
in writing. − the quality of administration of succession law is not the same in the member states of the union. accordingly, those burdens may be particularly high for operators in the lower categories of the market, as they are more affected by the administrative costs of enforcement. i voted in favour of mrs gallo's report, because her proposals suggest that the right of succession does not affect the market on which works are sold and does not have a negative impact on the circulation of the art market. for this reason, i - in agreement with the rapporteur - consider it of the utmost importance that the commission continue to work closely with the parties concerned to consolidate the situation on the european art market and to remedy the difficulties that have arisen.
in writing. − the succession directive introduces a compulsory succession right for the benefit of the author of the work and his heirs after his death. successive rights are rights in intellectual property rights which entitle the author and his successors in title to a share in the sale price of any subsequent sale of the work after the first sale by the author. the right of succession has been recognised in hungary for decades, but the directive has only been fully applied in all member states since 1 january 2012. the purpose of the legislation is to harmonise the basic conditions for the application of the succession right in order to consolidate the position of the european art market in the world.
in writing. − the report aims to make the ordinary legislative procedures more efficient and transparent by introducing binding rules to enhance accountability. the european parliament ensures that citizens have the opportunity to know what their elected representatives are saying and how they are voting during each legislative procedure. the public nature of debates and votes strengthens representative democracy at european level.
in writing. − in the context of the current economic crisis, the european parliament is following the efforts of the member states and has adopted a cost-saving package for the 2012 budget. as part of this, parliament has undertaken to reduce administrative costs, which includes limiting the automatic translation of verbatim minutes of proceedings into all languages. i am voting in favour of limiting the current costly dual system of translation and interpretation. in my view, the use of the original language for oral proceedings alone leads to considerable budgetary and resource savings, while maintaining the principle of multilingualism. modern technology has enabled parliament to make audio and video recordings of its meetings available to all on the internet, not only after each sitting, but also during live broadcasts, with simultaneous interpretation into all the official languages of the union.
in writing. − the two-wheeler industry is also an important part of the european union's economy and jobs. it is necessary to improve the functioning of the internal market, on the one hand, in order to provide unrestricted access to vehicle repair information through the availability of technical information in a standardised format, and on the other hand, in order to ensure genuine competition in the market for vehicle repair and maintenance information services. i voted in favour of the report by my colleague wim de kamp, because its innovative proposals can help to tackle many of the problems of type-approval and market surveillance in the motorcycle sector. i believe that this long awaited report provides sufficient attention to the motorcycle sector and to the requirements that can be expected for the production of two-, three- and four-wheel vehicles.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report by mr panzeri, as it proposes to contribute to the competitiveness of the industry by simplifying the existing legislation on vehicle type-approval. i am convinced that, in order to ensure a high level of road safety, occupational safety and environmental protection and to complete the internal market for agricultural and forestry vehicles, it is appropriate to introduce an eu type-approval procedure for all vehicle categories, while taking due account of cost-benefit considerations, with particular attention to small and medium-sized enterprises. i myself believe that the rapporteur has proposed a considerable number of technical amendments, the purpose of which is to clarify certain technical aspects and to ensure greater coherence in the text. i believe that mr panzeri's report draws sufficient attention to the problem areas of the sector and to the new requirements.
in writing. − in recent years, the hungarian card payment market has been on a steady growth path. although it may take another 10-15 years for the domestic card payment rate to catch up with western european rates, the catch-up has been clear and unbroken. i myself believe that it is impossible to guarantee that the benefit of the reduction in interchange fees is passed on to consumers by the traders. as i indicated in my plenary speech, a possible regulation of commissions might ensure a reduction in the costs of traders, but it would also have a number of adverse effects on the market as a whole. i therefore supported the report, but i voted against the amendments relating to points 41 and 43 of the proposal, in view of the foreseeable consequences of the regulation.
in writing. − three years after the financial, social and economic crisis, the proposal voted today is timely, as close monitoring of shadow banking activities is indispensable both to manage the crisis and to prevent future financial market turbulence. it is well known that while their activities can stimulate trade and money flows, they can also cause sudden and massive damage in the absence of the safety nets of the traditional banking system. the report aims to develop a database and information system that will facilitate the monitoring of securities market operations and repo transactions and thus increase the prospects of avoiding systemic risks in the future. for these reasons, i voted in favour of the proposal with a clear yes.
in writing. − rapidly changing technology requires rapid responses. the vast storehouse of the digital world is no longer limited to desktop computers, but also includes smartphones, tablets, and so on. educational institutions are adapting to the digital world, minors are becoming more familiar with the internet and are moving around much more at home than adults. several studies have shown that, on average, children spend an hour and a half a day surfing the internet, starting at age nine, and in many cases, they're learning to use the internet before they've learned to read and write. however, ensuring wise, responsible, and safe use of the internet would require adult help. it is important that they are aware of the potential dangers to their internet use, including internet and video game addiction, and that distraction from reality can distort the way minors relate to their identity. the proliferation of chat rooms and other messaging options has increased the availability of minors' personal data in the digital world. you have to wear gloves in the fight against illegal and harmful content. the easy access to online content related to online harassment, racism, violence, discrimination allows the unwary and naive child to be unaware of the violation of human dignity. the responsibility for preventing minors from accessing illegal content lies with the family, the school and civil society. the intention to set up an electronic age verification system is welcome. however, it is regrettable that the agreement between the 17 community site operators and the commission, which was concluded in 2009 and was intended to protect the safety and security of minors, has not been implemented. the owners and administrators of websites should provide a system for the mandatory provision of parental consent for the processing of personal data of children under 13 years of age. the biggest problem with this is that when minors face age-related internet restrictions, they either give false identities or authentic information but an older age when registering on the site.
in writing. − protecting children is a key issue everywhere, whether in the real world or even in the digital world. it is a sobering fact that european children spend 40 hours a week in front of the tv, and 88 minutes a day on the internet. i myself believe that children, in the absence of supervision and control, are exposed to many dangers. fortunately, programmes and working methods have already been introduced in many eu member states to try to keep pace with the demands of the digital world. i voted in favour of the costa report because, like my colleague, i believe that the main tasks for member states now are to regulate harmful content, to combat sexual harassment online, to protect children from online crime, to protect data protection and digital identity, and not least perhaps the most important is the safer internet programme, which is being implemented in all eu member states.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the above report, which aims to put the role of social enterprises in the european union at the forefront. there is no doubt that the number of social enterprises is growing across europe, but their support requires more and more regional and cross-border cooperation and additional funding. however, these areas of work are far from complete, as there is still further work to be done to define the concept and scope of social enterprises, to define the role of the employees involved and to identify possible business models for social enterprises in europe. the report before us has outlined ideas on all these issues, which are also forward-looking for the future of the single market.
in writing. − social enterprises may significantly contribute to the fulfilment of the eu2020 objectives and therefore the twofold goal set forth by the commission’s communication and also by this report to recognize already existing social businesses and to pave the way for the creation of new and innovative forms needs to be pursued and more effectively communicated. the european commission should create an online public database of fair businesses, quality schemes and labels to serve as a multilingual information and exchange platform for social entrepreneurs, business and social investors, also in order to facilitate accessibility of csr information and improve transparency. a european regulatory framework for ethical investment funds and a quantified headline target on social businesses needs also to be developed by the commission in cooperation with the member states, in order to support social businesses through eu policies and to increase their number, and consequently the jobs they create.
in writing. − the european parliament voted today in strasbourg on a draft report that would introduce stricter fiscal discipline in the european union and take concrete steps towards the completion of the banking union. the thyssen report, towards a genuine economic and monetary union, was drawn up in response to the decisions taken by the european council in june. the document points out that in recent years financial markets have become increasingly fragmented and opaque, a situation which has contributed to the european financial, economic and social crisis that continues to this day. it is therefore essential that, while respecting the economic policies and fiscal restraints of the member states, which are also coordinated at european level, the union should have at its disposal a system of supervision capable of accurately covering, predicting and, where appropriate, managing systemic risks. the creation of the new structure will create a more predictable regulatory environment and, in addition to financial market confidence, will also restore the distorted financing relationship between banks and sovereign debt. i therefore voted in favour of the draft report.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report, which responds very well to the deepening of economic and monetary union set out in the june council meeting by including the integrated financial framework as one of its objectives, alongside the integrated fiscal and economic austerity. the main objective to be achieved - which has been incorporated in the report in the form of amendments - is that we should have equal rights and obligations as non-euro area member states when we wish to join the emerging banking union system in the future. another positive aspect of the report is that it openly calls on the member states and the commission not to disregard the position and role of the european parliament in the design of european banking supervision and in its subsequent accountability.
in writing. − the initiative to create a genuine economic and monetary union is well-suited to the range of economic policy measures adopted by the eu so far, since, in addition to national and fiscal discipline, banking supervision is also included in the common economic and monetary policy. the initiative is also of great importance to non-euro area member states, as national supervision of banks has so far proved insufficient to stem the economic crisis. i consider it important that when the new banking supervision system is established, we ensure that hungary can join the common supervisory system with the same rights and obligations.
my fellow members, gentlemen of the jury! in fact, in the united states, the use of unconventional gas has fundamentally transformed the energy market in recent years. the alternative source of gas, which accounts for more than half of the production, has led to a significant decrease in gas prices in america and the need to rely on imported sources, as pointed out by several in this debate. in the midst of a protracted crisis, europe cannot afford the luxury of not weighing such an option carefully, and of course responsibly weighing the pros and cons. so i would like to highlight some factors that can help us to form a balanced view of the subject. what can shale gas mean for security of supply, competitiveness and environmental protection? first, the security of supply. the union can mitigate its gas supply exposure by exploiting alternative gas sources. the new resources will enable europe not only to offset the depletion of traditional gas resources in the north sea, but also to reduce the unilateral energy dependence of some member states. secondly, the question of competitiveness. as the us example shows, the proliferation of alternative gas sources can lead to lower prices as competition in the gas market increases and consumers' bargaining power improves vis-à-vis large gas suppliers. the new resources can thus improve the competitiveness of european industry and reduce the burden on the population. and finally, we look at the environmental aspect. maybe it's the most complex. on the one hand, as gas is one of the cleanest fuels, the exploitation of alternative gas reserves could be an appropriate transition to a low-carbon energy production. on the other hand, proper regulation and effective supervision are needed to ensure that companies comply with the highest environmental standards when extracting.
my fellow members of the house of representatives! i think this conversation has gone wrong. europe cannot afford to be ideologically biased towards any one energy source. we need both renewable energy, nuclear energy, conventional and non-conventional fossil fuels such as shale gas, and new forms of energy such as those that nobel prize winner george olah has discovered with the methane economy. the only place in europe it's in is iceland. china provides half of the world's methane energy. in hungary, my country, there are four promising gas fields. i myself, as a member of the environmental committee, consider it very important to respect environmental considerations. i think this is a key issue, but we can't take a step that will close the path of development to shale gas extraction while maximizing environmental protection, so i ask that you make a wise decision on this matter.
in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague, mr boguslaw sonik, on the excellent work he has done to reach a compromise that is acceptable to all on the extremely important report on shale gas. as a hungarian representative, i welcome the fact that the european parliament is not initiating a european moratorium on unconventional gas extraction, thus allowing each member state to decide independently in the future on the exploitation of its energy resources. as with all human activities, shale gas extraction can never be risk-free, and it is therefore important that the existing environmental regulatory framework is fully applied. but we must not make the mistake of creating additional rules based on fears that have no scientific basis, which make it impossible to adapt this promising technology to europe. it should be borne in mind that shale gas can increase security of supply in certain regions of europe and can also have a positive impact on energy prices by creating new supply on the fossil fuel market. the commission also found that the union industry was able to increase its sales volume by more than 50% during the period considered. it is also a well-known fact that the us 'shale gas revolution' will significantly reduce us demand for middle east gas supplies, making them cheaper and easier to supply to european consumers. in view of all this, i would not consider it fortunate if the eu regulation made it difficult or impossible to exploit an energy source that could, under the right conditions, contribute to the energy supply of the union and hungary.
in writing. the automotive industry is one of the last industrial sectors in europe to be able to support a significant workforce. europe must break with the faulty economic policy of excluding production from our economic community. the european union needs a patriotic economic structure in which the car industry is given particular protection. restructuring is imperative, as the economic crisis has brought new challenges to european societies. i would like to stress that hungary is exemplary in this respect, where investments by mercedes or audi have created a lot of new jobs, which have also been complemented by infrastructure developments that are useful for everyone. i think it is unacceptable that local smes should be given a greater role as suppliers.
let me start with a historical recap. when we were preparing the accession of the nine new member states to schengen, we heard from the council that this could not happen on schedule because sis ii was not yet ready. then they said we'd have to wait a few more months. this was early 2007. then, thanks to the portuguese presidency, accession finally took place in december 2007. we're about to celebrate its fifth anniversary, and we're still waiting for sis ii. there were a lot of promises. let's hope this is the last of them. we argued then that schengen was not a technical issue but a political one. it shall be based on and strengthened by trust between member states. one of the most important conditions for the functioning of the internal market. the introduction of sis ii is a step forward in areas where it is badly needed. it contributes to the security of the region, improves data protection, enforces fundamental rights and supports community decision-making in this area. we sincerely hope that it will also help to bring the long-standing issue of romania and bulgaria joining schengen to a head. the portuguese presidency has been committed to helping the cause of the countries joining in the previous round. the commission's commitment and the council's commitment are still very much needed.
my dear colleagues, the committee on petitions is the conscience of the european parliament, and in many ways of the european union as a whole. for many european citizens, it is the ultimate refuge in protecting their right to asylum. it is therefore very important that it does its job well and i fully agree with mr chichester's report, the committee on petitions is doing excellent professional and political work and it is very important that there should be greater coherence between the european commission and the committee on petitions. this is all the more necessary because petitions concerning violations of fundamental democratic rights have increased in recent years. it is regrettable that in recent months a number of petitions have been received from my country, hungary, to the commission because the hungarian government has retroactively abolished the old-age and service disability pensions, as well as the support for regular social allowance recipients and the support for law enforcement officers. i am confident that the committee on petitions will provide substantial assistance to those whose basic human rights have been violated in these matters.
i congratulate you on your excellent report. the growing number of petitions is an important signal. respect for our common values still leaves much to be desired in the union. more and more european citizens are aware of their rights, want to exercise them and know that they can petition the commission, the european parliament if their fundamental rights are infringed or if they are discriminated against. the increase in the number of petitions in one area is a sensitive sign of the increase in national restrictions, in violations. for example, in 2011 the number of petitions concerning freedom of the press and expression increased from 26 the previous year to 131. our aim should be to not only respond to complaints but also to find real solutions to petitions in cooperation between the committee on petitions and the european commission.
in writing. it is a matter of common and indisputable fact that the growing role of the committee on petitions in the european parliament accurately demonstrates and reflects the seriousness of european citizens in their dialogue and direct participation with the eu institution. both the themes and the number of themes summarised in the report leave no doubt that the committee on petitions should be given a new look and a more prominent role and greater powers, as the report states. the weight and cause of this is, moreover, not only due to the foregoing but also to two other factors. on the one hand, this is justified by the 'more europe' approach that has been formulated and announced, since it is impossible to imagine a political and structural representation in which a weak and incompetent committee acts as a mediator between the citizen represented and the member. on the other hand, the growing euroscepticism in some member states is the result of an effective and fruitless dialogue. in summary, i agree with the report in its content and in its stated and expected objectives and i support it fully. it should be a genuine point of contact with an autonomous and clear role.
on behalf of the s&d group. this report, together with the previous discussion, shows where we stand on the implementation of our core values. it raises a lot of important questions. it emphasises the role of the member states' judiciaries, the importance of training and cooperation. it welcomes the growing number of petitions. it is important to the foundation of democratic governance, the rule of law. i would like to support the rapporteur's idea that the narrow interpretation of the charter of fundamental rights, that is to say that it can only be held accountable at eu level if the member state transposes and applies eu law, is often a source of dissatisfaction and frustration. many see it as a sign of the union's weakness, its lack of credibility. i would therefore encourage the commission, together with the rapporteur, to use the means at its disposal to safeguard the letter and spirit of the treaty, if it is not ensured at member state level.
blue-card question. – do you think, mr swoboda, that it is fair to eu taxpayers that israel destroys gaza every now and then? every couple of years the citizens of the european union have to build it up again. would it not be fairer to stop israel killing innocent people in gaza and stop the violence and stop the occupation as well?
the council is about to make the same mistake it made in handling the crisis. just as the crisis management has led to recession and record unemployment instead of growth and job creation, the planned large-scale reduction of the eu budget would lead to further fragmentation, widening the development gap. some policy priorities: it is important that the resources of the european social fund, its share, remain intact in order to mitigate the serious social and labour consequences of the crisis. we have to stand by our values. accordingly, we must provide resources to support the common refugee policy, the integration of migrants, fundamental rights and the fight against discrimination. the stakes are whether we choose to catch up, to represent the value of solidarity, or to break down, to tear apart. parliament cannot allow this!
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen, members of the house of representatives, the crisis of the european union is now obvious, but it is also clear that cohesion policy is one of the eu's success stories. let's not try to fix what's not broken. cutting cohesion funding would only add to the problem. it would cause further severe economic and social tensions, further undermine political stability and further reduce trust in the european institutions. mr president, allow me to give you the example of hungary. over the past two and a half years, the hungarian government and the hungarian people have made extraordinary efforts to ensure that the country finally escapes the excessive deficit procedure and avoids the risk of suspension of cohesion funds. as the european commission's recent country report shows, the hungarian government's successful crisis management programme has kept the government deficit below 3% for three consecutive years for the first time since hungary's accession to the eu. this result is both a success for the hungarian people and for europe. hungary can finally break out of the dead end into which the previous governments led it. however, this also requires cohesion resources. my fellow members of the house of representatives! solidarity is the key to europe's success. and cohesion policy has clearly proved to be the most successful instrument of solidarity so far. let's keep her safe.
in writing. today we are ostensibly discussing the next eu budgetary framework, but in reality it is about much more than that: it is about the future of europe. while we talk about a europe that wants to emerge from the crisis and develop, and even demand sustainable structural reforms from the member states, we fail to recognise that neither new ones can be built nor can we catch up with abstract resources. the fact that the solidarity pencil of the budget contributors is worn out is understandable from a certain point of view, but it is not acceptable. although cohesion policy has been a real success story in the history of the union, you are taking steps to destroy it too. but i agree with mr farage that it is unacceptable that the previous hungarian socialist government used public funds to support dog-training. as president barroso said, there is no magic wand here. as an economist, i say that this is not the time to look at the budget in detail, but to make a clear political decision now.
in writing. – i cannot agree with those who based on short-sided and economically incoherent views, are urging the european union to do more with less money when we need so desperately to boost growth and employment. how can we explain to the increasing number of working poor in the eu that the funding aimed at combating social exclusion and poverty will be significantly reduced? how can we explain to our citizens that the gap between new and old member states will be widened due to the reduction of cohesion funds? to women victims of violence that successful european programmes such as daphne are likely to be significantly curtailed? how do we expect the eu to keep its commitments to development and the promotion of human rights with such large cuts in the european development fund? we are at a turning point of the european construction. we are faced with two choices. we can either continue to pave the way for austerity and national withdrawal hoping for the better without any concrete vision to exit the crisis. or we can make the choice of an ambitious european union willing to invest for the future of its citizens.
in writing. the european union is one of the largest economic players in the world. to further increase its economic and political power, it is essential to support a budget that fosters solidarity between different member states and generations, creates synergies between different policies, while further enhancing competitiveness. i therefore reject the political aspirations which seek to weaken or destroy the european union through the budget. in order to preserve a united europe, the next seven-year budget must therefore contain the following: each budget chapter should ensure that the objectives and commitments set out therein are achieved and should not be based on the detriment of individual chapters. each restriction shall be distributed proportionally between the chapters. it seeks a level of cohesion where free movement is not a compulsion caused by lack of prospects, but a free choice. it ensures effective catch-up of cohesion countries, which is not attempted by circumventing these countries. it does not question the european social model. the budget should clearly emphasise priorities. the socialist group believes that the topics of priority are budgetary unity and full flexibility, the european stability mechanism and political consensus on own resources.
in writing. − the legality of immediate financial support from the solidarity fund should not be called into question by the european union in view of the magnitude of the earthquakes in italy in may 2012. the natural disaster caused heavy human losses and enormous material damage. in view of all this, i voted in favour of the report. however, i consider it important to emphasise that within the union, not only natural disasters can have serious consequences, but also industrial disasters caused by human error or natural disasters, for which the solidarity fund cannot provide support to a country in difficulty. i believe that this situation should be changed and that a union instrument should be established to provide immediate and effective financial assistance to member states in the event of industrial disasters.
