in writing. − i voted in full and strong support for the report on the activities of the committee on petitions in 2011, as it became clear that there is a growing need for independent tasks and powers. the report leaves no doubt that this is the forum most used by european citizens to report european infringements, thus placing an increasingly important and responsible role on the commission. in summary: the report is strongly supported by the demand that the role of the committee on petitions be reassessed in terms of its own competence and procedural role.
in writing. − the committee on petitions has an important role in eu practice in offering public assistance to eu citizens who feel they have been the victim of a miscarriage of justice or a deprivation of rights in their own country in relation to something that is the subject of common eu standards. at the same time, the lack of decision-making power of the committee is a constant source of frustration for petitioners and, in many cases, for committee members. it may, at the very most, decide that the european commission, which has the power to take a decision, may not close the case too early. the committee on petitions played a very positive role, for example, when the representative of the european commission, in a way that is incomprehensible to many of us, refused to acknowledge, in the context of the venice decrees, that the continued application of the venice decrees today does not mean the healing of wounds from a closed historical past, but rather a serious violation of fundamental rights applicable to the whole of the union. the overwhelming majority of the committee on petitions, regardless of party affiliation, rejected the ombudsman's position.

my dear colleagues, the doha climate summit, which begins next week, is our last chance to get answers to one of humanity's greatest global challenges. i fully agree with the commissioner's position, but it would be good if we could find our partners, in particular the usa, china and russia and other emerging countries, to support this. it is particularly important for the central european member states, as my colleague mr banki has said, that the kyoto emissions system should continue to function, because we, the new member states, would lose very substantial sums, including our own country. in our country, we would really need this money for renewable energy, energy efficiency, the panel housing renovation program, so we would be very interested in this system continuing and getting access to our legitimate money.
in writing. international climate negotiations have been widely regarded as a failure, with no binding agreement being reached. from the negotiations so far, it seems that we have to accept the idea that instead of big clashes, small steps can be taken to show success. in the commitment period 2013-2020, such a step forward could be the establishment of an international financing structure that provides an adequate basis for joint financing of the fight against climate change. the establishment of the climate change fund seems a promising initiative, but it is necessary to ensure that the large emitters mobilise their support resources despite the economic crisis. in addition to international efforts, it is important that the union's climate policy does not place member states that have achieved emissions reductions in excess of those set out in the international agreement at a disadvantage. the central and eastern european member states have recently exceeded their kyoto commitments and can carry over the surplus to the post-2012 commitment period under the international treaty. in the current difficult economic situation, a union proposal which would prevent surplus countries from having the right to transfer quotas is unacceptable. it is essential for european economic growth to be launched by enabling surplus countries to sell their allowances, which are important national assets, beyond 2012, so that the proceeds can be used to invest in renewables and energy efficiency.
my dear officer füle! this is an excellent report and it would be good to point out to the candidate countries that the copenhagen criteria (the rule of law, human rights, regionalisation, minority rights) and reforms should not be done because brussels expects them to be done, but because it is in the interest of these peoples. so it would be very important to reverse this, and it would be very important to warn these member states not to follow the wild capitalist, neoliberal model in one area of economic transformation, which unfortunately has been implemented in the new member states that have already joined. and one more thing. it has the credit of the european commission and the european union to meet the copenhagen criteria if the member states that already exist meet the copenhagen criteria, even if they are a minority... in writing. i believe that the enlargement process is not just about the countries wishing to join. the enlargement will also provide lessons for the member states. for example, in order to improve the credibility and effectiveness of the enlargement strategy, member states should also fully respect the copenhagen criteria, thus avoiding imposing stricter expectations on applicant countries than on individual member states, for example, in terms of the protection of national minorities. it is important that the union takes account of the commitments not only during the accession negotiations but also after accession to monitor their fulfilment. the current enlargement package gives a strong role to traditional national minorities, as these communities are present in significant numbers in the candidate countries. mutual understanding and historic reconciliation are key to enabling these states to embark on a genuine european path. therefore, there is a need to educate and learn about each other's history, language and cultural heritage during and after the accession process. finally, i would like to emphasise that civil society, in addition to the public authorities, has a major role to play in the enlargement process. it is therefore important to encourage greater involvement of non-state actors, in particular the social partners, and the development of close partnerships and cooperation during the enlargement process, both from the candidate countries and the member states. no one can solve problems on their own.
in writing. throughout its history, the european union has succeeded in moving many countries towards democratic consolidation and the development of market economies. the western balkan countries, which have been at war with each other for a decade and a half, have also taken this path. croatia will be welcomed as the 28th member of the union next year, with the other former yugoslav successor states and albania, albeit at different speeds, moving closer and closer to joining. membership requires serious effort on the part of those who wish to join. it is right that the conditions have been tightened and that the situation of the judiciary and fundamental rights is given priority in the process. it is important that civil society and non-state actors are involved in the ongoing dialogue between brussels and the capitals. if we want the european union to remain an attractive community, we cannot afford to be complacent about our achievements. i support the wording of the report which calls on the european commission to develop a detailed proposal for a monitoring mechanism to assess whether eu member states continue to comply with the eu's fundamental values. this is the only way to ensure that the union's greatest success to date - enlargement - remains authentic.

the following joint debate item is prepared by: report by arnaud danjean, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, on the implementation of the common security and defence policy (based on the annual report from the council to the european parliament on the common foreign and security policy) (2012/2138 ((ini)) (a7-0357/2012); report by ioan mircea paşcu, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, on the eu's mutual defence and solidarity guarantees: policy and operational dimensions (2012/2223 ((ini)) (a7-0356/2012); report by tunne kelam, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, on cybersecurity and defence (2012/2096 (ini)) (a7-0353/2012); report by tarek tarand, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, on the role of the common security and defence policy in the event of natural disasters and climate change (2012/2095 (ini)) (a7-0349/2012);
in writing. i congratulate mr kelam on his report, which addresses one of the most important phenomena in european security, the issue of cybersecurity. the concept of cybersecurity is an inseparable link between the internal and external dimensions of security, whether we're talking about cyber espionage, cyber terrorism, or cyber crime. cyberattacks are global in nature, so effective defence must be global as well. the need for continuous and close cooperation and the avoidance of duplication between the eu institutions, the european union, nato and the main international actors, member states, civil and military defence forces, and the public and private sectors should be emphasised. the knowledge and capabilities that are built up in the private sector are needed to ensure effective protection, whether it's from cybersecurity companies, software developers, hardware manufacturers, online system operators. critical it infrastructures may become the main targets of cyberterrorism in the future. in order to protect them, there can be no further delay in developing a common european threat assessment system and in expanding common european practices in both preparedness, prevention, detection and response. international organised crime is moving with a furious vengeance into cyberspace, often using specialists trained in military or intelligence organisations. for example, a notable feature of russian and russian-style cybercrime is that the network is often run by former kgb officers and is fed with information that can be effectively used for blackmail or coercion.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen of the house, first of all, i would like to thank mr celdeira and the staff of the court for the annual report. this is a great starting point for parliament to conduct discharge procedures in the coming months. secondly, let me say that according to a well-known hungarian proverb, the person who says that a glass is half full may be talking about the same thing as the person who says that a glass is half empty. i myself am one of those who think that this glass of the disciplined implementation of the union budget is half empty. it is not acceptable that the downward trend in the error rate has not simply stopped but that the overall error rate has increased for the second year running. my constituents would not be able to say yes to the fact that the proportion of eu funds being used regularly has deteriorated in recent years. they couldn't support this report. thirdly, let me say that it is unacceptable that the council representatives are not here in parliament when we are discussing this issue. for the past three years, the european council has had a permanent president. both the permanent president and his staff are constantly missing. the european council always has a rotating presidency, and the staff of the rotating presidency is often missing. if 62% of the errors detected in the area of cohesion policy could have been prevented by governments, then the council, representing governments, should be here in parliament. last but not least, i agree with those who say that the trend is more favourable in the use of cohesion resources. the under-utilisation rate of resources decreased from 7,7% in 2010 to 5,1%. i think this is a good message for the european council meeting that is just starting today, where the main question is whether one of the union's fundamental principles, the principle of solidarity, can be maintained in the seven-year budgetary period with regard to the preservation of cohesion funds.
overall, the union managed its resources better than in the previous year, with eur 129 billion. although the annual budget is decided jointly by the council and the parliament, the commission has a crucial role to play in ensuring that the money is used correctly, and there is still room for improvement in this area. importantly, what the 2011 report also showed is that while the direction is good, the error rate we're talking about is high. in fact, for r & d expenditure this is increasing but very significant, for agricultural and cohesion items, which account for 80% of expenditure, there is increasing inaccuracy in other areas and there are ineligible payments. the commission and the member states share responsibility for this, of course, but it points out that the problem is systemic. the commission's mechanisms are only partially effective and, unfortunately, these resources have increasingly failed to achieve their objectives or have not been used effectively. i agree with the commissioner, added value and simplicity. the challenge is to transform our system in the face of growing deficits and dwindling resources, and to make controls much more effective.
in writing. − the common commercial policy is one of the pillars of the european union's external relations. the eu's role in the context of globalisation has evolved considerably through its efforts to promote the harmonious development of world trade and to encourage fair and sustainable behaviour. the aim of the leichtfried report is to enable the european parliament, as the directly elected representative of the citizens of the union, to exercise its newly acquired powers with full effect and to exercise appropriate parliamentary scrutiny of the implementing authority, while allowing for the flexible, timely and predictable implementation of the common commercial policy. i voted in favour of this report because i believe that the recommendations and conclusions contained in it are sound and that the rapporteur has done a great job.
in writing. − i did not support the parliament's decision on the doha climate change conference because it does not take into account the interests of hungary and the other east-central european member states. i find it unacceptable that while the eu15 could not have met their kyoto commitments without the large-scale emission reductions of the new member states joining, they are now ignoring the legitimate demands of these countries to pass on their excess kyoto quota. without a compromise that is acceptable to all, there will be no common position, and this jeopardises the effective pursuit of the eu's interests in international climate negotiations.
in writing. − in today's vote, i did not support the parliament's resolution on the doha climate change conference, because it does not take into account the interests of hungary and the other east-central european member states. the eu's position on climate change is that it is not possible to achieve a common position on climate change without a common approach to the issue. i would like to point out that, at the initiative of my colleague mr erik bánki, five central and eastern european member states have issued a joint press statement in which they stress their commitment to the position of their countries at the doha climate negotiations. the communication, signed by representatives of the hungarian, bulgarian, slovak, czech and latvian people's parties, stresses that the cee member states should insist on being able to use the remaining surplus of quotas after 2012 due to their significant over-compliance with their emission reduction commitments under the kyoto protocol. this is particularly important because the revenue generated can be used to develop new, green and energy-efficient industries.
in writing. − the european parliament voted today on the report entitled 'enlargement: policies, criteria and the eu's strategic interests', which continues to highlight the importance of the copenhagen criteria. thanks to the amendments proposed by my colleague mr gálinga, the protection of national minorities as one of the copenhagen criteria continues to be given equal weight in the report with the other criteria. the current western balkans enlargement package gives a strong role to traditional national minorities, as these communities are present in significant numbers in the candidate countries. the representatives' motions can help the hungarians in vojvodina in connection with the accession of serbia, and the hungarians in the upper and transnistrian regions in the event that the commission asks for stronger enforcement of the law in this area from the member states. thanks to further amendments proposed by my colleague gál kinga, the report also incorporates the concept put forward during the hungarian presidency that mutual understanding and historic reconciliation are key to enabling the candidate countries to really embark on the european path. it is therefore important to educate and learn about each other's history, language and cultural heritage during and after the accession process. i voted in favour of the report as above.
in writing. − in his comprehensive report, arnaud danjean makes a convincing case for the union to become an independent political actor on the international stage. europe should have an autonomous defence strategy and an effective security and defence policy which allows for independent action to resolve conflicts, where necessary. but this requires real civilian and military capabilities. while the union considers the alliance with nato to be a priority and an indestructible one, it must also clearly define its strategic interests in line with changing threats and the military capabilities of its member states. there is a need to assess the military strengths and weaknesses of each member state. i would like to stress that it is important that the psc (permanent structured cooperation) does not become an 'elite club' of large and strong countries, excluding weaker countries and thus distorting cooperation towards an undesirable two-speed model. the solution, as i have already stressed in previous exchanges of views, is to provide the member states with weaker military capabilities with a role and a role that will integrate them into the overall common security and defence policy, based on their specific capabilities and skills. we must agree with the rapporteur that the revival of the csdp at institutional, operational and capability level is an unavoidable task for the european union.
in writing. − today, cybercrime is unprecedentedly global, and the fight against it requires a change of approach, a global approach. defence against cyberattacks can only be effective and efficient if it is based on a unified approach, close cooperation between the eu institutions, member states, nato, international actors, civil and military defence forces, and appropriate use of the specialised expertise accumulated in the private sector. i voted in favour of the report accordingly.
in writing. − the changes in the procedure for the election of the european parliament will have important consequences. moving the election date to may will give more time for a new parliament to convene well before the summer break, making its business less hurried. more significant is the idea that the candidates for the next president of the commission be named by each political family and, by that process, be elected by the citizens of europe. most striking is that as many members of the commission as possible be drawn from elected members of the european parliament. these changes will basically serve as an answer to the accusation that the commission is made up of unelected bureaucrats, who – on this argument – should not be exercising as much power as they do. in sum, what the changes should effect is to invest those commissioners who are elected as meps with a degree of accountability towards the voters. ideally, even though they will give up their mandates, these elected commissioners will maintain some contact with their electorates. this process will strengthen the legitimacy of the commission and should give it a greater element of autonomy with respect to council than it currently has.
mr president, since the 2009 protests which followed the last iranian presidential elections, the human rights situation in iran has steadily deteriorated. the growing numbers of political prisoners, the rising number of executions, unfair trials, the use of torture, and restrictions of fundamental liberties are just some examples of the increase in human rights violations. the recent death of sattar beheshti is a tragic reminder of the situation. this issue is sometimes obscured by tensions over the iranian nuclear power programme, which also needs to be firmly addressed. it is high time to reflect on the most effective ways to engage in a productive dialogue with the iranian authorities on the basis of the eu dual-track approach. we should keep in mind that the effect of wide-reaching sanctions can be counterproductive, as they result in increasing difficulties for ordinary citizens. therefore, we should make full use of new tools, such as the instrument for democracy and human rights, and swiftly assess its efficiency. finding an effective balance between dialogue and sanctions is crucial if we want to achieve tangible results.
mr president, while the first democratically elected libyan government has just been formed, we should not forget the fragility of the local situation and the many challenges that remain to be overcome. the terrible conditions and severe human rights abuses faced by migrants should be a priority among these. i am deeply preoccupied by the conditions faced by women and children, especially those who have arbitrarily been put into detention. we have received several reports of sexual and gender-based violence in those detention centres, where pregnant women, women with young children and unaccompanied children are being held alongside adults. their lack of recourse to adequate protection makes them vulnerable to all kinds of exploitation and violence. the situation in libya was widely discussed in the framework of the last acp–eu joint parliamentary assembly in may 2012, and i underlined this tragic situation at that time. i welcome the implementation of migration programmes by the commission and eeas and i urge you to do your utmost to put an end to this situation.

in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the creation of a digital single market is beneficial for both consumers and businesses. consumers would be able to choose from a wider range of products and thus benefit from lower prices as a result of increased competition. for companies, the same rules and conditions make it easier to operate on the markets of the member states. the digital single market report takes into account the tasks needed to complete the single market by 2015. the union digital market remains fragmented due to the lack of common standards and e-signature and e-invoicing are not yet widespread across europe. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the new rules and to monitor the implementation of the new rules. the development of a pan-european business model in the areas of vat, postal services and intellectual property rights imposes particularly high costs on these undertakings. hungary is lagging behind in this respect, both in terms of innovation capacity of smes and in terms of training. the right innovation support, such as the activities of the innovation centre in central hungary, and the right preparation of start-up entrepreneurs can help in this regard. the european connectivity facility can accelerate the creation of the digital single market by developing infrastructure and services based on it and will also provide efficient access to broadband internet to europe's most backward regions.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen of the house. it is a very important problem indeed, and i think we should proceed with due care. the prudent use of antibiotics is of vital importance not only in veterinary medicine but also in human medicine. i am a member of the board of the hungarian branch of the international child rescue service, and i see the studies that our medical team is conducting in transylvania, in the territory of romania, where these drugs are practically applied to the neck and neck of abandoned small farms, of families living in extreme poverty, with side effects that are almost impossible to eliminate in these social groups. so it would be very important, not only to get older and with the right research to be able to extract the right active ingredients, but to prescribe it for only those diseases that it actually does have a beneficial effect on, and to account for these serious side effects, because we can do a lot of harm to our fellow human beings.
in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague rapkay on his report, which i think has done an excellent job. it was a very difficult task, as the european union has been trying unsuccessfully since 2000 to adopt a unified european patent system that operates in parallel with national patents granted by the national patent offices of the member states and european patents granted by the european patent office under the european patent convention. i believe that the present proposal is the most significant and concrete step towards the implementation of this long overdue measure, as it provides a balanced and feasible solution. i am proud to say that my country, hungary, has not only made a significant contribution to the successful conclusion of the decades-long european patent dispute during its eu presidency in the first half of 2011, but will also play an important role in the functioning of the european patent system, as the centre for the training of patent judges will be established in budapest. the single european patent system reduces to a fraction of what it was before the costs necessary to create european protection for a patent. i am also confident that the creation of a truly unitary patent protection will result in a much-needed simplification of the current system. this will enable users in europe to reduce their administrative burden and patent costs by up to 80%, improving the functioning and protection of the free movement of goods in the internal market and stimulating innovation and entrepreneurship in europe.
in writing. the need for the creation of an eu patent is hardly in dispute, as the substantial simplification and lowering of the cost of the patent procedure will help to promote creative but capital-poor inventors, thus bringing to light otherwise dormant intellectual property. new eu patents can improve europe's competitiveness, unless they are also given away free to competing far eastern industries. it is important that the revenues from patent fees should not be used for all-consuming bureaucracy but should provide a credit line of at least 80% of the revenues to be used interest-free for the inventors and micro, small and medium-sized enterprises exploiting the inventions. the distribution of fee revenue should not be based on the size of the market, but on the level of creativity, i.e. the annual average of the proportion of patents that are viable in a given country.
in writing. after 40 years of negotiations, a final agreement on the european patent may finally be reached today in parliament. the overwhelming majority of the reforms to the unitary patent system, developed under the hungarian presidency of the eu, include the creation of unitary patent protection, the associated language translation regime and an international agreement to standardise patent litigation. as part of the new patent institution system, a centre for the training of patent judges will be established in budapest. under the current system, patents must be registered in each member state through the european patent office in order to obtain eu patent protection. the vast cost difference is largely due to translation costs. under the rules in force, applicants for patent protection must have their patent translated into the languages of all the countries where they wish to obtain protection. because of translation and other costs, it can cost up to ten times as much as in the united states. this system will be replaced by the new system under which the new unitary patent will be valid in all participating member states and can be obtained by a single application. this will make the current fragmented patent system simpler and cheaper, and will greatly contribute to the european union's global competitiveness.
in writing. − in times of economic crisis, it is important to expect that eu regulation imposes as little unnecessary burden as possible on indebted municipalities. i therefore vote in favour of the eu labelling provisions for office equipment not being binding on local authorities, but extending the scope of the programme to central government bodies in line with the recently adopted energy efficiency directive.
in writing. − the digital revolution of recent years has transformed the way businesses operate. some studies have shown that the internet accounts for 20 percent and 25 percent of economic growth and job creation in some g8 countries. for every job lost offline in the world, there are 2.6 new jobs in the online sector. companies that take full advantage of the opportunities offered by the internet create more than twice as many jobs as those that do not. however, in the european union, this culture has not developed as much, due in particular to the lack of trust in the digital single market, and the fact that across europe, businesses with cross-border activities face different regulatory regimes (tariffs, postal services, intellectual property rights, etc.) which make it very costly to conduct pan-european transactions. for these reasons, it is understandable why some webshops only take delivery to certain countries, which account for 61% of all online transaction failures. online shoppers can choose from twice as many products as they do in the traditional shopping environment, and up to 16 times more products in the eu. 99% of european businesses, and therefore 85% of jobs, are smes. smes that are technology-intensive are twice as likely to achieve growth, job creation and exports as their peers. there is a need for greater use of technology in all sectors, in all companies. this year, as we celebrate the 20th anniversary of the single market, we need to give our economy the momentum needed to create a digital single market. the single market would be a huge opportunity for entrepreneurs if it were to be extended by a digital single market.
in writing. − europe is moving rapidly from the industrial age to a global, networked, knowledge and services-based economy. this will require inevitable changes not only in the economy but also in institutions and systems designed for other eras. online commerce is growing fast, but there are other opportunities here. for example, new innovative services can reduce it costs for businesses by 10-20%. this is just one example of the benefits that can be gained from the creation of a single market in the digital economy and the removal of barriers to cross-border e-commerce. so i think there's a lot of work to be done in this area. in his report, our spanish colleague responded very well to the problems of the functioning of the digital single market, pointing out the difficulties that european citizens and businesses considered most pressing, and i therefore voted in favour of the report.
in writing. - 99% of european enterprises, i.e. 85% of jobs (two thirds of private sector jobs) are provided by smes. the commission estimates that this amounts to 23 million smes, thus constituting the backbone of the union economy. however, unfortunately, despite the fact that the eu remains a key destination for foreign direct investment, eurostat data show that the total eu27 fdi outflows decreased by 30% in 2008, by a further 28% in 2009 and by a further 62% in 2010. furthermore, 25% of smes operate beyond the borders of their home member state and 13% are present on markets outside the eu. smes face a wide range of challenges when expanding into markets outside their own country, including a weaker entrepreneurial spirit, lack of funding and information, a wide range of bureaucracies, strict rules and compliance with the energy and resource efficient economy, etc. it is well known that smes have been hit hardest by the financial and economic crisis, with almost a third of smes applying for bank loans being consistently rejected or receiving loans well below the amount requested over the past two years. it is for these reasons that the new programme for the competitiveness of enterprises and smes (cosme) proposed by the commission is welcome, providing enterprises and smes with a loan guarantee facility offering guarantees for credit financing through loans, subordinated and dividend-bearing loans or leases to mitigate the particular difficulties faced by smes in accessing financing for growth, allowing the securitisation of their debt financing portfolios to mobilise additional resources to finance sme debt, and will operate with a budget of eur 2,5 billion for the period 2014-2020.
in writing. − small and medium-sized enterprises form the backbone of economies all over the world. they are particularly important in terms of employment, technological innovation and gross value added, and their share is higher than that of the large companies in the spotlight. for this reason, financing smes is a very topical, exciting and wide-ranging issue. in my view, hungary can remain on the path of sustainable economic growth if it relies fundamentally on exports. economic policies driven by domestic demand are accompanied by only a temporary recovery, causing serious imbalances. the main beneficiaries of export growth are large companies. smes can only benefit from the growth of exports if they are better integrated into the supply chain. i think there is still a lot to be done in this area. in his report, mr zahradil responds very well to the problems of sme financing, highlighting the difficulties that european businesses find most challenging. i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because i too believe that it is very important to finally get out of the 'dead-end' of technical standards and thus enjoy the benefits of interoperability, data portability and reversibility. the eu intends to support eu-wide certification schemes for the identification of trusted cloud service providers. the commission will also consider developing secure and fair service terms for cloud computing contracts. the commission wants to set up a european cloud partnership with member states and industry to tap into the purchasing power of the public sector. this sector accounts for 20% of total it spending and is already able to influence the european cloud market to increase the competitiveness of european cloud providers while opening up the way to cheaper and better quality egovernment services.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the union granting macro-financial assistance to georgia of up to eur 46 million. the international community, including the european union, has repeatedly provided and planned financial assistance to the country. in the current economic circumstances, georgia, which is seeking a way out of the crisis, needs the support of the european union. this assistance is specifically granted by the union at the request of georgia, given the difficult economic situation of the country. the eu's policy towards georgia must maintain momentum within the european neighbourhood policy, as well as georgia's commitment to the euro-atlantic partnership. it is in the union's essential interest to promote public confidence in the west and to act proactively in the south caucasus region in strengthening democracy. in the case of macro-financial assistance, the commission must monitor whether the expenditure is in line with the objectives pursued.
in writing. − i support the single european patent because it helps smes in particular to realise and exploit their inventions. the new rules will also enable these companies to become more competitive at international level. unitary patent protection may be granted for all or part of the territory of the participating member states. the report emphasises that this reduces costs and takes into account the special situation of the companies. in addition, the fees will reflect the size of the market and will be similar to the average national renewal fee for european patents that come into force when they are first granted. each participating member state shall cover a certain proportion of the reduced costs incurred in the course of the patent protection tasks. overall, they will reduce administrative and translation burdens, patent costs and improve the free movement and protection of goods in the internal market, thereby also supporting european innovation and entrepreneurship.
in writing. − after 40 years of negotiations, the european parliament today approved the european patent agreement. the decision will make the currently fragmented european patent system cheaper and simpler and will significantly reduce the costs of patent litigation for businesses. this is a great help for smes, as it enables them to become more competitive internationally. for these reasons, i also voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − the european patent agreement, which had been awaited for decades, was concluded, the decisive part of which was carried out by the hungarian presidency, putting an end to the disputes over the language and patent adjudication system. the new package of legislation is aimed at abolishing the current fragmented patent system. the single system will also significantly reduce the costs of patent litigation for businesses.
in writing. − i support the amendments concerning the applicable translation rules, because i believe they make the original text clearer. i am in favour of the introduction of a unitary patent protection in europe, because it will greatly help small and medium-sized enterprises to protect their inventions. clarifying the compensation for translation costs and setting a ceiling also helps to prevent abuses that may be several times the market price. the costs of translating the patent have been borne by the applicant. the amendments clearly highlighted the importance of publishing a supplementary english translation voluntarily provided by the notifier after the end of the transition period. high-quality machine translations of european patent applications and european patent descriptions into all the official languages of the union should be made available free of charge on the internet, in order to ensure that they are accessible in all the official languages of the union and thus avoid patent infringement. by emphasizing this, it would limit the intruder's ability to argue that he acted in good faith. machine translations would also allow the english language to advance in the patent field in international trade relations. however, it also points out that in certain situations machine translation is not sufficient and that it is necessary to involve representatives of the national patent offices.
in writing. - i voted in favour of mr baldassare's report, because i fully agree with the proposals outlined and with the need for specific measures to facilitate smes' access to the european patent market and the innovation area. in this respect, i am convinced that the legal measures on unitary patent protection provide an excellent opportunity to respond to the requests made by smes, in particular with regard to costs and legal assistance.
in writing. − i am voting in favour of the creation of a unitary patent system, because under the new system it will be cheaper and easier to obtain an eu patent, which is very much in the interest of small and medium-sized enterprises. the future unitary patent will be valid in all participating member states and will be available on the basis of a single application. the current system for language use in the european patent office is modelled on the one in which english, french and german are the working languages, thus significantly reducing the cost of obtaining patent protection.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report on the patent litigation justice system, as it will significantly reduce the costs of patent litigation for businesses through a unified patent litigation system. i support the creation of a unified patent court to hear cases relating to european patents with unitary legal effect. i consider it essential that national constitutional and parliamentary procedures be consistent. the new system would balance the progress and development of the internal market, reduce costs and simplify the system. to operate more efficiently, it is important that a unified judiciary operates within the european union. the court of justice, as guardian of the law, would ensure the primacy of eu law and the unity of the legal order. i agree that this should be done in a decentralised system, with local units but with the same procedural rules at all levels. i consider it important that the language of procedure in regional offices should be the official language of the contracting member state, thereby also facilitating communication. the qualifications of patent judges should ensure that they have appropriate expertise and specialised knowledge. it should be noted in particular that as part of the new patent institutional system, a centre for the training of patent judges will be established in budapest.
in writing. - 99% of european businesses, i.e. 85% of jobs, are smes. the commission estimates that this amounts to 23 million smes, thus constituting the backbone of the union economy. however, smes face a number of problems which, in addition to a complex system of bureaucracy, also constitute a barrier to the patent system, entailing significant costs. an efficient european patent system could make a major contribution to growth through innovation. the current european patent system consists of national patents granted by the industrial property offices of the member states or european patents granted by the european patent office (epo). when a patent is granted, the european patent shall divide it into a number of national patents subject to national legislation. this costly system involves extensive and risky multi-jurisdictional litigation, often resulting in conflicting judgments. the proposals for unitary patent protection and the language regime, currently before the parliament and the council in the legislative process, aim to end the current fragmented patent system. the creation of a unified patent court and a unified patent court would ensure that contracting member states are liable for damages resulting from a breach of union law by a court and would be in line with the judicial system of the union, since contracting member states can only be member states of the union, and the quality and efficiency of proceedings would be maximised and the costs of litigation would be significantly reduced. in light of this, we would also create legal certainty.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this own-initiative report because i believe that after decades of unsuccessful attempts, a genuine, single and internationally competitive patent market can finally be achieved. in my view, a unified and functioning patent court will create legal certainty through a uniform interpretation of the applicable legislation. this is particularly important for smes, as the court system to be established will be able to make more efficient but high-quality decisions through this measure. in addition, the qualification of the judges is also essential for the functioning of the patent adjudication system. in addition to patent law, judges should have a basic knowledge of antitrust law. this will help the judges to detect any attempt by the parties to abuse the patents, thereby abusing their market dominance to the detriment of their competitors.
in writing. − the unified patent court will have exclusive jurisdiction in proceedings relating to the validity or infringement of the unified european patents. this will avoid the risk of multiple litigation cases concerning the same patent in different member states or of different court decisions in the same dispute in different member states. the central body of the court of first instance of the unified patent court will have its seat in paris, with two additional specialised divisions in london and munich. the training centre for patent judges will be located in budapest, which is a significant achievement for hungary.
in writing. − population ageing is not just a european problem, it is a global one. in europe, the number of people aged 65 and over is over 87 million, representing 17.4% of the european population, which is expected to double by 2060. the definition of old age varies from country to country and from nation to nation, but what is certain is that over a certain age, they face negative stereotypes and prejudices about the elderly, which sooner or later lead to their exclusion from society. furthermore, women have a higher life expectancy at birth than men (82.4 years for women and 76.4 years for men), so that most women remain single into old age. according to eurobarometer surveys, the proportion of women who are left alone as a result of divorce, separation or widowhood is very high (75.8%) in those over 65. loneliness, segregation and the causes of lost (perhaps elevated) status, as well as hormonal changes due to menopause and other emerging and potentially permanent harmful diseases (e.g. breast cancer, cervical cancer, etc.) are causes and diseases linked to women's age that should not be ignored, and should be prevented and addressed at the highest levels of the european union institutions. this is also due to the fact that women are paid less than men during their working lives (an average wage gap of 17.5%) and that, just before or after retirement, far fewer women find a job or are able to keep their old job than men. this is almost twice as high among men as among women. the inevitable consequence of this is that women face greater financial hardship in later life. prevention and information are essential. in addition to screening programmes, easier access to health services and reducing waiting lists, it is important to consider the return of women to society or their retention after retirement, for example through voluntary work, various cultural events, etc.
in writing. − timely and adequate diagnosis, quality treatment and access to quality medical devices are essential in preventing the further health deterioration of elderly women and in many cases – such as hearing impairment, eye disorders and other functional limitations – may partially restore the healthy functioning of affected organs. special attention should be paid to the gender aspects of fighting dementia, since partly due to their higher life expectancy, there are many more women than men living with the disease. depression and anxiety, the two most common and most serious types of mental disorder also affect women to a greater extent than men and the who estimates that their incidence in europe ranges between 2% and 15% in the over-65 age group. the commission and member states should therefore fully recognise the gender dimension in health as an essential part of eu health policies and national health policies.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the own-initiative report on the microbial challenge because it highlights a very important public health problem. the irresponsible use of antibiotics has now left us virtually defenseless against a host of pathogens, as evidenced by the increasing number of deaths in europe that can be traced back to this cause. the european commission and the member states should therefore act as soon as possible to put the recently adopted action plans into practice.
for years, our parliament has been whipping the reduction of solidarity. i still agree that we must ensure the financial foundations for the union's operation on the basis of solidarity, but we cannot be blind to the financial difficulties of the member states. the european community has a tradition of being willing to compromise, of being able to reach an agreement, and of being equal to solidarity. we can't forget that either. the current fragile agreement could be jeopardised if even one budgetary amendment is adopted by the plenary tomorrow. the council has already adopted a number of amendments to the draft budget for the financial year 2013 and the commission has already adopted a number of amendments to the draft budget for 2013. the agricultural and cohesion resources of the member states and hungary in this year's and next year's budgets would also be at risk. the hungarian people's party delegation, together with the european people's party, is therefore the only rational decision. it also votes in favour of the agreement in plenary and rejects any budgetary amendments that would jeopardise it.

the rapporteur of the opinion of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection. - mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. the problem presented in my colleague zasada's report is twofold. on the one hand, the ground handling service is not sufficiently efficient and on the other hand, its quality does not keep pace with the growing demand. as the draftsman of the imco opinion, i therefore proposed the introduction of standards which could improve the quality of services and promote the more efficient operation of airports. although the final tran proposal represents a significant step forward compared to the current legislative framework, i believe that there are still substantial issues that need further discussion. in agreement with the imco committee, i believe that full market opening is not necessary at this stage, as it would only increase the pressure on ground handling companies, which is already considerable. we therefore proposed, commissioner, that for airports whose annual traffic has reached 5 million passengers or 100 000 tonnes of freight in the previous three years, the european commission should assess whether it is really justified to increase the number of groundhandling operators to three and, on that basis, make a recommendation to the national supervisory authority, which will decide as an independent regulator whether european, national, regional or other considerations make it necessary to increase the number of operators. unfortunately, this proposal was not included in this report at all, so it was not sufficiently considered. in my view, it may also lead to a fall in wage levels, a deterioration in employment security and training opportunities for workers, and an increase in part-time and temporary employment. unfortunately, i cannot support this report in its present form and will therefore be voting in favour of it on wednesday.

mr. chairman, members of the house of representatives, there were many questions before me. i would like to speak about the introduction of noise-related operating restrictions at airports in the union. i consider it extremely important that this issue should be regulated again, since the last time this regulation was adopted was in 2002. and over the past 10 years, numerous scientific sources have proven that noise pollution is a significant and growing environmental hazard today. a recent study by the joint research centre of the who and the european commission shows that traffic noise is taking 1 million healthy life years from european citizens at eu level. that's an incredibly large number. moreover, people with hearing loss are not the ones who have been affected by hearing loss through no fault of their own, since they did not lose their hearing at a rock concert as teenagers. i think that the current regulations that tighten up these provisions might be able to achieve something. the who guidelines for noise exposure to health should not be ignored, which set the noise exposure limit at 40 decibels. nevertheless, 3 million european citizens still live in the vicinity of airports - there are about 70 in europe - where they are exposed to night noise levels of more than 50 decibels. i therefore think that the review should take place and that this matter should be regulated again as soon as possible in a stricter way than it is now.

in writing. whatever the fate of the earth service report, i thank my colleagues for their support of my amendments. i was always guided by the need to place particular emphasis on the level of minimum requirements in order to further open up the market in a balanced way and to increase the efficiency of services. i would particularly stress the importance of vocational training, which should cover the improvement of ground handling and information conditions for disabled people and the special conditions for the transport of wheelchairs and other medical aids. training adapted to the needs of people with disabilities can greatly improve the quality of services provided on the ground. we must not allow the failure of ground staff to prepare for the event to result in disabled passengers being able to get off the plane within hours when they are visiting the european parliament at my invitation. the rules should therefore be consistent with the regulation of 2006 on the rights of disabled persons and persons with reduced mobility travelling by air. these objectives should not be ignored in the future.
the current debate also shows that the european union's crisis management technique is very wide-ranging. growth requires resources, but they are scarce. each member state is working on its own and in cooperation with the other member states. it's a subject full of such doubts. what is certain is that when the ftt was introduced, the proposal was clearly not developed. this is particularly true for redistribution to member states. there was more question mark than answer. it is no coincidence that only 11 member states have since decided to do so in enhanced cooperation. but other member states also feel that this social justice tax has a place in the tax system. they just chose a different approach. the current draft report, which seeks to give the ftt a green light in enhanced cooperation, is good and should be supported, but we should not forget the individual solutions of the member states either, because we can perhaps hope for greater consensus on this issue in the future.
it is regrettable that the so-called transaction tax introduced by the hungarian government this year and further tightened from 2013 is completely contrary to the spirit of the european transaction tax. the orbán government's check tax does not penalise speculative banking transactions, but primarily the movements of money by private individuals and companies. hungarian workers pay taxes because they get paid, employers pay taxes because they pay the public debt, and the population pays taxes to cover the costs of the recession. in addition to the average salary, the transaction fee costs huf 10 000 per year for all hungarian citizens. instead of the orbán government's check tax, hungary should also apply the european transaction tax, which would tax speculative banking transactions in a fair way instead of the population and medium-sized enterprises.
in general, i am not in favour of introducing new animal welfare rules because they undermine the international competitiveness of farmers, since competitors do not have to apply them and in many cases they also lead to higher co2 emissions. in general, i believe that new animal welfare rules should only be introduced on a voluntary basis. let the consumer and the farmer decide the rules by which they produce and buy. the introduction of the 8-hour shipping limit is exceptional, and i support it because it not only promotes animal welfare, but also helps the european union's economy, and because transporting processed products emits less co2 than transporting them, it is also good for the climate. the eight-hour transport restriction will prevent live animals from being transported to countries outside the eu where they are often slaughtered in appalling conditions. and then, here in the european union, these poor animals will end up in better conditions. that's why it's very important that we don't allow any loopholes in the system, that we don't allow any loopholes in the eight-hour shipping limit. the shorter transportation will inevitably lead to the creation of more local slaughterhouses and thus more jobs in europe and especially more jobs in rural areas. for these reasons, i support the introduction of an eight-hour shipping limit.

i should like to say a few words about the van rompuy report, which my colleague referred to with the vague indication before. indeed, this report takes us into an idealistic world, one that seems to be perfect. here, member states have already put their budgets in order, put their debt on a downward path, comply with the required capital requirements and even restore the wrong, harmful debt relationship between banks and individuals, and there is no further step to be taken than to establish a shock-absorbing relationship that will protect the euro area member states from a typical future economic shock. in fact, this material goes further in its vision, that in the second integrated budgetary framework of emu, in the case of countries using the single currency, in cases where structural reforms are being carried out, these states would be supported by temporary financial incentives, recognizing their sacrifices, recognizing that this transformation entails a loss of wages, living standards, popularity and even heavy expenditure, so that these obligations would later be regulated even in contracts. i also read that the instruments for competitiveness and convergence could easily be replaced by a separate budget for the euro area in the future, and then this material goes on to say that, of course, all this requires resources, new resources, a treasury. i ask you, what? i ask whether the author of this report takes these visions seriously or whether we finally think that we need to complete our previous steps and move forward together on this path instead of just ideas.
mr. vice president, colleagues, i would like to thank you for your support. a double wall is being built around the euro area, threatening the unity of the european union and the single market itself. one wall is being built by the countries that use the euro: the rules for the banking union, the single european banking supervision, the bank rescue fund are tailored to the members of the euro area. the other countries, including my own, have only one choice: to join or not. it is important, but not sufficient, that the representatives of the states which do not yet use the single currency should be represented in a meaningful way in the single banking supervision. moreover, it is essential that distressed banks in non-euro area member states can also receive capital injections if necessary, as without them they would be at a competitive disadvantage. at the same time, another wall is being built for those countries that are not yet using the euro, countries that are not doing everything in their economic policies, including hungary, to join the eurozone as soon as possible.
in writing. mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house, in hungary, the government enters into strategic cooperation agreements with groups of companies that contribute significantly to the performance of the domestic economy in order to keep them in hungary in the long term. these agreements contribute to increasing the competitiveness of hungary and the performance of the hungarian economy. mutual trust will improve the investment environment, create new jobs, expand domestic r&d activity and open up a broad supplier base for hungarian smes. the first cooperation agreement was signed by the prime minister of hungary on 20 july 2012. since then, the hungarian government has signed agreements with half a dozen other large companies, and in december it will sign similar agreements with two more multinational companies. these agreements will result in tens of billions of forints of investment in hungary in the near future and thousands of new jobs. i think this is a great example for the whole of europe of how we can keep big companies and even encourage them to invest more.
