in writing. − the ground handling industry has high hopes for the european commission's proposal, hoping that it will increase the efficiency and overall quality of services through a further balanced opening of the market. while the proposal represents a significant step forward compared to the existing legislative framework, there are still substantial issues that need further discussion. i agree with many of my colleagues and with the market players that there is no possibility of opening the market at the moment, as this would further increase the pressure on the ground handling companies. this would also lead to a decline in wage levels, worsening of job security and training opportunities for workers, and an increase in part-time and temporary employment. i cannot support the report in its current form and therefore voted to refer it back to the committee responsible.
in writing. − the proposal aims to improve the efficiency, flexibility and competitiveness of the european groundhandling market. the commission therefore concludes that the aid granted to ryanair and ams is compatible with the internal market. the proposal would provide airlines with free access to the market for self-service and for third-party services at airports with an annual passenger traffic of more than 5 million. the subject of the proposal is of great importance, as the commission estimates that the revenue generated by groundhandling is around eur 50 billion worldwide and it is estimated that it employs at least 60 000 people in europe. however, it is important to bear in mind that full market liberalisation has a number of risks and therefore requires special attention. the union market for ground handling services should be opened up in a strong and balanced manner in order to avoid that liberalisation causes instability in the system, as such a negative outcome could lead to loss of services and downsizing of airport ground handling services and, in the long term, to the creation of oligopolies among operators.
in writing. − due to the rapid growth of air traffic and capacity constraints, the priority has been re-established and the importance of improving the quality of airport services, including ground handling, has increased. efficient ground handling is of paramount importance for passengers, airports and airlines and is essential for the proper performance of the air transport system. as i indicated in the plenary debate on tuesday, i see the problem identified in the report by artur zasada, a polish mep, as twofold: on the one hand, ground services are not sufficiently efficient due to barriers to entry and expansion, and on the other hand, the quality of ground services is not keeping pace with changing needs in terms of reliability, flexibility, protection, security and environmental protection in general. although the final tran proposal represents a significant step forward compared to the current legislative framework, i believe that there are still substantial issues that need further discussion. in agreement with the market players, i believe that full market opening is not possible at present, as it would further increase the pressure on the ground handling companies. unfortunately, for this reason, i could not support the report in its present form, and i therefore voted in favour of it.
in writing. − efficient ground handling is of paramount importance to passengers, airports and airlines. the full market opening proposed in the field of groundhandling services as a result of the economic crisis would not bring any tangible benefits to consumers but would further increase the burden on the undertakings providing groundhandling services. the full opening of the market may also lead to a fall in wage levels, a deterioration in employment security and worker training opportunities, and i therefore do not support the proposal in its present form.
in writing. − the commission's impact assessment shows that the current timetable system in the eu is not favourable for airports with limited capacity. eurocontrol has pointed out that one of the main transport challenges in europe is overcrowding at airports. the commission considers that the aid granted to ryanair and ams is compatible with the internal market on the basis of article 107 (3) (c) of the treaty. the current system therefore needs to be renewed, modernised and, above all, made more efficient. that is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. − i believe that the recasting of the regulation is an important contribution to making more efficient use of scarce infrastructure capacity. in view of the expected growth of air traffic and the corresponding growth of airport infrastructure, airlines are faced with increasing constraints in the provision of the required transport services. the commission notes that the airport services agreement concluded between ryanair and ams was signed on the same day. i welcome the introduction of market economy instruments for the allocation of slots. this ensures that slots are allocated to the airlines that can best exploit them. i also welcome the possibility for airports to influence the early return of unnecessary or unused slots through pricing. i voted in favour of this report because it is the only way to ensure that slots are not blocked and that other airlines can use them.
in writing. − i consider it extremely important that the old legislation on noise protection issues at airports in the union is updated, since it was adopted in 2002 and numerous scientific sources have demonstrated over the last 10 years that noise pollution is a significant and increasing environmental problem. according to the world health organisation (who), traffic noise is taking one million years of healthy life from europeans every year. air transport currently exposes more than 3 million inhabitants across the union to night noise levels of more than 50 db, with irreversible adverse health effects. this is why we must do everything we can to protect our citizens, especially children, from the constant noise. the proposed regulation provides for this possibility, thus bringing financial, economic, health and environmental benefits.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report, as i fully agree with the european commission's approach of taking a balanced approach to the most cost-effective measure to achieve the noise reduction targets set. i believe that the measures laid down in the proposal will finally enable member states to reconcile the economic consequences of noise abatement measures with the legitimate protection needs of the population exposed to noise. i also welcome the objective of clarifying the rules and procedures for introducing noise-related restrictions and making the deliberation process more transparent. i hope that by converting the existing directive into a regulation, noise protection measures will be more effective and distortions of competition within the european union will be avoided.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report, as it is very important that in the next statistical programming period 2013-17, member states have at their disposal a much more advanced, better developed and even more evidence-based european statistical framework, capable of serving the area it targets in a high quality, complex multidimensional way. timeliness is of paramount importance for short-term statistics, but we should not forget the increasing burden on businesses and citizens in the provision of statistical data, which must be reduced in the future.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report because i believe that a financial transaction tax could be a solution to the problems of reducing the eu's deficit and revenue. the issues at issue concern rather the manner, scope and practical implementation of the imposition of this common tax, on which there are a number of unresolved points which may explain why only 11 member states are planning to introduce this tax in the context of enhanced cooperation in the first place. however, i believe that the idea that the financial sector should be better involved in the sharing of burdens at european level, in accordance with the principle of solidarity, is correct.
in writing. − the european parliament adopted its report on the transport of animals at its plenary session in strasbourg today, in which it calls on the european commission to limit the transport time of animals to slaughter. the legislation calls on the european commission to take the initiative to limit the transport time of animals to slaughter to 8 hours. hungary, due to its central location, is crossed by a number of important transport routes to western european, southern european, turkish and middle eastern markets. if transport time were limited, more animals could be processed in our country, creating new jobs and making better use of our available slaughterhouse capacity. i voted for this report, which is very beneficial for our country.
in writing. − the report calls on the european commission to take the initiative of limiting the transport time of animals to slaughter to 8 hours, thereby also encouraging the establishment of local and mobile slaughterhouses. hungary, due to its central location, is crossed by a number of important transport routes to western european, southern european, turkish and middle eastern markets. i voted in favour of the report because i believe that if we restrict the time of transport, we could process more animals in our country, which would create new jobs and make better use of our available slaughterhouse capacity.
in writing. − the closest possible slaughter of animals to the farms is a top priority for the protection of livestock farming in the eu. the importation of animals for slaughter from third countries by sea is detrimental to the prospects of farmers in the union. it is therefore important that transport of animals for slaughter by sea is not subject to more favourable rules than transport by road. in order to protect hungarian livestock, i am voting in favour of extending the eight-hour time limit to transport of animals for slaughter transported by sea in addition to road transport.
in writing. − i am pleased that the report voted by the plenary pays particular attention to the issues and problems affecting national minorities. however, i was not able to vote for the report by the slovak socialist mp monika flašíková-beňová. the text of the report sets out in many places expectations of the member states which run counter to the values espoused by the epp and concern areas which fall specifically within the competence of the member states on the basis of the principle of subsidiarity. these issues challenge the traditions, values and constitutional arrangements of each country, which should be respected as a basic human right. it should be stressed, however, that this does not mean that there are two types of fundamental human rights in the union. these are the same rights, no matter which side of the parliamentary rat we sit on. but behind these same basic rights, the people's party does not consider itself fortunate to hide an ideological struggle. since the european people's party has always stood for the protection of fundamental rights - which are also the values of the union to which it is committed - it saw no other way of expressing its commitment than to table an alternative resolution with a more balanced text.
in writing. - i am pleased that the report voted by the plenary, thanks also to the amendments tabled by the people's party, pays particular attention to the issues and problems affecting national minorities. however, i was not able to vote for the report by the slovak socialist mp, mónika flasiková-benová. the text of the report sets out in many places expectations of the member states which run counter to the values espoused by the epp and concern areas which fall specifically within the competence of the member states on the basis of the principle of subsidiarity. these issues challenge the traditions, values and constitutional arrangements of each country, whose respect should be based on mutual respect as a fundamental human right. it should be stressed, however, that this does not mean that there are two types of fundamental human rights in the union. these are the same rights, no matter which side of the parliamentary rat we sit on. but behind these same basic rights, the people's party does not consider itself fortunate to hide an ideological struggle. since the european people's party has always stood for the protection of fundamental rights - which are also the values of the union to which it is committed - it saw no other way of expressing its commitment than to table an alternative resolution with a more balanced text.
in writing. − as the shadow rapporteur of the pp, i am pleased that the report voted by the plenary, including my amendments, pays particular attention to the issues and problems affecting national minorities. however, i was not able to vote for the report by the slovak socialist mp, mónika flasiková-benová. the text of the report sets out in many places expectations of the member states which run counter to the values espoused by the epp and concern areas which fall specifically within the competence of the member states on the basis of the principle of subsidiarity. these issues challenge the traditions, values and constitutional arrangements of each country, whose respect should be based on mutual respect as a fundamental human right. it should be stressed, however, that this does not mean that there are two types of fundamental human rights in the union. these are the same rights, no matter which side of the parliamentary rat we sit on. but behind these same basic rights, the people's party does not consider itself fortunate to hide an ideological struggle. since the european people's party has always stood for the protection of fundamental rights - which are also the values of the union to which it is committed - it saw no other way of expressing its commitment than to table an alternative resolution with a more balanced text.
in writing. − i did not vote in favour of the report by monika flasiková-benová, because i believe that the document is both contrary to christian values and also concerns areas which are specifically within the competence of the member states. i do not think it is right that the european left and liberals have used the protection of these rights as a tool for their own political, ideological struggle.
in writing. − as chief whip, my duty is to ensure the voting discipline of the house of commons, which is reflected in the fact that i am the one who sets the position for the whole house of commons during the votes in plenary. the aim of the epp is to create a european unity with decisive political weight, based on common european values, traditions and culture. however, since the hungarian people's party group is actively working to promote the interests of our country, we also represent our own national interests in the european parliament, so the position of the hungarian fidesz-kdnp delegation may differ from the people's party position. in order to avoid any contradiction with my duties as vice-president, i shall not take part in the vote directly in cases where the group votes differently from the hungarian members in the roll-call votes. i do not press the ballot button, but i do raise my hand to indicate to the pp group the voting position that we had previously agreed on.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr president! the european parliament has been issuing its annual reports on the human rights situation in countries around the world for more than a decade, a good reflection of the union's commitment to universal human rights. these reports provide a unique opportunity to also monitor the deterioration of human rights around the world and to fulfil our moral obligation to put pressure on governments that violate fundamental human rights. i call on the european commission, the union's external service, to develop, in close cooperation with the european parliament and in response to their violations, a unified and effective strategy for the protection of human rights, based on solid principles. experience shows that without sufficient consistency and legitimacy, and without a common position of the eu institutions, coordinated with civil society, the so-called third countries do not take the european union seriously. in any event, the union has a duty to take stronger action than ever before in the face of the worsening human rights abuses, in particular in relation to the still existing communist dictatorships of china and north korea, and in relation to certain post-communist countries, notably russia and the post-soviet states. we must always stand up for human rights, because in the midst of the current severe global and eu crisis, we must stand up for our principles more strongly than ever.
i welcome and congratulate mr taveres on his report, which reviews the current eu human rights strategy framework with a view to increasing the union's effectiveness, efficiency and coherence in the protection of human rights. it is essential that the new eu strategic framework on human rights and democracy, adopted by the foreign affairs council on 25 june, and the related action plan, are put into practice as soon as possible and as effectively as possible. it is also essential to coordinate the actions of the various eu and other international institutions and organisations. the union must set an example in the protection of human rights worldwide. the fundamental rights, freedoms and dignity of every person must be protected both within and outside the union. the work of the new special representative for human rights and all the messages we deliver here in the european parliament will be central to this endeavour. i have one of the most important tools of this, the annual human rights report. i am pleased that this year's report has given greater emphasis to the protection of the rights of children and minorities and has mentioned the creation of a new programme of volunteering in humanitarian aid, which will finally lead to concrete action in the field of human rights protection. congratulations again to both rapporteurs.
i agree with the conclusions of the excellent report by mr swoboda. relations between the eu and russia are stagnating. on the eu side, we have not found an antidote to president putin's rejection of moderate and well-founded eu criticism, as has hungarian prime minister viktor orbán. nevertheless, the european union must continue to criticise on a principled basis the anti-democratic developments in russia, the judicial failings in the magnitsky case, and the actions of non-governmental organisations. we must take a principled stance on these, but at the same time we must support visa liberalisation, visa facilitation. this is what the people of both the union and russia want, and we must continue to strive for proper energy cooperation in the future.
i'll close this discussion.

next item statement by the vice-president of the commission / high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy on the situation in ukraine
my dear officer füle! i agree with the commission's assessment that the ukrainian parliamentary elections could hardly be described as democratic, even if they were not accompanied by large-scale fraud. as international observers have confirmed, the circumstances, the electoral conditions, the electoral system, the campaign controls, the media coverage, were not democratic. the field has dropped heavily in favor of the ruling party. i would like to draw your attention to the fact that the electoral law recently adopted by the hungarian governing majority restricts democracy even more than the ukrainian law by introducing mandatory registration, the electoral system that compensates the winner, the redistricting of electoral districts, the electoral commission filled with party delegates elected for 9 years. it would be very good if this european commission would not only take action outside the union's borders, but also within the union's borders in order to ensure that democratic and fair elections are held.
in writing. – the progresses achieved by albania this year needs to be underlined, especially after two years of political stalemate. however, a lot remains to be done as to fulfilling the membership criteria. i welcome the legislation adopted against domestic violence but i notice at the same time that several reports pointed out the very high number of cases of trafficking in human beings. this is an issue that the albanian government has to acknowledge and firmly address. the protection of minorities, especially the living situation of the roma community, also needs to be improved. when it comes to justice and the rule of law, despite some important positive signs, such as the functioning of the parliamentary institutions, the dialogue between the government and the opposition must be further encouraged in order to pursue the much needed reforms. i am referring here to the fight against corruption and organised crime, and to the reform of the judiciary. it is my understanding that the socialist opposition had asked for a national debate on the rule of law in albania, and i regret that the ruling majority did not accept this proposal.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam vice-president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to begin by congratulating my colleague, mrs ždanoka, on her report, in which she has taken her decision in the interests of both parliament and the agency, and the people's party agrees with her. more than six years ago, i was fortunate to be part of the creation of the fundamental rights agency as rapporteur. i have been following the work of the agency ever since, and it has undoubtedly become an indispensable aid to our work, both here in parliament and, as we see, in the commission. five years ago, the first framework programme of the agency was discussed in this house. in the debate at the time, we argued that all three eu institutions, including parliament, should be equally involved in the development of the agency's mandate. we regret that the european parliament has not been able to give more details of the current negotiations, but we consider it important that the agency should be able to move as quickly as possible to a post-lisbon status, three years after the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. parliament does not wish to impede the agency's normal work after 1 january and therefore gives its consent to the procedure. we hope that the council will also remove the obstacle to extending the mandate of the agency to police and judicial cooperation as soon as possible. we hope that the bridge solution proposed by the cypriot presidency will be a temporary measure and that the mandate of this agency will finally extend to police and judicial cooperation. however, we believe that this solution can contribute to ensuring the continuity of the agency's work. i ask for your support in giving this feedback to the european parliament and i ask for the support of the member states.

in writing. − the nature and extent of the work that the eu fundamental rights agency is obliged to carry out has significantly increased during the years that have passed since its establishment. one of these recent duties is the monitoring of the implementation of the eu framework for national roma inclusion strategies. the effective policy overview of such an all-european action plan needs significant human and financial resources as well as new and enhanced competences. a revision of the institutional structure and the functioning of the fra should probably therefore be considered, in order to enable the agency to successfully participate in the robust monitoring and supervision that the framework strategy calls for and, furthermore, all parallel and overlapping efforts need to be eliminated to enhance institutional and professional control over the related programmes.
in writing. − the importance of written declarations has changed with the enlargement of parliament's powers following the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon and the introduction of the european citizens' initiative. i believe that written declarations currently have only a very limited impact on the decisions taken by the eu institutions. as vice-president of the group of the european people's party responsible for legislation, i consider it particularly important that we in the european parliament increasingly deal with proposals for legislative action that provide a real opportunity for individual members to influence eu legislation. i therefore support the provision in the proposal that in future only written statements by at least ten representatives of at least three political groups may be submitted on subjects falling within the exclusive competence of the european union.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution calling on the ukrainian parliament to reject bill 8711, which was recently adopted at first reading and which would restrict freedom of expression and assembly. i would like to stress that i am concerned about the rise of nationalism in ukraine. it is therefore important that the resolution states that racist, anti-semitic and xenophobic views are contrary to the eu's fundamental values and principles. the ep calls on the parties in the ukrainian parliament committed to democracy not to support or form a coalition with the party.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report calling on the council to grant albania eu candidate status as soon as possible. the union must continue its enlargement to the western balkans, and albania is undoubtedly playing a stabilising role in this region. i agree with the report that the future of the country is linked to the european union and that the eu accession process should be the driving force behind the continuation of reforms. however, i myself stress the importance of maintaining political dialogue between the ruling majority and the opposition and the need to improve the political climate. during the election campaign next year, they must not allow albania to stray from the eu path and must ensure that parliamentary elections are free and fair. in addition, political actors must make the population understand that eu accession is in the common interest of all albanians. finally, i urge action against corruption in line with the report, which is a prerequisite for eu accession. the albanian leadership should pay close attention to this matter.
in writing. − very little progress was made in terms of the social inclusion of roma in the past years in albania, so it can be stated that the strategy for social inclusion for 2007-2013 could not fulfil the expectations. the government has approved decisions to assist in birth registration, but institutions have not properly coordinated the registration process and, although roma have benefited from the distribution of free text books and making public schooling compulsory, high school drop-out rates still remain high and enrolment rates in pre-schools are low. roma communities still live in substandard living conditions, and local government units are still insufficiently involved and aware of the problems, with critical services for roma still being largely provided by civil society organisations and financed by international donors. the programme therefore has proved to be unsustainable and in most areas achieved no or very limited progress due to financial constraints and the lack of coordination and expertise. albania must therefore draw the conclusions of its failed strategy and develop an action plan for the 2013-2020 period that is in line with he eu framework strategy and the eu must monitor and support its implementation through ipa and sap.
in writing. − steel is one of the world's most widely traded industrial products, used in large quantities in industries such as automotive, railway and construction. the eu steel industry accounts for 1.4% of gdp and more than 360 000 people are employed in more than 50 steel mills in the 23 eu member states. in the light of these data, i myself believe that it is of the utmost importance for the future of europe and its citizens that the steel industry is active and competitive, and that we should therefore encourage the european commission to propose how to restructure the european steel industry. i voted in favour of this motion because it is clear that the european steel industry is not in an easy situation and that is why we must do everything we can to maintain its competitiveness.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, the armed rebellion of the m23 movement has reopened the wounds of a country that had already suffered a bloody conflict that lasted for years. the negotiations and dialogue process started last week are a first step towards a resolution, but the situation remains very unstable. given the deeply-rooted and multiple reasons for this conflict, as well as its implications for the whole region, a comprehensive plan and strategy is urgently needed. besides the sensitive ethnic situation, the economic and social environment, the role of the neighbourhood countries, and the struggle for the drc’s natural resources are issues that must also be addressed. the illicit exploitation and trading of natural resources, including illegal mining, must be prevented before a lasting solution can be found. i would like to underline the repeated atrocities that the citizens of the democratic republic of congo – and especially its eastern part – have endured over the years. i would like to highlight, in particular, that the use of sexual violence and rapes on a massive scale, including on underage girls, cannot be tolerated and needs to be put to an end. not only must we prevent such hateful crimes, but the utmost must be done to help the victims recover both physically and psychologically. more generally, the security of internally-displaced people needs to be strengthened, and the consequences of the conflict need to be seriously dealt with. without a proper strategy for the demobilisation and reintegration of ex-combatants and the repatriation of refugees, a lasting peace is unlikely.
mr president, caste discrimination in india remains a striking reality, despite the successive legislative acts enacted by the indian government. among these victims of social exclusion, the situation of female dalits is particularly alarming. they suffer from a double form of exclusion and face multiple forms of violence and exploitation. the act on the protection of women from domestic violence has proved to be rather inefficient so far, especially as the social environment makes it very difficult for them to report abuses. moreover, the women who dare to do so face strong prejudice from those who are supposed to ensure their protection, such as police forces or legal officers. in addition, many female dalits are employed in work that can be compared to modern slavery, such as manual scavenging or in factories in which they endure terrible working conditions. the report that some of these factories produce garments for western companies is very disturbing to say the least. i would therefore ask you, commissioner, what measures can be taken against european companies who would rely on such exploitation?
